For the radical $\operatorname{rad}(n)=\prod_{p\mid n}p$ write $R(n)=\operatorname{rad}(n)/n$, and for a window of length $d$ call the permutation $\pi_0\cdots\pi_{d-1}$ of $\{0,\ldots,d-1\}$ the \emph{order pattern}---the ordinal pattern of the window---at $n$ when $R(n+\pi_0)<\cdots<R(n+\pi_{d-1})$, counting only windows with no ties.  Write $\delta_\pi$ for the density of the order pattern $\pi$ among the untied windows, the limiting ratio of the two window counts, so that $\sum_\pi\delta_\pi=1$; ties have positive density, adjacent squarefree integers already tying at $R=1$, so an unconditional normalization would be deficient.  For the abundancy index $\sigma(n)/n$ and the totient ratio $\phi(n)/n$ call an adjacent triple \emph{monotone} when it is strictly increasing or strictly decreasing, and for a stride $W$ let $\mu(W)$ be the density of $n$ for which $\sigma(n)/n,\sigma(n+W)/(n+W),\sigma(n+2W)/(n+2W)$ is monotone.  Write $\Omega(m)$ for the number of prime factors of $m$ with multiplicity, and set the binomial slice $A_{k,n}=\binom{kn}{n}$, the central multinomial $M_{k,n}=(kn)!/(n!)^k$, and the Fuss--Catalan number $F_{r,n}=\tfrac1{rn+1}\binom{(r+1)n}{n}$, with single-step jump $J_{k,n}=\Omega(A_{k,n+1})-\Omega(A_{k,n})$.  Finally, for consecutive odd primes $p<q$ set
\[
D(p,q)=\Omega(p+1)+\Omega(q-1)-\Omega(p-1)-\Omega(q+1),
\]
the inward-minus-outward prime-factor balance of the four composites bracketing the gap.

\begin{proposition}[Factorial-ratio jump identities]\label{w:jump}
For all $k\ge2$ and $n\ge1$,
\[
J_{k,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Omega(kn+j)-\Omega(n+1)-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\Omega((k-1)n+j).
\]
In particular $\Omega\binom{2n+2}{n+1}-\Omega\binom{2n}{n}=1+\Omega(2n+1)-\Omega(n+1)$, and for the Catalan numbers $C_n=\tfrac1{n+1}\binom{2n}{n}$, $\Omega(C_{n+1})-\Omega(C_n)=1+\Omega(2n+1)-\Omega(n+2)$.  Consequently the central binomial jump $J_{2,n}$, the Catalan jump $\Omega(C_{n+1})-\Omega(C_n)$, and the central multinomial jump $\Omega(M_{k,n+1})-\Omega(M_{k,n})$ for every $k\ge2$ are unbounded below, while the binomial slice $J_{k,n}$ for $k\ge3$ is settled by the forcing construction of Proposition~\ref{w:shock}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Short-window radical exchangeability]\label{w:radunif}
For every window length $d\le4$ the strict order-pattern densities of $R(n)=\operatorname{rad}(n)/n$ exist and are uniform, each equal to $1/d!$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Existence of radical-pattern densities]\label{w:raddens}
For every fixed window length $d$, each unconditional strict order-pattern count for $R(n)=\operatorname{rad}(n)/n$ has a natural density.  The untied windows have positive density.  Consequently every conditional density $\delta_\pi$ exists.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Triangular--hexagonal finite-sieve equivalence]\label{w:trihex}
For every modulus $m\ge1$ and residue $c\bmod m$,
\[
\frac{1}{2m}\#\{n\bmod 2m:P_3(n)\equiv c\pmod m\}
=\frac{1}{m}\#\{k\bmod m:P_6(k)\equiv c\pmod m\}.
\]
Consequently the triangular and hexagonal local compass frequencies are exactly equal for every finite set of sieving primes.
\end{proposition}

\section{Prime compasses at polynomial centres}

\begin{conjecture}[Local-sieve limit for polygonal compasses]\label{w1}
For every fixed $s\ge3$ the limit $\theta_s=\theta_{P_s}$ exists.  Moreover, if the nearest-prime race is replaced by the first offset on each side surviving every prime at most $y$, the resulting local compass frequency tends to $\theta_s$ as $y\to\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Pentagonal and pronic compasses]\label{w2}
The pentagonal compass satisfies $0.56<\theta_{P_5}<0.62$, while the pronic compass satisfies $0.42<\theta_Q<0.48$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Opposite polygonal orders]\label{w3}
The $60$-gonal compass satisfies $\theta_{60}>0.58$ and the $69$-gonal compass satisfies $\theta_{69}<0.49$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Triangular and hexagonal equality]\label{w4}
The triangular and hexagonal compass constants are equal, $\theta_{P_3}=\theta_{P_6}\in(0.52,0.56)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Square compass by root class]\label{w5}
For each residue $r\bmod6$ the conditional square compass $\theta_{\square,r}$ over roots $n\equiv r$ exists, with $\theta_{\square,r}<0.45$ for $r\in\{1,5\}$ and $\theta_{\square,r}>0.54$ for $r\in\{0,2,3,4\}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Two-sided order spectrum]\label{w6}
The polygonal compass spectrum straddles one half in the limit of many sides,
\[
\liminf_{s\to\infty}\theta_s<0.49<0.58<\limsup_{s\to\infty}\theta_s,
\]
so infinitely many polygonal orders are left-biased and infinitely many are right-biased.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Order patterns of the radical ratio}

\begin{conjecture}[Permanent nonuniformity from length five]\label{w7}
For every fixed window length $d\ge5$ the strict order-pattern densities of $R(n)=\operatorname{rad}(n)/n$ exist, and their distribution is not uniform.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Thirteen-fold pattern separation]\label{w8}
At window length five the strict densities satisfy $12<\delta_{30241}/\delta_{13240}<15$, with $30241$ in the dominant class and $13240$ in the suppressed class.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Order laws for multiplicative ratios}

\begin{conjecture}[Abundancy order constant]\label{w9}
The density of $n$ for which $\sigma(n)/n,\sigma(n+1)/(n+1),\sigma(n+2)/(n+2)$ is monotone exists and lies in $(0.094,0.098)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Totient order constant]\label{w10}
The density of $n$ for which $\phi(n)/n,\phi(n+1)/(n+1),\phi(n+2)/(n+2)$ is monotone exists and lies in $(0.019,0.022)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Five-term totient barrier, resolved false]\label{w11}
No five consecutive values of $\phi(n)/n$ are strictly increasing, and no five are strictly decreasing.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False, by a theorem of Martin~\cite{Martin06}: strictly monotone runs of $\phi(n)/n$ of every length occur on a set of positive lower density, though none below $4\times10^9$, whereas the abundancy analogue already fails at $n=36{,}721{,}681$; retained for the contrast it draws.

\begin{remark}[Resolution]
The conjecture is false.  By a theorem of Martin~\cite{Martin06}, for every $k$ and every prescribed strict ordering of $\phi(n+1),\ldots,\phi(n+k)$ with successive ratios exceeding any fixed $C$, the realizing $n$ have positive lower density; taking $k=5$ and ratios exceeding $2$ forces strictly monotone runs of $\phi(n)/n$ of length five in either direction, on positive lower density.  The runs are astronomically rare, none below $4\times10^9$, whereas the abundancy analogue already fails at $n=36{,}721{,}681$.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Primorial-stride order constant]\label{w12}
Along primorial strides $W_y=\prod_{p\le y}p$ the abundancy order density $\mu(W_y)$ has a limit as $y\to\infty$, and $\lim_{y\to\infty}\mu(W_y)\in(0.16,0.19)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Dyadic completion overshoot]\label{w13}
For every even squarefree $W$ divisible by six the limit $\lim_{e\to\infty}\mu(2^eW)$ exists and exceeds $1/3$, so $\mu(2^eW)>1/3$ for all large $e$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Prime-factor shocks in combinatorial sequences}

\begin{conjecture}[Binomial-slice negative shocks, resolved true]\label{w14}
For every fixed $k\ge3$ the jump $J_{k,n}=\Omega\binom{k(n+1)}{n+1}-\Omega\binom{kn}{n}$ is unbounded below.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true: Proposition~\ref{w:shock} forces arbitrarily deep negative jumps for every $k\ge3$ by a Chinese-remainder construction that loads the denominator forms $(k-1)n+j$ with many prime factors while a first-moment bound keeps the numerator forms typical.

\begin{conjecture}[Binomial-slice positive shocks, resolved true]\label{w15}
For every fixed $k\ge2$ the jump $J_{k,n}$ is unbounded above.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true: Proposition~\ref{w:shock} applies the same forcing with the two form families exchanged, loading the numerator forms $kn+j$; the second negative block that resisted the power-of-two argument is controlled on average rather than pointwise.

\begin{conjecture}[Fuss--Catalan two-sided shocks, resolved true]\label{w16}
For every fixed $r\ge1$ the jump $\Omega(F_{r,n+1})-\Omega(F_{r,n})$ is unbounded both below and above.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true in both directions for every $r\ge1$: Proposition~\ref{w:shock} applies to the Fuss--Catalan ratio's two families of forms, which are pairwise nonproportional since $(r+1)j'=rj$ has no admissible solution.

\begin{proposition}[Two-sided shock forcing]\label{w:shock}
For every fixed $k\ge2$ the jump $J_{k,n}$ is unbounded below and unbounded above, and for every fixed $r\ge1$ the Fuss--Catalan jump $\Omega(F_{r,n+1})-\Omega(F_{r,n})$ is unbounded below and unbounded above.  In particular Conjectures~\ref{w14}, \ref{w15}, and~\ref{w16} are true.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{w:jump} and $\Omega(kn+k)=\Omega(k)+\Omega(n+1)$ give $J_{k,n}=\Omega(k)+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\bigl(\Omega(kn+j)-\Omega((k-1)n+j)\bigr)$, and cancelling $rn+1$ against the denominator block and $(r+1)(n+1)$ against $n+1$ in the exact ratio $F_{r,n+1}/F_{r,n}$ gives $\Omega(F_{r,n+1})-\Omega(F_{r,n})=\Omega(r+1)+\sum_{j=1}^{r}\Omega((r+1)n+j)-\sum_{j=2}^{r+1}\Omega(rn+j)$.  Each claim is therefore an instance of the following: for affine forms $L_i(n)=a_in+b_i$, $1\le i\le s$, and $L'_j(n)=a'_jn+b'_j$, $1\le j\le s$, with positive integer coefficients and no $L_i$ proportional to any $L'_j$, the difference $\sum_i\Omega(L_i(n))-\sum_j\Omega(L'_j(n))$ is unbounded above.  Nonproportionality holds in all four applications: $kj'=(k-1)j$ forces $k\mid j$ with $1\le j\le k-1$, and $(r+1)j'=rj$ forces $r+1\mid j$ with $1\le j\le r$, both impossible.

For the claim, extract the contents $\gcd(a'_j,b'_j)$, whose contribution to $\Omega(L'_j)$ is constant, so the $L'_j$ may be taken primitive; let $R$ bound the coefficients and the cross-resultants $|a_ib'_j-a'_jb_i|$, fix $m$, and choose $sm$ distinct primes exceeding $R$, one block $Q_i$ of $m$ primes for each $L_i$.  Each congruence $L_i(n)\equiv0\pmod{Q_i}$ is a single residue class since the primes exceed $a_i$, and the classes combine by the Chinese remainder theorem into $n\equiv\rho\pmod Q$ with $Q=Q_1\cdots Q_s$, on which $\Omega(L_i(n))\ge m$ for every $i$.  No forced prime $p$ divides any $L'_j(n)$: together with $p\mid L_i(n)$ it would divide $a_iL'_j(n)-a'_jL_i(n)=a_ib'_j-a'_jb_i$, a nonzero integer of absolute value below $p$.  Now let $n$ run over $\rho+tQ$ for $1\le t\le Q^3$, so that every value $L'_j(n)$ is at most a constant times $Q^4$.  A prime power $p^v\le Q^3$ with $p\nmid Q$ divides $L'_j(n)$ along at most $Q^3/p^v+1$ values of $t$, primes dividing $a'_j$ divide no value of the primitive form, and summing $1/p^v$ over prime powers gives an average of $\log\log Q+O(1)$ per form, the $+1$ terms totalling $O(Q^3/\log Q)$.  Prime powers exceeding $Q^3$ add $O(1)$ per form on average: a prime above $Q^3$ divides a value at most once and to the first power, since $(Q^3)^2$ exceeds every value, while prime powers $p^v>Q^3$ with $v\ge2$ are squarefull, number $O(Q^2)$ below the value bound, and each catches at most two values of $t$.  By Markov some $t\le Q^3$ has $\sum_j\Omega(L'_j(n))\le2s\bigl(\log\log Q+O(1)\bigr)$, whence $\sum_i\Omega(L_i(n))-\sum_j\Omega(L'_j(n))\ge sm-2s\log\log Q-O(s)\to\infty$ as $m\to\infty$, since $\log\log Q=O(\log(sm\log m))$.  Explicit witnesses are cheap: six primes per form already force $J_{3,n}\le-20$ and $J_{4,n}\le-30$ at heights near $10^{21}$ and $10^{35}$, and $J_{3,n}\ge21$ on the positive side, beyond every scanned extreme.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Negative shock covariance]\label{w17}
In every fixed binomial-slice ($k\ge2$), central-multinomial ($k\ge2$), and Fuss--Catalan ($r\ge1$) family the lag-one covariance of successive jumps has a finite strictly negative limit.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Exact truncated covariance for the central-binomial slice]\label{w:cov53}
For a cutoff $y\ge2$ write $\Omega_y(m)=\sum_{p\le y}v_p(m)$ and $J^{(y)}_n=1+\Omega_y(2n+1)-\Omega_y(n+1)$.  The lag-one covariance of the truncated central-binomial jump over $n\le N$ converges as $N\to\infty$, to $-1$ at $y=2$ and to
\[
\lim_{N\to\infty}\operatorname{Cov}\bigl(J^{(y)}_n,J^{(y)}_{n+1}\bigr)=-\tfrac53\qquad\text{for every }y\ge3.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Existence of the shock covariance]\label{w:covexist}
In each fixed binomial-slice ($k\ge2$), central-multinomial ($k\ge2$), and Fuss--Catalan ($r\ge1$) family, the lag-one covariance of successive $\Omega$-jumps over $n\le N$ converges as $N\to\infty$ to a rational number.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Dyadic multinomial slope]\label{w18}
For every fixed $k\ge2$ the dyadic jump of the central multinomial obeys
\[
\frac{\Omega(M_{k,2^m})-\Omega(M_{k,2^m-1})}{m}\longrightarrow-(k-1).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[The dyadic multinomial slope for $k=2$]\label{w:k2slope}
The conclusion of Conjecture~\ref{w18} holds for $k=2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Eventual sign balance, resolved true]\label{w19}
For every fixed binomial-slice and Fuss--Catalan family the positive-jump and negative-jump densities each tend to $\tfrac12$, while the zero-jump density tends to $0$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true.  By the identities of Propositions~\ref{w:jump} and~\ref{w:shock}, each jump is, up to a bounded term, a signed sum of $\Omega$ over $2(k-1)$, respectively $2r$, pairwise nonproportional affine forms, whose primitive parts are pairwise coprime linear polynomials.  The multivariate Erd\H{o}s--Kac theorem of El-Baz, Loughran, and Sofos~\cite{ELS22}, whose one-variable case recovers Tanaka's theorem for pairwise coprime irreducible polynomials, makes the vector of $\omega$-values asymptotically Gaussian with identity covariance, so the signed sum, normalized by $\sqrt{2(k-1)\log\log n}$, is asymptotically standard normal; replacing $\omega$ by $\Omega$ shifts each coordinate by a quantity of bounded mean, negligible at this scale.  The diverging variance swamps the bounded centring, giving sign densities $\tfrac12$, and the zero-jump density vanishes because the limit law assigns every shrinking central window vanishing mass.

\section{The arithmetic anatomy of prime-gap boundaries}

\begin{conjecture}[Boundary Euler sum]\label{w20}
For each prime $\ell$ let $r$ be uniform on $\Z/\ell^a\Z$ conditioned on $r(r+g)\not\equiv0\pmod\ell$, and set
\[
m_{\ell,a}(g)=\mathbb E\bigl[v_\ell(r+1)+v_\ell(r+g-1)-v_\ell(r-1)-v_\ell(r+g+1)\bigr].
\]
Then for every fixed even $g$ occurring infinitely often as a consecutive-prime gap,
\[
\lim_{x\to\infty}\mathbb E\bigl[D(p,q)\mid p\le x,\ q-p=g\bigr]=\sum_\ell\lim_{a\to\infty}m_{\ell,a}(g).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\part{Topological invariants of geometric families}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 12 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The twenty conjectures below leave arithmetic for algebraic topology while keeping the same discipline.  Each takes a canonical family of spaces, computes an exact classical invariant across the whole family, and takes the resulting shape law seriously.  They form four programmes.  A Calabi--Yau programme (Conjectures~\ref{x1} to~\ref{x5}) studies the Euler indices of smooth complete intersections in projective space, ending in an exact dimension-wise gcd law.  A link programme (Conjectures~\ref{x6} to~\ref{x10}) studies the middle Betti numbers of Brieskorn--Pham links under degree caps and common scaling.  A moment-angle programme (Conjectures~\ref{x11} to~\ref{x15}) studies the Hochster strands of flag moment-angle complexes, their support, shape, and zero locations.  A monodromy programme (Conjectures~\ref{x16} to~\ref{x20}) studies torus mapping tori with signed-permutation monodromy over the signed-orbit calibration proved below.  The calibration layer is classical and exact: the Chern-class formula for complete intersections, the Milnor--Orlik formula for Brieskorn links, Hochster's formula for bigraded Betti numbers, and the Wang sequence for mapping tori.  Six adversarial controls are retained in place, each refuting a tempting strengthening.  Two of the twenty, the limit clause of Conjecture~\ref{x1} and Conjecture~\ref{x19}, are the strongest statements of the part.  Two more, Conjectures~\ref{x4} and~\ref{x8}, lie close to classical methods.  Since deposit, Conjectures~\ref{x1} and~\ref{x2} have been resolved true by Propositions~\ref{x:qladder} and~\ref{x:merge}, and Conjecture~\ref{x19} has been resolved false by the Landau floor of Proposition~\ref{x:landau}, which fixes the compression exponent at $2$.  Conjecture~\ref{x9} and the convexity clause of Conjecture~\ref{x10} have since been proved by the Ehrhart model of Proposition~\ref{x:ehrhart}.

For $n\ge2$ and a multidegree $\mathbf d=(d_1,\ldots,d_r)$ with every $d_i\ge2$ and $\sum_id_i=n+r+1$, let $X^{(n)}_{\mathbf d}\subset\mathbf{CP}^{n+r}$ be a smooth complete intersection of multidegree $\mathbf d$, a Calabi--Yau $n$-fold, with Euler characteristic computed exactly by $c(TX)=(1+H)^{n+r+1}/\prod_i(1+d_iH)$ and $\chi=\bigl(\prod_id_i\bigr)[H^n]\,c(TX)$; write $E_n(\mathbf d)=(-1)^n\chi(X^{(n)}_{\mathbf d})$, $D(\mathbf d)=\prod_id_i$, and let the \emph{merge} $M_{ij}\mathbf d$ replace $d_i,d_j$ by $d_i+d_j-1$, preserving both $n$ and the Calabi--Yau condition.  Write $Q_n=E_n(2,\ldots,2)$ for the all-quadric configuration of $n+1$ quadrics.  For $\mathbf a=(a_1,\ldots,a_N)$ with $a_i\ge2$, let $L(\mathbf a)$ be the Brieskorn--Pham link $\{z_1^{a_1}+\cdots+z_N^{a_N}=0\}\cap S^{2N-1}$, of real dimension $2N-3$, with middle Betti number $\beta(\mathbf a)=b_{N-2}(L(\mathbf a);\mathbf Q)$ given exactly by the Milnor--Orlik formula~\cite{MO70}
\[
\beta(\mathbf a)=(-1)^N+\sum_{\varnothing\ne J\subseteq[N]}(-1)^{N-|J|}\frac{\prod_{j\in J}a_j}{\operatorname{lcm}_{j\in J}a_j},
\]
the \emph{Fano} tuples being those with $\sum_i1/a_i>1$, with cap $A=\max_ia_i$ and scale sequence $\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)=\beta(ka_1,\ldots,ka_N)$.  For a finite simple graph $G$ on $m$ vertices with clique complex $K=\operatorname{Cl}(G)$, define the Hochster strands
\[
h_{r,s}(G)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq V(G)\\|I|=s}}\dim_{\mathbf F_2}\widetilde H_r(K_I;\mathbf F_2),
\qquad
H_{G,r}(z)=\sum_sh_{r,s}(G)\,z^s,
\]
the bigraded Betti sectors of the Stanley--Reisner ring and of the moment-angle complex $\mathcal Z_K$ by Hochster's formula~\cite{Hoch77,BP15}.  Finally, for a signed permutation matrix $A\in SL(d,\Z)$ let $M_A$ be the mapping torus of the induced automorphism of the torus $T^d$, set $f_k(A)=\dim_{\mathbf Q}\ker(\wedge^kA-I)$, so that the Wang sequence gives $b_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)=f_k(A)+f_{k-1}(A)$~\cite{Wang49,Mil68}, and let $\tau(A)=\sum_kd\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}H_k(M_A;\Z)\bigr)$ count minimal torsion generators.  The cap $A$ of the link programme and the matrix $A$ of the monodromy programme are unrelated symbols, each local to its own sections.

\begin{proposition}[Signed-orbit calibration]\label{x:orbit}
Let $A$ be a signed permutation matrix on $\Z^d$.  Then $\wedge^kA$ permutes the wedge basis up to sign, and every orbit of the induced permutation of $k$-subsets carries a holonomy sign, the product of the signs collected around one period.  If $p_k$ and $n_k$ are the numbers of positive and negative orbits at level $k$, then
\[
f_k(A)=p_k,\qquad
\operatorname{coker}(\wedge^kA-I)\cong\Z^{\,p_k}\oplus(\Z/2)^{\,n_k},
\]
and consequently $b_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)=p_k+p_{k-1}$, $\operatorname{Tor}H_k(M_A;\Z)\cong(\Z/2)^{\,n_k}$, and $\tau(A)=\sum_kn_k$, while $\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)=2\sum_kp_k$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Euler indices of Calabi--Yau complete intersections}

\begin{conjecture}[Quadric-ladder ratio law, resolved true]\label{x1}
For every $n\ge5$,
\[
\frac{Q_{n+1}}{Q_n}<8,
\qquad
\frac{Q_{n+2}}{Q_{n+1}}>\frac{Q_{n+1}}{Q_n},
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{Q_{n+1}}{Q_n}=8.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{x:qladder} below, which proves all three clauses with the sharpening $8-Q_{n+1}/Q_n\sim4/n$.

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of the quadric-ladder ratio law]\label{x:qladder}
Conjecture~\ref{x1} holds in full, with the sharpening $8-Q_{n+1}/Q_n\sim4/n$.  The generating function $A(z)=\sum_{n\ge0}Q_nz^n$, with $Q_0=2$ and $Q_1=0$, is algebraic,
\[
A=\frac{2(1-y)^3}{1-3y},\qquad z=\frac{y(1-2y)}{2(1-y)^2},\quad y(0)=0,
\]
and the coefficients satisfy $(n+1)(n+2)Q_{n+2}=(n+1)(7n+9)Q_{n+1}+4(n+2)(2n+3)Q_n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Chern-class formula gives $Q_n=[t^n]\varphi(t)^{n+1}$ for $\varphi(t)=2(1-t)^2/(1-2t)$: indeed $E_n(2,\ldots,2)=(-1)^n2^{n+1}[H^n](1+H)^{2n+2}(1+2H)^{-n-1}$, and the substitution $t=-H$ converts one extraction into the other.  For each small $z$ the equation $y=z\varphi(y)$ has exactly one root $y(z)$ near the origin, and the residue form of Lagrange inversion, summing the geometric series $\sum_n(z\varphi(t)/t)^n$ on a small contour and picking up the simple pole at $y(z)$, gives
\[
A(z)=\sum_{n\ge0}[t^n]\varphi(t)^{n+1}z^n=\frac{\varphi(y)}{1-z\varphi'(y)};
\]
inverting $y=z\varphi(y)$ gives $z=y(1-2y)/(2(1-y)^2)$, and the residue value simplifies to $2(1-y)^3/(1-3y)$, the stated parametrization.  Differentiating along it shows that $(1+z)(1-8z)A''-9(1+4z)A'-24A$ is a rational function of $y$ that vanishes identically, a finite identity checked by exact expansion, and extracting the coefficient of $z^n$ from this differential equation yields the recurrence.

Now the three clauses.  Set $q_n=Q_{n+1}/Q_n$, so the recurrence reads $q_{n+1}=\tfrac{7n+9}{n+2}+\tfrac{4(2n+3)}{(n+1)q_n}$, strictly decreasing as a function of $q_n$.  Write $\delta_n=8-q_n$, and call the two-sided bound $4/(n+2)<\delta_n<4/(n+1)$ the corridor at index $n$.  The corridor holds at the base $n=5$: $Q_5=6912$ and $Q_6=51{,}072$ give $\delta_5=11/18\in(4/7,4/6)$.  For the step, the map carries the corridor at index $n$ into the interval bounded by the images of its two walls, since it is decreasing, and the two inequalities placing that image inside the corridor at index $n+1$ clear denominators to polynomials with positive coefficients in $n-5$; so the corridor propagates to every $n\ge5$.  The corridor gives $q_n<8$; it gives $q_{n+1}>8-4/(n+2)>q_n$, the strict increase; and both walls are asymptotic to $4/n$, so $q_n\to8$ with $8-q_n\sim4/n$.  For $2\le n\le4$ the ratios are $16/3$, $15/2$, and $36/5$, all below $8$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Normalized merge monotonicity, resolved true]\label{x2}
For every $n\ge3$, every multidegree $\mathbf d$ with $r\ge2$, and every pair $i\ne j$,
\[
\frac{E_n(M_{ij}\mathbf d)}{D(M_{ij}\mathbf d)}>\frac{E_n(\mathbf d)}{D(\mathbf d)}.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{x:merge} below.

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of normalised merge monotonicity]\label{x:merge}
Put $a_\ell=d_\ell-1$, and let $a=a_i$ and $b=a_j$ for the entries being merged.  Then
\[
\frac{E_n(M_{ij}\mathbf d)}{D(M_{ij}\mathbf d)}-
\frac{E_n(\mathbf d)}{D(\mathbf d)}
=ab[u^{n-2}]\frac{1}{(1+u)^2(1-au)(1-bu)(1-(a+b)u)}
\prod_{\ell\ne i,j}\frac{1}{1-a_\ell u}>0.
\]
Consequently Conjecture~\ref{x2} holds in full.  The same inequality also holds in dimension $n=2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Calabi--Yau condition gives $\sum_\ell a_\ell=n+1$.  The Chern-class formula and the coefficient substitution $t=u/(1+u)$ give
\[
\frac{E_n(\mathbf d)}{D(\mathbf d)}
=[t^n]\frac{(1-t)^{n+r+1}}{\prod_\ell(1-d_\ell t)}
=[u^n]\frac{1}{(1+u)^2}\prod_\ell\frac{1}{1-a_\ell u}.
\]
The merge replaces $a,b$ by $a+b$.  Subtracting the two rational series and using
\[
\frac{1}{1-(a+b)u}-\frac{1}{(1-au)(1-bu)}
=\frac{ab u^2}{(1-au)(1-bu)(1-(a+b)u)}
\]
gives the displayed coefficient formula.  For every integer $c\ge1$ and $q\ge0$,
\[
[u^q]\frac{1}{(1+u)(1-cu)}=\frac{c^{q+1}+(-1)^q}{c+1}\ge0.
\]
Pair the two factors $(1+u)^{-1}$ with $(1-au)^{-1}$ and $(1-bu)^{-1}$.  Every remaining factor is a geometric series with nonnegative coefficients.  The coefficient of degree $n-2$ is strictly positive because $(1-(a+b)u)^{-1}$ supplies the term $(a+b)^{n-2}u^{n-2}$ while all other factors supply their constant terms.  Since $ab>0$, the merge increment is positive.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Maximal-degree merge monotonicity]\label{x3}
For every $n\ge3$ and every $\mathbf d$ with $r\ge2$, if $d_i=\max_jd_j$ then $E_n(M_{ik}\mathbf d)>E_n(\mathbf d)$ for every $k\ne i$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Extremal multidegrees]\label{x4}
For every $n\ge3$ and every multidegree $\mathbf d$,
\[
E_n(\underbrace{2,\ldots,2}_{n+1})\le E_n(\mathbf d)\le E_n(n+2),
\]
with equality only at the all-quadric and hypersurface configurations.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Dimension-wise Euler gcd law]\label{x5}
Let $g_n$ be the greatest common divisor of $\chi(X^{(n)}_{\mathbf d})$ over all multidegrees at dimension $n$.  Then
\[
g_n=\frac{24}{\gcd(24,n)}\times
\begin{cases}
2,&n\equiv0,2\pmod8,\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
\end{conjecture}

\section{Middle Betti numbers of Brieskorn--Pham links}

\begin{conjecture}[Five-link cap extremum]\label{x6}
For $N=4$ and every cap $A\ge9$, every sorted Fano tuple $\mathbf a$ with $\max_ia_i=A$ satisfies $b_2(L(\mathbf a))\le A-1$, with equality only for $\mathbf a=(2,2,A,A)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Seven-link cap extremum]\label{x7}
For $N=5$ and every cap $A\ge13$, every sorted Fano tuple with $\max_ia_i=A$ satisfies $b_3(L(\mathbf a))\le(A-1)(A-2)$, with equality only for $(2,2,A,A,A)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Connected gcd-graph positivity]\label{x8}
For $N=4$, if the graph on $\{1,2,3,4\}$ joining $i\sim j$ when $\gcd(a_i,a_j)>1$ is connected, then $b_2(L(\mathbf a))\ge1$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Common-scale monotonicity, resolved true]\label{x9}
For every $N\ge4$ and every base tuple $\mathbf a$, the scale sequence satisfies $\beta_{\mathbf a}(k+1)\ge\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)$ for every $k\ge1$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true: Proposition~\ref{x:ehrhart} identifies the scale sequence as a sum of interior Ehrhart counting functions of $N-1$ integral weighted hypersimplices, whose forward differences of every order through $N-1$ are nonnegative, strictly more than monotonicity.

\begin{proposition}[Ehrhart model for common scaling]\label{x:ehrhart}
For a base tuple $\mathbf a$ and $1\le m\le N-1$ let $P_m(\mathbf a)=\{x\in\mathbf R^N:0\le x_i\le a_i,\ \sum_ix_i/a_i=m\}$, a lattice polytope of dimension $N-1$.  Then
\[
\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)=\sum_{m=1}^{N-1}\#\bigl(\operatorname{relint}(kP_m(\mathbf a))\cap\Z^N\bigr),
\]
and consequently every forward difference of the scale sequence of order $r\le N-1$ is nonnegative at every $k\ge1$.  In particular Conjecture~\ref{x9} is true, and the discrete-convexity clause of Conjecture~\ref{x10} is true.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The multiplication map $\prod_{j\in J}\mu_{a_j}\to\mu_{\operatorname{lcm}(a_j)}$ on roots of unity is surjective with kernel of order $\prod_{j\in J}a_j/\operatorname{lcm}_{j\in J}a_j$, so that quantity counts the tuples over $J$ with product $1$; by inclusion--exclusion over the coordinates forced to equal $1$, the Milnor--Orlik sum counts the tuples $(\zeta_1,\ldots,\zeta_N)$ with each $\zeta_i$ a nontrivial $a_i$-th root of unity and $\prod_i\zeta_i=1$, the empty set contributing the term $(-1)^N$.  For the scaled tuple $k\mathbf a$ write $\zeta_i=\exp(2\pi ir_i/(ka_i))$ with $0<r_i<ka_i$; the product is $1$ exactly when $\sum_ir_i/a_i=km$ for an integer $m$, and since each summand lies strictly between $0$ and $k$, the integer satisfies $1\le m\le N-1$.  These conditions say precisely that $(r_1,\ldots,r_N)$ is a lattice point of the relative interior of $kP_m(\mathbf a)$, proving the displayed identity.  Each $P_m(\mathbf a)$ is integral: the substitution $y_i=x_i/a_i$ carries it to the hypersimplex $\{0\le y_i\le1,\ \sum_iy_i=m\}$, whose vertices are $0$--$1$ vectors, so every vertex of $P_m(\mathbf a)$ has coordinates $0$ or $a_i$.  For a lattice polytope $P$ of dimension $d$ with $h^{*}$-vector $(h^{*}_0,\ldots,h^{*}_d)$, Ehrhart reciprocity~\cite{Mac71} turns the expansion $L_P(k)=\sum_ih^{*}_i\binom{k+d-i}{d}$ into $L_{P^\circ}(k)=\sum_ih^{*}_i\binom{k+i-1}{d}$, and the $h^{*}_i$ are nonnegative integers~\cite{Sta80}; applying the forward difference $r$ times gives $\Delta^rL_{P^\circ}(k)=\sum_ih^{*}_i\binom{k+i-1}{d-r}\ge0$ for $0\le r\le d$.  Summing over the $N-1$ polytopes $P_m(\mathbf a)$, all of dimension $N-1$, yields $\Delta^r\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)\ge0$ for every $k\ge1$ and $r\le N-1$; the case $r=1$ is Conjecture~\ref{x9}, and the case $r=2$ is the convexity clause of Conjecture~\ref{x10}.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Scale convexity and log-concavity]\label{x10}
For every $N\ge4$ and every base tuple, the scale sequence is simultaneously discretely convex and log-concave:
\[
\beta_{\mathbf a}(k+1)-2\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)+\beta_{\mathbf a}(k-1)\ge0,
\qquad
\beta_{\mathbf a}(k)^2\ge\beta_{\mathbf a}(k-1)\,\beta_{\mathbf a}(k+1).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\section{Hochster strands of flag moment-angle complexes}

\begin{conjecture}[Hochster interval support]\label{x11}
For every finite simple graph $G$ and every $r\ge0$, the support $\{s:h_{r,s}(G)>0\}$ is an interval of integers.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Hochster-strand log-concavity]\label{x12}
For every $G$, $r$, and $s$,
\[
h_{r,s}(G)^2\ge h_{r,s-1}(G)\,h_{r,s+1}(G).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strand Hurwitz stability]\label{x13}
For every $G$ and $r$ with $H_{G,r}$ nonconstant, every zero of $H_{G,r}(z)/z^{\min\operatorname{supp}}$, the strand polynomial divided by its lowest monomial, satisfies $\operatorname{Re}z<0$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Cycle extremality at connectivity two]\label{x14}
If $G$ is $2$-vertex-connected on $m\ge4$ vertices, then $h_{0,s}(G)\le h_{0,s}(C_m)$ for every $s$, with equality in every coefficient only for the cycle $C_m$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict component-strand log-concavity]\label{x15}
For every connected noncomplete graph, every triple of positive consecutive component-strand coefficients is strictly log-concave: $h_{0,s}^2>h_{0,s-1}h_{0,s+1}$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Torus mapping tori with signed monodromy}

\begin{conjecture}[Parity-strand unimodality]\label{x16}
For every orientation-preserving signed permutation $A$, the sequences $(f_0(A),f_2(A),f_4(A),\ldots)$ and $(f_1(A),f_3(A),f_5(A),\ldots)$ are unimodal.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Mapping-torus Betti unimodality]\label{x17}
For every orientation-preserving signed permutation $A$ in dimension $d$, the Betti sequence $(b_0(M_A),\ldots,b_{d+1}(M_A))$ is unimodal.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Sparse-cycle minimizer structure]\label{x18}
For every $d\ge8$, some minimizer of the total Betti number $\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)$ over orientation-preserving signed permutations has no positive signed cycle when $d$ is even, exactly one when $d$ is odd, and pairwise distinct negative-cycle lengths.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Square-root-two compression exponent, resolved false]\label{x19}
Let $M_d$ be the minimum of $\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)$ over orientation-preserving signed permutations in dimension $d$.  Then $M_d^{1/d}\to\sqrt2$ as $d\to\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False, by the Landau floor of Proposition~\ref{x:landau} below: the true compression exponent is $2$, and the square-root-two fit was a finite-size illusion.

\begin{proposition}[Landau floor for the minimal total cohomology]\label{x:landau}
For every signed permutation $A$ on $\Z^d$,
\[
\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)\;\ge\;\frac{2^{d+1}}{\operatorname{ord}(A)}\;\ge\;\frac{2^{d}}{g(d)},
\]
where $g(d)$ is Landau's function, the maximal order of a permutation of $d$ letters.  Consequently $2^d/g(d)\le M_d\le2^{d+1}$, and since $\log g(d)\sim\sqrt{d\log d}$, the minimal total cohomology satisfies $M_d^{1/d}\to2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{x:orbit}, $\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q)=2P$ with $P=\sum_kp_k=\sum_k\dim_{\mathbf Q}\ker(\wedge^kA-I)$, the dimension of the fixed space of $A$ on the full exterior algebra $\Lambda^\bullet(\mathbf Q^d)$.  Since $A$ has finite order $m$, that fixed space is the invariant subspace of the cyclic group generated by $A$, and averaging characters gives $P=\frac1m\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\operatorname{tr}\Lambda^\bullet(A^j)=\frac1m\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\det(I+A^j)$.  Every term is nonnegative: $A^j$ is again a signed permutation, and a signed cycle of length $L$ with holonomy $\varepsilon$ contributes $\prod_{\zeta^L=\varepsilon}(1+\zeta)=1-(-1)^L\varepsilon\in\{0,2\}$ to the determinant.  The term $j=0$ alone is $2^d$, so $P\ge2^d/m$.  If the underlying permutation has cycle lengths $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r$, then $A^{\operatorname{lcm}(\ell_i)}$ is diagonal with entries $\pm1$, so $m\le2\operatorname{lcm}(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r)\le2g(d)$, giving the displayed floor.  The identity monodromy gives $M_d\le2\cdot2^d$, and Landau's asymptotic $\log g(d)\sim\sqrt{d\log d}$~\cite{Lan03} makes $(2^d/g(d))^{1/d}\to2$, so the two-sided bound forces $M_d^{1/d}\to2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Resolution]\label{x19:res}
The conjecture is false: by Proposition~\ref{x:landau} the compression exponent is $2$, not $\sqrt2$, with the whole deviation from $2^{d+1}$ confined to a subexponential factor at most $2g(d)$.  The finite scans could not separate the two laws, because $\log_2g(d)\approx d/2$ throughout the tested range---$\log_2g(21)\approx9.7$ against $d/2=10.5$---so the floor $2^d/g(d)$ is numerically indistinguishable from $2^{d/2}$ there, and the square-root fit was a finite-size illusion.  The observed minima respect the floor with room to spare, $M_{12}=160$ against $2^{12}/g(12)=68$, and the surviving question is the exact order of the subexponential correction $\log_2(2^{d+1}/M_d)$, now pinned between $0$ and $\log_2(2g(d))=O(\sqrt{d\log d}\,)$.  The torsion budget of Conjecture~\ref{x20} is untouched: it is consistent with the true growth, and no incompatibility remains once the exponent is corrected.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Torsion budget inequality]\label{x20}
For every signed permutation $A\in SL(d,\Z)$,
\[
\tau(A)\le\sum_kb_k(M_A;\mathbf Q),
\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad
\sum_kn_k\le2\sum_kp_k
\]
in the orbit counts of Proposition~\ref{x:orbit}.
\end{conjecture}

\part{Shape laws for enumerative geometric invariants}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 12 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The twenty conjectures below continue the topological turn of Part~VII with a second discipline: take a classical enumerative invariant of a geometric family, compute it exactly across the family, and conjecture the shape of the resulting array.  They form five programmes.  A hypersurface programme (Conjectures~\ref{y1} to~\ref{y4}) studies the Euler indices of anticanonical hypersurfaces in products of projective spaces under an order dual to Part~VII's: where Part~VII merged defining equations at fixed ambient, these statements split ambient factors at fixed dimension.  A Hilbert-scheme programme (Conjectures~\ref{y5} to~\ref{y8}) studies the Betti profiles of G\"ottsche's product over a proved pair of exact moment identities.  An orbifold programme (Conjectures~\ref{y9} to~\ref{y12}) studies the twisted-sector age histograms of cyclic Calabi--Yau quotients.  An entropy programme (Conjectures~\ref{y13} to~\ref{y16}) studies the exterior torsion-entropy profile of hyperbolic toral automorphisms.  A matroid programme (Conjectures~\ref{y17} to~\ref{y20}) lifts the Hochster strand laws of Part~VII from graphs to simple binary matroids.  The calibration layer is exact throughout: the Chern-class evaluation, G\"ottsche's product, the age grading of the McKay correspondence, the eigenvalue evaluation of torsion growth rates, and Hochster's formula.  Retained controls again mark every calibrated boundary.  The limit clauses of Conjectures~\ref{y3} and~\ref{y7} assert limits; Conjectures~\ref{y10} to~\ref{y12} are random-model statements resting on Monte Carlo evidence, with Conjectures~\ref{y10} and~\ref{y11} likely within reach of classical equidistribution machinery; Conjecture~\ref{y9} lies close to classical methods.  Since deposit, Conjectures~\ref{y15} and~\ref{y16} have been proved by convexity, while Conjectures~\ref{y17} to~\ref{y20} have been refuted by two explicit graphic matroids.

For a partition $\lambda=(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r)$ of $d+1$ let $A_\lambda=\prod_i\mathbf{CP}^{\lambda_i}$ and let $X_\lambda\in|{-K_{A_\lambda}}|$ be a smooth anticanonical hypersurface, a Calabi--Yau $d$-fold; its Euler characteristic is evaluated exactly from $c(TA_\lambda)=\prod_i(1+H_i)^{\lambda_i+1}$ and $[X_\lambda]=\sum_i(\lambda_i+1)H_i$, and we write $E_d(\lambda)=(-1)^d\chi(X_\lambda)$.  A \emph{split} $S_{m,a}\lambda$ replaces one part $m$ by $a$ and $m-a$; a \emph{dominance move} transfers one unit from a smaller positive part to a weakly larger part.  For $b\ge1$ define $h_b(n,k)$ by
\[
\sum_{n,k\ge0}h_b(n,k)\,q^nz^k=
\prod_{m\ge1}\frac{1}{(1-z^{m-1}q^m)(1-z^mq^m)^b(1-z^{m+1}q^m)},
\]
so that $h_{22}(n,k)=b_{2k}(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(\mathrm{K3}))$ by G\"ottsche's theorem~\cite{Go90}.  For $r\ge3$ and weights $a_1,\ldots,a_d\in(\Z/r)^\times$ with $\sum_ia_i\equiv0\pmod r$, the cyclic quotient $\tfrac1r(a_1,\ldots,a_d)$ has ages $\operatorname{age}(j)=\tfrac1r\sum_i(ja_i\bmod r)$ for $1\le j<r$, integers between $1$ and $d-1$, with histogram $N_k=\#\{j:\operatorname{age}(j)=k\}$ grading twisted sectors in the McKay correspondence~\cite{Reid02}.  For a hyperbolic $A\in SL(d,\Z)$ with eigenvalue log-moduli $x_1,\ldots,x_d$ summing to zero, set
\[
h_k(x)=\sum_{|S|=k}\Bigl(\sum_{i\in S}x_i\Bigr)_{\!+},\qquad1\le k\le d-1;
\]
away from exterior resonances $h_k$ is the exponential growth rate of $|\det(\wedge^kA^n-I)|$, the degree-$k$ torsion order of the mapping torus of $A^n$, since each factor $|\lambda_S^n-1|$ grows like $|\lambda_S|^n$ or stays bounded~\cite{BV13,BLP10}.  Finally, for a simple binary matroid $M$ on ground set $E$ with independence complex $\Delta(M)$, put $\beta(A)=\dim\widetilde H_{r(A)-1}(\Delta(M|_A);\mathbf F_2)$ and
\[
H_{c,s}(M)=\sum_{\substack{A\subseteq E,\ |A|=s\\|A|-r(A)=c}}\beta(A),
\]
the aggregated multigraded Betti numbers of the Stanley--Reisner ring by Hochster's formula~\cite{Hoch77}, graded by restriction size and nullity, with strand polynomial $\sum_sH_{c,s}(M)z^s$ at each fixed nullity $c$.  The profile $h_b(n,k)$ and the spectral functional $h_k(x)$ are unrelated symbols, each local to its own programme.

\begin{proposition}[Profile moment identities]\label{y:mom}
For every $b\ge1$ and $n\ge1$, the probability measure on $\{0,\ldots,2n\}$ proportional to $h_b(n,\cdot)$ has mean exactly $n$ and variance exactly $2n/(b+2)$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Euler indices of anticanonical hypersurfaces in products}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict dominance law]\label{y1}
For every $d\ge3$ and every dominance move $\lambda\to\mu$ on partitions of $d+1$,
\[
E_d(\mu)>E_d(\lambda).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict refinement contraction]\label{y2}
For every $d\ge3$, every $\lambda$, and every nontrivial split of one part,
\[
E_d(S_{m,a}\lambda)<E_d(\lambda).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[The $8/9$ refinement barrier]\label{y3}
Let $S_d$ be the maximum of $E_d(S_{m,a}\lambda)/E_d(\lambda)$ over all $\lambda$ and all one-part splits.  Then $S_d\to8/9$ as $d\to\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Positive interaction of disjoint refinements]\label{y4}
For $d\ge6$ and splits $S,T$ acting on distinct parts of $\lambda$,
\[
E_d(\lambda)\,E_d(ST\lambda)>E_d(S\lambda)\,E_d(T\lambda).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\section{Betti profiles of Hilbert schemes of surfaces}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict Betti log-concavity]\label{y5}
For every $b\ge3$, $n\ge1$, and $0<k<2n$,
\[
h_b(n,k)^2>h_b(n,k-1)\,h_b(n,k+1).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Cohomology-seed total positivity]\label{y6}
For every $b\ge1$, $n\ge1$, and $0\le k<n$,
\[
h_{b+1}(n,k+1)\,h_b(n,k)\ge h_{b+1}(n,k)\,h_b(n,k+1).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Kurtosis trichotomy]\label{y7}
Let $\kappa_b(n)$ be the standardized fourth moment of the profile of Proposition~\ref{y:mom}.  Then: for $b=3,4,5$, $\kappa_b(n)$ increases strictly to $21/5$; for $b=6$ it has a unique maximum at $n=9$ and decreases thereafter to $21/5$; for $b=7$, $\kappa_7(1)=\kappa_7(2)$ and it decreases strictly thereafter; for $b\ge8$ it decreases strictly to $21/5$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Fixed-charge log-concavity]\label{y8}
For every $b\ge3$ and fixed $j\ge0$, the sequence $n\mapsto h_b(n,n+j)$, $n\ge\max(1,j)$, is strictly log-concave.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Twisted-sector ages of cyclic quotients}

\begin{conjecture}[Sharp low-dimensional age unimodality]\label{y9}
For $d=4$ and $d=5$, the age histogram $(N_1,\ldots,N_{d-1})$ is unimodal for every isolated cyclic Calabi--Yau quotient $\tfrac1r(a_1,\ldots,a_d)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Gaussian bulk age law]\label{y10}
Let $r$ be prime and let distinct nonzero weights $a_1,\ldots,a_d$ be uniform conditioned on zero sum.  If $d,r\to\infty$ with $d=o(r)$, then in probability the empirical law of $(\operatorname{age}(j)-d/2)/\sqrt{d/12}$ over $1\le j<r$ converges weakly to the standard Gaussian.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Local central-sector law]\label{y11}
Under the sampling law of Conjecture~\ref{y10} with $d$ even, $N_{d/2}/(r-1)\sim\sqrt{6/(\pi d)}$, and uniformly for $|k-d/2|=O(\sqrt{d\log d})$ the ratio $N_k/(r-1)$ is asymptotic to the corresponding lattice Gaussian mass.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[High-sampling restoration of unimodality]\label{y12}
In the random prime-order model, if $r/d^3\to\infty$ then the entire age histogram is unimodal with probability tending to one.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Exterior torsion entropy of toral automorphisms}

\begin{conjecture}[Exterior torsion-entropy log-concavity]\label{y13}
For every nonzero zero-sum real spectrum $x$ and $1<k<d-1$,
\[
h_k(x)^2\ge h_{k-1}(x)\,h_{k+1}(x).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Equality rigidity]\label{y14}
If an interior equality $h_k(x)^2=h_{k-1}(x)h_{k+1}(x)$ occurs, then the multiset $\{x_1,\ldots,x_d\}$ has exactly two distinct values.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Sharp binomial upper envelope, resolved true]\label{y15}
For every zero-sum spectrum and every $1\le k\le d-1$,
\[
h_k(x)\le\binom{d-2}{k-1}h_1(x),
\]
with equality in every degree for spectra proportional to $(1,0,\ldots,0,-1)$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{y:upper} below.

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of the sharp binomial upper envelope]\label{y:upper}
Conjecture~\ref{y15} holds in full.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If $h_1(x)=0$, then the zero-sum spectrum is zero and the result is immediate.  Otherwise scale so that $h_1(x)=1$.  Fix the sets $P$ and $N$ of positive and negative coordinates.  The positive coordinates lie in the simplex $\sum_{i\in P}x_i=1$, while the magnitudes of the negative coordinates lie in the simplex $\sum_{j\in N}(-x_j)=1$.  On the closure of their product,
\[
h_k(x)=\sum_{|S|=k}\left(\sum_{i\in S}x_i\right)_+
\]
is a convex function, since it is a sum of positive parts of linear forms.  A convex function on a compact polytope attains a maximum at a vertex.  At such a vertex exactly one coordinate is $1$, exactly one is $-1$, and every other coordinate is zero.  A $k$-subset contributes $1$ precisely when it contains the positive coordinate and avoids the negative coordinate.  There are $\binom{d-2}{k-1}$ such subsets.  Rescaling proves the inequality, and the vertex calculation proves the asserted equality for spectra proportional to $(1,0,\ldots,0,-1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Two-level lower envelope, resolved true]\label{y16}
Normalize $h_1(x)=1$.  For every $d$ and $k$, the minimum of $h_k$ over zero-sum spectra is attained by a two-level spectrum $x^{p,q}$ with $p$ entries $1/p$, $q$ entries $-1/q$, and $d-p-q$ zeros.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{y:lower} below.

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of the two-level lower envelope]\label{y:lower}
Conjecture~\ref{y16} holds in full.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $x$ be a zero-sum spectrum with $h_1(x)=1$, and let $P$ and $N$ be its positive and negative index sets with sizes $p$ and $q$.  The function $h_k$ is convex and invariant under permutations within $P$ and within $N$.  Replace two positive coordinates by their average.  The new spectrum is the midpoint of the original spectrum and the spectrum obtained by swapping those coordinates, so convexity and symmetry show that $h_k$ cannot increase.  Repeating this operation makes every positive coordinate equal to $1/p$.  The same argument applied to the negative magnitudes makes every negative coordinate equal to $-1/q$.  The zero coordinates remain zero.  Thus
\[
h_k(x^{p,q})\le h_k(x).
\]
There are only finitely many pairs $p,q\ge1$ with $p+q\le d$, and every $x^{p,q}$ is admissible.  The global minimum is therefore attained by one of these two-level spectra.
\end{proof}

\section{Matroidal Hochster tables}

\begin{conjecture}[Nullity-strand interval support, resolved false]\label{y17}
For every simple binary matroid $M$ and every nullity $c$, the support $\{s:H_{c,s}(M)>0\}$ is an interval of integers.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False, by Proposition~\ref{y:k33} below: the cycle matroid of $K_{3,3}$ has nullity-one strand $(9,0,6)$ at sizes four to six.

\begin{proposition}[A graphic nullity-strand certificate]\label{y:k33}
Let $M=M(K_{3,3})$ be the cycle matroid of the complete bipartite graph.  Then
\[
H_{1,4}(M)=9,\qquad H_{1,5}(M)=0,\qquad H_{1,6}(M)=6.
\]
The matroid $M$ is simple, binary, and $3$-connected.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In a nullity-one graphic restriction, the top reduced homology is nonzero exactly when the restricted edge set is a circuit.  Indeed, any edge outside the unique circuit is a coloop, which makes the independence complex a cone.  The circuits of $M(K_{3,3})$ are the cycles of $K_{3,3}$.  There are $\binom32^2=9$ cycles of length four, no cycles of length five by bipartiteness, and six Hamilton cycles of length six.  This gives the displayed strand.  The graph $K_{3,3}$ is simple and $3$-vertex-connected, so its cycle matroid is simple, binary, and $3$-connected.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Resolution]\label{y17:res}
Conjecture~\ref{y17} is false.  Proposition~\ref{y:k33} gives nullity-one support $\{4,6\}$, with an internal gap at size five.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Row-and-column log-concavity, resolved false]\label{y18}
Both coordinate directions of the table are log-concave: $H_{c,s}^2\ge H_{c,s-1}H_{c,s+1}$ and $H_{c,s}^2\ge H_{c-1,s}H_{c+1,s}$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False: the same nullity-one strand $(9,0,6)$ of $K_{3,3}$ gives the horizontal failure $0^2<9\cdot6$; the vertical direction remains a separate open question.

\begin{remark}[Resolution]\label{y18:res}
Conjecture~\ref{y18} is false.  For the matroid of Proposition~\ref{y:k33}, the horizontal inequality at $(c,s)=(1,5)$ reads $0^2\ge9\cdot6$.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Nearest-rhombus log-concavity, resolved false]\label{y19}
Both diagonal directions are log-concave: $H_{c,s}^2\ge H_{c-1,s-1}H_{c+1,s+1}$ and $H_{c,s}^2\ge H_{c-1,s+1}H_{c+1,s-1}$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False, by Proposition~\ref{y:rhombus} below: a ten-edge graphic matroid has $H_{2,6}=2$, $H_{1,5}=2$, $H_{3,7}=3$, giving $4<6$.

\begin{proposition}[A graphic rhombus certificate]\label{y:rhombus}
Let $G$ have vertex set $\{0,\ldots,6\}$ and edge set
\[
\{03,04,05,13,14,23,24,26,34,56\},
\]
where $ij$ denotes the edge $\{i,j\}$.  For its cycle matroid $M(G)$,
\[
H_{1,5}=2,\qquad H_{2,6}=2,\qquad H_{3,7}=3.
\]
The graph $G$ is simple and $2$-connected.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a restriction $A$, its contribution is the absolute reduced Euler characteristic of its independence complex,
\[
\beta(A)=\left|-1+\sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne I\subseteq A\\I\ {\rm independent}}}(-1)^{|I|-1}\right|.
\]
Deletion and contraction on the ten displayed edges gives the following complete list of nonzero contributions to the three cells.  At $(1,5)$ the two five-cycles $\{03,05,23,26,56\}$ and $\{04,05,24,26,56\}$ contribute one each.  At $(2,6)$ the restriction $\{03,04,13,14,23,24\}$ contributes two.  At $(3,7)$ that restriction together with $34$ contributes three.  Every other restriction of the required size and nullity has zero top reduced homology.  Summing gives the displayed values.  Deleting any one vertex leaves $G$ connected, so $G$ is $2$-connected.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Resolution]\label{y19:res}
Conjecture~\ref{y19} is false.  Proposition~\ref{y:rhombus} gives
\[
H_{2,6}^2=4<6=H_{1,5}H_{3,7}.
\]
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Hurwitz stability of nullity strands, resolved false]\label{y20}
For every simple binary matroid and every nonconstant nullity strand, every zero of the strand polynomial $\sum_sH_{c,s}(M)\,z^{s-s_{\min}}$, with $s_{\min}$ the least size in its support, has strictly negative real part.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} False: strict Hurwitz stability fails at the boundary of the left half-plane, linked to the same support gap that refutes Conjectures~\ref{y17} and~\ref{y18}.

\begin{remark}[Resolution]\label{y20:res}
Conjecture~\ref{y20} is false.  The nullity-one polynomial of Proposition~\ref{y:k33}, after removing its lowest monomial, is $9+6z^2$.  Its zeros are $\pm i\sqrt{3/2}$ and have real part zero.
\end{remark}

\part{Spectra and filtrations of combinatorial geometries}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 12 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The twenty conjectures below extend the geometric turn of Parts~VII and~VIII to spectra and filtrations.  They form four programmes.  A toric programme (Conjectures~\ref{z1} to~\ref{z5}) studies the $h$-polynomials of graph associahedra, the even-Betti spectra of their smooth toric varieties, through root location, interlacing, and a Mahler-measure order.  A McKay programme (Conjectures~\ref{z6} to~\ref{z10}) filters the Cayley graph of an isolated cyclic Calabi--Yau quotient by orbifold age and conjectures one-crest laws for the low homology of the resulting flag complexes.  A torsion programme (Conjectures~\ref{z11} to~\ref{z15}) studies microcanonical ensembles of lens-space sine factors, their temperature-dependent sector shapes, and a $\zeta(3)$ central parabola over the log-sine moment identities proved below.  An entropy programme (Conjectures~\ref{z16} to~\ref{z20}) orders the boundary-map pseudodeterminants of simplicial spheres across degree and under stellar refinement.  The calibration layer is exact where the objects are: tubing $f$-vectors, $\mathbf F_2$ homology, and coefficient convolutions are integer computations, while root locations and pseudodeterminants are checked spectrally in floating point and.  Four retained controls mark calibrated boundaries.  Conjectures~\ref{z13} and~\ref{z14} assert limits in a random or iterated-limit model and are the boldest; Conjectures~\ref{z1}, \ref{z5}, \ref{z8}, \ref{z13}, and~\ref{z14} sit closest to classical machinery; and Conjecture~\ref{z14} deliberately strengthens the annealed Gaussian age law drawn from Conjecture~\ref{y10} to a joint decoupling law.

For a connected simple graph $G$ on $n\ge2$ vertices, a \emph{tube} is a proper connected induced subgraph, two tubes being compatible when nested or disjoint without adjacency, and a \emph{tubing} is a set of pairwise compatible tubes; the graph associahedron $P_G$ is the simple $(n-1)$-polytope whose codimension-$k$ faces are the tubings with $k$ tubes~\cite{PRW08}.  Write $h_G(t)=\sum_ih_i(G)t^i$ for its $h$-polynomial, so that $h_i(G)=b_{2i}(X_G)$ for the smooth projective toric variety of the normal fan, a positive palindromic sequence; put $\mathcal M(G)=\sum_{h_G(\rho)=0}\log\max(1,|\rho|)$, the logarithmic Mahler measure, and $\mu_G(i)=h_i(G)/h_G(1)$.  For an odd prime $p$ and pairwise distinct weights $a=(a_1,\ldots,a_d)\in(\mathbb F_p^\times)^d$ with $\sum_ia_i=0$, the quotient $\tfrac1p(a_1,\ldots,a_d)$ acts on the vertex set $\Z/p$, including $0$, through the Cayley graph $x\sim y$ when $y-x\in\{\pm a_1,\ldots,\pm a_d\}$; with $\operatorname{age}(j)=\tfrac1p\sum_i[ja_i]_p$ and $\operatorname{age}(0)=0$, let $K_k$ be the flag complex of the induced graph on $\{j:\operatorname{age}(j)\le k\}$ for $0\le k\le d-1$, and write $c_k$ and $b_{q,k}$ for its number of components and its $q$-th $\mathbf F_2$ Betti number.  For a prime $p\ge5$ let $\Omega_{p,d,m}$ be the tuples $x\in\{1,\ldots,p-1\}^d$ with $\sum_ix_i=mp$, let $H_p(x)=\sum_i\log(2\sin(\pi x_i/p))$, the lens-space log-torsion energy~\cite{NO92}, and set $Z_{p,d,m}(s)=\mathbb E[e^{sH_p}]$ and $\Phi_{p,d,m}=\mathbb E[H_p]$ over uniform $\Omega_{p,d,m}$.  Finally, for a finite simplicial $d$-sphere $K$ with boundary maps $\partial_k$, put $D_k(K)=\operatorname{pdet}(\partial_k\partial_k^{*})$, the product of nonzero eigenvalues, $E_k(K)=\log D_k(K)$, $r_k(K)=\operatorname{rank}\partial_k$, and $A_k(K)=E_k(K)/r_k(K)$, the entropy per boundary rank, with higher matrix-tree readings of these determinants in~\cite{DKM09,MMRW15}.

The sector expectations $Z_{p,d,m}$ and $\Phi_{p,d,m}$ are defined only when $\Omega_{p,d,m}$ is nonempty.  This holds exactly when $d\le mp\le d(p-1)$.

\begin{proposition}[Log-sine moment identities]\label{z:logsine}
For every integer $p\ge2$,
\[
\prod_{j=1}^{p-1}2\sin\frac{\pi j}{p}=p,
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\int_0^1\Bigl(u-\tfrac12\Bigr)^2\log(2\sin\pi u)\,du=-\frac{\zeta(3)}{2\pi^2}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\section{Toric spectra of graph associahedra}

\begin{conjecture}[Universal negative real roots]\label{z1}
For every connected simple graph $G$, every zero of $h_G(t)$ is real and strictly negative.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Connected-deletion interlacing]\label{z2}
If $v$ is a vertex of $G$ with $G-v$ connected, the zeros of $h_{G-v}$ weakly interlace the zeros of $h_G$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict Mahler growth under edge addition]\label{z3}
For every missing edge $e$ of a connected graph $G$, $\mathcal M(G+e)>\mathcal M(G)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Edge addition centralizes the spectrum]\label{z4}
For every missing edge $e$ of a connected graph $G$ and every symmetric outer tail that is nonempty and proper, $\sum_{|i-c|\ge r}\mu_{G+e}(i)\le\sum_{|i-c|\ge r}\mu_G(i)$, where $c=(n-1)/2$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Path--star Mahler extremality]\label{z5}
For every tree $T$ on $n\ge4$ vertices, $\mathcal M(P_n)\le\mathcal M(T)\le\mathcal M(K_{1,n-1})$, with equality on the left only for the path and on the right only for the star.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Age-filtered McKay complexes}

\begin{conjecture}[One-crest component law]\label{z6}
For every isolated cyclic quotient with pairwise distinct weights, the component sequence $(c_0,\ldots,c_{d-1})$ is unimodal.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Early component crest]\label{z7}
The first index attaining $\max_kc_k$ is at most $\lfloor d/2\rfloor$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Half-age connectivity]\label{z8}
For $d\ge5$, the sublevel complex $K_{\lceil d/2\rceil}$ is connected.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[One-crest first homology]\label{z9}
The sequence $(b_{1,0},\ldots,b_{1,d-1})$ is unimodal.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[One-crest second homology]\label{z10}
The sequence $(b_{2,0},\ldots,b_{2,d-1})$ is unimodal.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Sine-torsion sectors of cyclic quotients}

\begin{conjecture}[Positive-temperature log-concavity]\label{z11}
For every $s>0$ and $2\le m\le d-2$ such that $\Omega_{p,d,q}$ is nonempty for $q\in\{m-1,m,m+1\}$, $Z_{p,d,m}(s)^2>Z_{p,d,m-1}(s)\,Z_{p,d,m+1}(s)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Concavity of mean log torsion]\label{z12}
For $2\le m\le d-2$ such that $\Omega_{p,d,q}$ is nonempty for $q\in\{m-1,m,m+1\}$, $2\Phi_{p,d,m}>\Phi_{p,d,m-1}+\Phi_{p,d,m+1}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[The $\zeta(3)$ central parabola]\label{z13}
Let $m_d$ satisfy $(m_d-d/2)/\sqrt{d/12}\to x$.  Then, taking $p\to\infty$ through primes first and $d\to\infty$ second,
\[
\lim_{d\to\infty}\lim_{p\to\infty}\Bigl(\Phi_{p,d,m_d}-\frac{d\log p}{p-1}\Bigr)=\frac{3\zeta(3)}{\pi^2}\bigl(1-x^2\bigr).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Age--torsion decoupling]\label{z14}
With weights uniform on distinct nonzero zero-sum tuples and $J$ uniform on $\mathbb F_p^\times$, the standardized pair of $\operatorname{age}_A(J)$ and of the centred log-torsion sum converges to a standard bivariate Gaussian as $p,d\to\infty$ with $d=o(p)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Negative-temperature reversal]\label{z15}
For $-1\le s<0$ and $2\le m\le d-2$ such that $\Omega_{p,d,q}$ is nonempty for $q\in\{m-1,m,m+1\}$, $Z_{p,d,m}(s)^2<Z_{p,d,m-1}(s)\,Z_{p,d,m+1}(s)$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Boundary-determinant entropy of simplicial spheres}

\begin{conjecture}[Log-concavity of boundary entropy]\label{z16}
For every simplicial $d$-sphere and $2\le k\le d-1$, $E_k(K)^2\ge E_{k-1}(K)\,E_{k+1}(K)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Entropy per rank decreases]\label{z17}
For every simplicial $d$-sphere, $A_1(K)\ge A_2(K)\ge\cdots\ge A_d(K)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Simplex rigidity of equality]\label{z18}
An adjacent equality $A_k(K)=A_{k+1}(K)$ occurs for some $k$ if and only if $K$ is the boundary of a simplex, in which case all adjacent equalities occur.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict stellar growth]\label{z19}
If $K'$ is obtained from $K$ by stellar subdivision of a facet, then $E_k(K')>E_k(K)$ for every $1\le k\le d$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Log-concave stellar increments]\label{z20}
With $\delta_k=E_k(K')-E_k(K)$ as in Conjecture~\ref{z19}, $\delta_k^2\ge\delta_{k-1}\delta_{k+1}$ for $2\le k\le d-1$.
\end{conjecture}

\part{Flux cohomology and protected index laws}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 12 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The twenty conjectures below carry the collection into string-geometric index theory.  They form three programmes.  A flux programme (Conjectures~\ref{aa1} to~\ref{aa8}) studies the cohomology of wedge multiplication by an integral three-form on a torus, the invariant-forms model of $H$-twisted cohomology, through saturation laws for random flux, sparse witnesses, and an arithmetic torsion staircase for the complete flux.  A profile programme (Conjectures~\ref{aa9} to~\ref{aa15}) studies the signed $\chi_y$-coefficient profile of projective Calabi--Yau complete intersections along the merge order of Part~VII, extending the proved Euler monotonicity of Conjecture~\ref{x2} to every protected coefficient, with root, log-concavity, and Mahler laws.  A signature programme (Conjectures~\ref{aa16} to~\ref{aa20}) does the same for the Hirzebruch signature, ending in a quadric ratio law with limit $27$ and a dimension-wise gcd law that is the signature sibling of Conjecture~\ref{x5}.  The calibration layer is exact where the objects are: finite-field cohomology, Smith normal forms, $\chi_y$-coefficients, signatures, merge inequalities, ratios, and gcds are integer or rational computations, while characteristic-zero ranks beyond dimension nine are dual-prime proxies and the root, Mahler, and radius laws of Conjectures~\ref{aa12}, \ref{aa14}, and~\ref{aa15} are checked spectrally in floating point and.  Two retained controls mark calibrated boundaries, a falsified all-prime torsion precursor and a falsified elementary-torsion strengthening.  Conjectures~\ref{aa1}, \ref{aa2}, \ref{aa4}, and~\ref{aa8} are stated in a random model and Conjecture~\ref{aa5} is stated in greater generality than the computations reach, the part's boldest statement; Conjectures~\ref{aa8}, \ref{aa13}, and~\ref{aa19} sit closest to classical machinery; and Conjecture~\ref{aa14} returns the Mahler order of Conjecture~\ref{z3} on a different polynomial.

For $n\ge3$ and a commutative ring $R$ let $E_n(R)=\Lambda^{*}R^n$, and for an integral three-form $H\in\Lambda^3\Z^n$ let $d_H$ be wedge multiplication by $H$, a differential because $H$ has odd degree; write $b_k(H;R)$ for the dimensions of the cohomology of $(E_n(R),d_H)$, $B(H;R)=\sum_kb_k(H;R)$, and $t_p(H)$ for the number of $p$-primary cyclic summands of the integral cohomology.  This complex is the invariant-forms model of $H$-twisted cohomology~\cite{Cav04}, whose K-theoretic refinement classifies Ramond--Ramond charges in a topologically nontrivial B-field~\cite{BM00}.  The \emph{generic target} $g_n$ is the cohomology profile on the Zariski-open locus of maximal wedge ranks, that of a generic principal ideal generated in degree three: for $n=2m$ it is $(3^{m-1},2\cdot3^{m-1},3^{m-1})$ in degrees $m-1,m,m+1$; for $n\equiv3\pmod4$ it is $3^{(n-1)/2}$ in each of the two middle degrees; for $n\equiv1\pmod4$ it has inner pair $3^{(n-1)/2}$ and outer pair $1$, except that the outer pair is $3$ at $n=9$; its total is $G_n$.  Lundqvist and Nicklasson prove a lower bound for the generic Hilbert series in every odd degree, disproving the Moreno-Soc\'ias--Snellman conjecture above degree three, and establish equality only in special cases, leaving the minimal series open in general and in degree three in particular~\cite{LN19}; the displayed profile is therefore the expected one and not a cited theorem, and the exceptional outer pair at $n=9$ is the four-dimensional kernel computed there.  The \emph{complete flux} is $H_\Delta=\sum_{i<j<k}e_i\wedge e_j\wedge e_k$, and the target totals are $F_n=\binom{n+1}{(n+1)/2}$ for odd $n$, $F_n=2\binom{n}{n/2}$ for even $n$, and $P_n=2^{n-1}+2^{(n-1)/2}$ for odd $n$, $P_n=2(2^{n-2}+2^{(n-2)/2})$ for even $n$.  Write $t_{p,k}(n)$ for the number of $p$-primary cyclic summands of the integral cohomology of $H_\Delta$ in degree $k$, and $T_p(n)=\sum_kt_{p,k}(n)$ for their total.  On the geometric side, $X^n_{\mathbf d}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{n+r}$ is the Calabi--Yau complete intersection of multidegree $\mathbf d=(d_1,\ldots,d_r)$, $d_i\ge2$, $\sum_id_i=n+r+1$, with degree $D(\mathbf d)=\prod_id_i$; merging replaces $d_i,d_j$ by $d_i+d_j-1$, preserving dimension and the Calabi--Yau condition, exactly as in Part~VII.  Writing $\chi_y=\sum_p\chi^py^p$ for the Hirzebruch genus~\cite{Hir66}, put $a_p(\mathbf d)=(-1)^{n-p}\chi^p(X^n_{\mathbf d})$ and $A_{\mathbf d}(y)=\sum_pa_p(\mathbf d)y^p$, a palindromic integer polynomial with nonnegative coefficients throughout the tested range; for even $n$ put $S(\mathbf d)=(-1)^{n/2}\sigma(X^n_{\mathbf d})$.  Let $Q=(2,\ldots,2)$ be the all-quadric and $Y=(n+2)$ the hypersurface configuration.

\begin{proposition}[Coefficient and parity calibrations]\label{aa:uct}
For every $H\in\Lambda^3\Z^n$ and every prime $p$, $B(H;\mathbb F_p)=B(H;\mathbb Q)+2t_p(H)$.  If every coefficient of $H$ is odd, then the complexes of $H$ and of $H_\Delta$ over $\mathbf F_2$ coincide, so $B(H;\mathbb F_2)=B(H_\Delta;\mathbb F_2)$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Integral flux complexes on tori}

\begin{conjecture}[Dense discrete flux saturation]\label{aa1}
Fix a finite symmetric set $V\subset\Z\setminus\{0\}$, for example $\{\pm1\}$, and let every coefficient of $H_n\in\Lambda^3\Z^n$ be independent and uniform on $V$.  Then $\Pr[(b_k(H_n;\mathbb Q))_k=g_n]\to1$, and the failure probability decays exponentially in $n$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Sparse-flux threshold at the coverage scale]\label{aa2}
Include each coordinate triple independently with probability $p_n$ and give included triples independent coefficients from a fixed finite symmetric set of nonzero integers.  If $p_nn^2/\log n\to\infty$ then $\Pr[(b_k;\mathbb Q)_k=g_n]\to1$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Coverage floor for the sparse threshold]\label{aa:coverage}
If $p_n=(c+o(1))\log n/n^2$ with a fixed $c<2$, then the saturation probability of Conjecture~\ref{aa2} tends to zero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Linear-complexity sparse witnesses]\label{aa3}
Let $\mu_n$ be the minimum support size of a $\{\pm1\}$-valued three-form on $\Z^n$ with rational profile $g_n$.  Then for all sufficiently large $n$ a saturating support of at most $n+C$ triples can be chosen to form a linear $3$-uniform hypergraph, for an absolute constant $C$; in particular $\mu_n=\Theta(n)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Coefficient-law universality in the sparse window]\label{aa4}
Fix $c>0$ and let the support be sampled at $p=c\log n/n^2$ as in Conjecture~\ref{aa2}.  For every finite symmetric coefficient set $V\subset\Z\setminus\{0\}$, the limiting saturation probability and the limiting joint law of the excess vector $(b_k(H;\mathbb Q)-g_{n,k})_k$ exist and depend only on $c$, not on $V$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Support obstructions and coefficient rescaling]\label{aa:support}
Let $H\in\Lambda^3\Z^n$ have support $S$, let $u$ be the number of coordinates lying in no triple of $S$, and let $A$ be the $|S|\times n$ incidence matrix of $S$.
(i) The Betti polynomial of $(E_n(\mathbb Q),d_H)$ is $(1+t)^{u}$ times that of the form read in the used coordinates, and $H$ attains the generic profile only if $g_n=(1+t)^{u}g_{n-u}$; for the deposited profile this forces $u\le1$, with $u=1$ possible only when $n\equiv0\pmod4$.
(ii) Consequently $\mu_n\ge\lceil(n-1)/3\rceil$, and if $p_nn^2/\log n\to0$ then the saturation probability of Conjecture~\ref{aa2} tends to zero.
(iii) If $\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbf F_2}A=|S|$ then the rational profile of $H$ depends only on $S$ and not on the coefficients.  Coefficient dependence therefore requires a nonempty $S'\subseteq S$ covering every coordinate an even number of times, and then $|S'|$ is even, $|S'|\ge4$, and $|V(S')|\le\tfrac32|S'|$.  Since $A$ has $n$ columns the criterion requires $|S|\le n$, so it is vacuous in the window of Conjecture~\ref{aa4}, where $|S|=\Theta(n\log n)$ and the left kernel of $A$ has dimension at least $|S|-n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Finite-field resonance power law]\label{aa5}
Fix $n\ge5$.  There are a positive integer $\delta_n$, a finite Galois extension $K_n/\Q$, and nonnegative integers $\kappa_{n,\sigma}$ indexed by the Frobenius conjugacy classes $\sigma$ of $K_n/\Q$, positive for at least one class, such that, for $H$ uniform on $\Lambda^3\mathbb F_p^n$, the probability of the resonance event $R_{n,p}$, that the profile $(b_k(H;\mathbb F_p))_k$ exceeds $g_n$ in some degree, satisfies
\[
\Pr(R_{n,p})=\kappa_{n,\mathrm{Frob}_p}\,p^{-\delta_n}+O_n\bigl(p^{-\delta_n-1/2}\bigr)
\qquad\text{as }p\to\infty.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional Lang--Weil resonance law]\label{aa:langweil}
Fix $n\ge5$ and suppose that the displayed $g_n$ is the characteristic-zero profile on the Zariski-open locus of maximal wedge ranks.  Then the conclusion of Conjecture~\ref{aa5} holds for every sufficiently large prime of good reduction.  More precisely, $\delta_n$ is the codimension of the largest geometric component of the resonance scheme, and $\kappa_{n,\mathrm{Frob}_p}$ is the number of its largest geometric components fixed by Frobenius.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Central-binomial law for the complete flux, resolved true]\label{aa6}
For every $n\ge4$, $B(H_\Delta;\mathbb Q)=F_n$ and $B(H_\Delta;\mathbb F_2)=P_n$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is true: Proposition~\ref{aa:flux} proves both formulas, reducing wedge multiplication by $H_\Delta=\alpha\wedge\omega$ to Boolean inclusion matrices, whose rational ranks are full and whose characteristic-two complex is exact.  The rational total is the binomial transform $\sum_s\binom gs2^{s}\binom{g-s}{\lfloor(g-s)/2\rfloor}=\binom{2g+1}g$ and the characteristic-two total is $4^{g}+2^{g}$, $g=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor$; the symmetric-group route anticipated at deposit is replaced by a symplectic normal form.

\begin{conjecture}[Prime torsion staircase for the complete flux, resolved false]\label{aa7}
For every prime $p$, the integral cohomology of $(E_n(\Z),d_{H_\Delta})$ has no $p$-primary torsion for $n\le4p-2$ and exactly one $p$-primary cyclic summand, isomorphic to $\Z/p$, at $n=4p-1$.  Moreover, for every $n$ the $2$-primary subgroup is elementary abelian of rank $t_2(H_\Delta)=(P_n-F_n)/2$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The staircase clause is true and the exponent clause is false, both by Proposition~\ref{aa:flux}: the onset at $n=4p-1$ with a single $\Z/p$ holds for every prime, while at $n=15$ the singleton-free sector carries the inclusion matrix $U_{7,3}$, whose Smith normal form $1^{20},2^{9},6^{5},12$ contributes a summand $\Z/4$ in degree nine.  This is exactly the divisor exceeding two that the statement named as its own refutation, and $n=15$ is the least dimension at which it can occur, so the exhaustive range through $n=13$ could not have seen it; the surviving question is the exact $2$-adic invariant factors, whose first departure from exponent one is now located.

\begin{proposition}[Complete-flux normal form]\label{aa:flux}
Write $\alpha=e_1+\cdots+e_n$ and $\omega=\sum_{2\le i<j\le n}e_i\wedge e_j$, and put $g=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor$.  Then $H_\Delta=\alpha\wedge\omega$, and the integral cohomology of $(E_n(\Z),d_{H_\Delta})$ satisfies:
(i) $B(H_\Delta;\mathbb Q)=F_n$ and $B(H_\Delta;\mathbb F_2)=P_n$;
(ii) for every prime $p$ there is no $p$-primary torsion for $n\le4p-2$, and exactly one $p$-primary cyclic summand, isomorphic to $\Z/p$, at $n=4p-1$;
(iii) whenever $p$-primary torsion is present, the positive support of $k\mapsto t_{p,k}(n)$ is the full interval $2p+1\le k\le n-2p+2$;
(iv) $T_p(n+1)>T_p(n)$ for every $n\ge4p-1$;
(v) at $n=15$ the $2$-primary subgroup is not elementary abelian: it contains a summand $\Z/4$ in degree $9$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Comparing coefficients, $\alpha\wedge\omega$ produces each triple meeting $\{1\}$ once, and each triple $\{a<b<c\}\subset\{2,\ldots,n\}$ three times, from $e_a\wedge(e_b\wedge e_c)$, $e_b\wedge(e_a\wedge e_c)$ and $e_c\wedge(e_a\wedge e_b)$ with signs $+,-,+$; so $H_\Delta=\alpha\wedge\omega$.  The substitution $(e_1,\ldots,e_n)\mapsto(\alpha,e_2,\ldots,e_n)$ is unimodular, so with $W=\langle e_2,\ldots,e_n\rangle$ we may write $E_n(\Z)=\Lambda^{*}W\oplus\alpha\wedge\Lambda^{*}W$.  Since $\alpha\wedge\alpha=0$ the differential annihilates $\alpha\wedge\Lambda^{*}W$ and carries $x\in\Lambda^{*}W$ to $\alpha\wedge Lx$, where $L=\omega\wedge-$ acts on $\Lambda^{*}W$; as $\alpha\wedge-$ is injective on $\Lambda^{*}W$, the cohomology is $\ker L\oplus\alpha\wedge\operatorname{coker}L$, so it is $\operatorname{coker}L$ that carries every torsion summand.

The alternating form $\omega$ has all elementary divisors equal to $1$, so an integral change of basis of $W$ writes it as $x_1\wedge y_1+\cdots+x_g\wedge y_g$, together with one null basis vector when $n$ is even.  Split $\Lambda^{*}W$ according to the state of each symplectic pair, empty, $x_i$, $y_i$, or $x_iy_i$.  Then $L$ annihilates every pair held in a singleton state, and on the remaining $r=g-s$ pairs it sends the set of doubly occupied pairs to the sum of its one-element extensions; that is, on a sector with $s$ singletons it is the $0$--$1$ inclusion matrix $U_{r,j}$ from $j$-subsets to $(j+1)$-subsets of an $r$-set.  There are $\binom gs2^{s}$ such sectors, doubled by the null vector when $n$ is even, and a monomial with $s$ singletons and $j+1$ doubly occupied pairs sits in cohomological degree $s+2j+3$.

Wilson's diagonal form for $t$-subset versus $k$-subset inclusion matrices~\cite{Wil90}, applied to $U_{r,j}$ when $2j+1\le r$ and to its transpose otherwise, gives diagonal entries $h-i$ with multiplicity $\binom ri-\binom r{i-1}$ for $0\le i<h$, where $h=\min(j+1,r-j)$.  These number $\binom r{h-1}=\min\bigl(\binom rj,\binom r{j+1}\bigr)$, so over $\mathbb Q$ every $U_{r,j}$ has full possible rank, a sector of $r$ active pairs contributes $2\binom r{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}$ to the total cohomology, and summing over sectors gives $\sum_s\binom gs2^{s}\cdot2\binom{g-s}{\lfloor(g-s)/2\rfloor}=2\binom{2g+1}g$, which is $F_n$ for odd $n$ and half of it for even $n$ before the null vector doubles it back.  Over $\mathbf F_2$ the operator is multiplication by $u_1+\cdots+u_r$ in $\mathbf F_2[u_1,\ldots,u_r]/(u_i^2)$, hence conjugate under a linear change of variables to multiplication by $u_1$ and therefore exact, so its ranks sum to $2^{r-1}$ and such a sector contributes $2^{r}$, or $2$ when $r=0$; summing gives $4^{g}+2^{g}$, again doubled by the null vector in even dimension, which is $P_n$.  This is~(i).

A diagonal entry divisible by $p$ forces $h\ge p$ and hence $r\ge2p-1$; since $r\le g$, no $p$-torsion occurs for $n\le4p-2$.  At $n=4p-1$ we have $g=2p-1$, the only qualifying sector is $s=0$, $r=2p-1$, $j=p-1$, and its diagonal contains the entry $p$ exactly once, giving a single summand $\Z/p$; this is~(ii).  In a sector with $r$ active pairs, $p$ divides a diagonal entry of $U_{r,j}$ exactly when $p-1\le j\le r-p$, so that sector realises the degrees $s+2j+3$ for $p-1\le j\le g-s-p$, together with their shifts by one when $n$ is even.  The sectors $s=0$ and $s=1$ already supply the two parities, running over $2p+1,2p+3,\ldots,2g-2p+3$ and $2p+2,2p+4,\ldots,2g-2p+2$ respectively, and the intervals for larger $s$ are nested inside these; the union is therefore the single interval $2p+1\le k\le n-2p+2$.  This is~(iii).

For~(iv) let $A_r$ count the $p$-primary summands contributed by one pure $r$-pair sector and put $T_g=\sum_r\binom gr2^{g-r}A_r$, so that $T_p(2g+1)=T_g$ while the null vector gives $T_p(2g+2)=2T_g$.  Pascal's rule yields $T_{g+1}-2T_g=\sum_{r\ge1}\binom g{r-1}2^{g+1-r}A_r$, which is positive as soon as some $A_r$ is, that is, after onset.

Finally let $n=15$, so $g=7$.  The sector $s=0$, $r=7$, $j=3$ has $h=4$, so Wilson's diagonal carries the entry $4$: the Smith normal form of the $35\times35$ matrix $U_{7,3}$ is $1^{20},2^{9},6^{5},12$, its cokernel is $(\Z/2)^{14}\oplus(\Z/3)^{6}\oplus\Z/4$, and the corresponding degree is $s+2j+3=9$.  This is~(v).
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Parity-locked torsion count for sign flux]\label{aa8}
For dense $\{\pm1\}$ flux as in Conjecture~\ref{aa1}, conditional on the rational profile equalling $g_n$, the number of $2$-primary cyclic summands is exactly $t_2(H_n)=(P_n-G_n)/2$; unconditionally, this count holds with probability tending to one.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Protected profiles of complete intersections}

\begin{conjecture}[Coefficientwise endpoint extremality]\label{aa9}
For $n\ge3$ and every interior index $1\le p\le n-1$, $a_p(Q)\le a_p(\mathbf d)\le a_p(Y)$, and equality across all interior indices holds only for the endpoint configuration itself.  Dimension $2$ is the equality calibration: every configuration has the K3 profile $(2,20,2)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Degree-normalized merge monotonicity]\label{aa10}
If $\mathbf d'$ is obtained from $\mathbf d$ by merging two entries, then $a_p(\mathbf d')/D(\mathbf d')>a_p(\mathbf d)/D(\mathbf d)$ for every $1\le p\le n-1$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Maximal-degree raw monotonicity]\label{aa11}
For $n\ge3$, if a merge uses an entry equal to $\max_id_i$, then $a_p(\mathbf d')>a_p(\mathbf d)$ for every $1\le p\le n-1$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Real-rooted protected polynomial]\label{aa12}
For every configuration, the polynomial $A_{\mathbf d}(y)$, after removing in odd complex dimension the single factor $y$ forced by $a_0=a_n=0$, has only simple, strictly negative real zeros.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Strict ultra-log-concavity]\label{aa13}
For every configuration and every interior triple of positive coefficients,
\[
\bigl(a_p/\tbinom np\bigr)^2>\bigl(a_{p-1}/\tbinom n{p-1}\bigr)\bigl(a_{p+1}/\tbinom n{p+1}\bigr).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Degree-normalized Mahler growth]\label{aa14}
For every merge, $M(A_{\mathbf d'})/D(\mathbf d')>M(A_{\mathbf d})/D(\mathbf d)$, where $M$ is the multiplicative Mahler measure, the modulus of the leading coefficient times $\prod_{\rho}\max(1,|\rho|)$ over the zeros $\rho$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Spectral-radius growth with low-dimensional rigidity]\label{aa15}
Let $\rho(\mathbf d)$ be the largest modulus of a zero of the reduced polynomial of Conjecture~\ref{aa12}, the polynomial $A_{\mathbf d}$ with its forced factor removed.  If a merge uses a maximal entry, then $\rho(\mathbf d')\ge\rho(\mathbf d)$, with equality for every such merge in complex dimensions $n=2,3,5$ and strict inequality in every other dimension $n\ge4$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Signature laws for complete intersections}

\begin{conjecture}[Signature endpoint extremality]\label{aa16}
In every even complex dimension $n\ge4$, $S(Q)\le S(\mathbf d)\le S(Y)$, with equality only at the corresponding endpoint.  Dimension $2$ is the calibration: every configuration is a K3 surface with $S=16$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Degree-normalized signature monotonicity]\label{aa17}
Every merge in even complex dimension satisfies $S(\mathbf d')/D(\mathbf d')>S(\mathbf d)/D(\mathbf d)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Maximal-degree signature monotonicity]\label{aa18}
In even complex dimension $n\ge4$, a merge using a maximal entry strictly increases $S$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[The quadric ratio law with limit $27$, resolved true]\label{aa19}
For even $n$ let $\Sigma_n=S(Q)$ for the all-quadric configuration of $n+1$ quadric equations in complex dimension $n$.  Then $R_n=\Sigma_{n+2}/\Sigma_n$ is strictly increasing in $n$, $R_n<27$, and
\[
R_n\to27,\qquad 27-R_n\sim\frac{27}{n}.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{aa:quad} below, the quadric-ladder ratio law with limit $27$.

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of the quadric ratio law]\label{aa:quad}
Conjecture~\ref{aa19} holds in full.  With $a_m=\Sigma_{2m}$ and $a_0=2$, the generating function $A(w)=\sum_{m\ge0}a_mw^m$ is algebraic,
\[
A(w)=\frac{2}{(1+y)(1-3y)},\qquad 4w=y(1-y)^2,\quad y(0)=0,
\]
and the coefficients satisfy $D_ma_{m+2}=(M_m-D_m)a_{m+1}+M_ma_m$ with $D_m=(m+2)(2m+3)(14m+9)$ and $M_m=6(3m+2)(3m+4)(14m+23)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For the all-quadric configuration in complex dimension $n=2m$ there are $2m+1$ defining quadrics, so the ambient dimension is $N=4m+1$, and the classical signature formula $\sigma=[x^{N}]\,(x/\tanh x)^{N+1}\prod_i\tanh(d_ix)$ for complete intersections in $\mathbb{CP}^N$~\cite{Hir66} gives $\sigma=[x^{4m+1}](x/\tanh x)^{4m+2}\tanh(2x)^{2m+1}$.  Setting $f(x)=x\tanh(2x)/\tanh^2x$, an even power series with $f(0)=2$, the expression under the coefficient equals $x^{2m+1}f(x)^{2m+1}$, so $\sigma=[x^{2m}]f(x)^{2m+1}$.  Because $f$ is even, replacing $x^2$ by $-z$ multiplies the coefficient of $x^{2m}$ by $(-1)^m$ and turns $f$ into $\psi(z)=\sqrt z\,\tan(2\sqrt z)/\tan^2\!\sqrt z$, analytic on $|z|<\pi^2/4$; hence $a_m=(-1)^m\sigma=[z^m]\psi(z)^{2m+1}$.  For each small $w>0$ the equation $z=w\psi(z)^2$ has exactly one root $z_0(w)$ near the origin, and the residue form of Lagrange inversion, summing the geometric series $\sum_m(w\psi(z)^2/z)^m$ on a small contour and picking up the simple pole at $z_0$, gives
\[
