\begin{conjecture}[Reversible shortcuts amplify winding skewness]\label{ag1}
For every even $N\ge4$ and affinity $F\ne0$, add to the homogeneous driven $N$-ring a reversible antipodal chord of strength $g$ carrying no winding increment, rescaling all rates to keep the activity fixed.  Then the standardized skewness $S_3(g)$ of the long-time winding current is strictly increasing in $g\ge0$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{The finite-budget frontier of linear diffusions}

\begin{conjecture}[Sequential spectral recruitment]\label{ag2}
For every diagonal $S$ with simple spectrum there are thresholds $0=\Sigma_0<\Sigma_1\le\Sigma_2\le\cdots$ and a choice of maximizer $A(\Sigma)$ of the frontier at every budget whose active-mode set is $\{1,\ldots,m\}$ with $m$ nondecreasing in $\Sigma$: no mode is recruited before every slower mode.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Convex arcs with marginal-gain resets]\label{ag3}
For simple $S$ the frontier $r_*(\Sigma)$ is continuous and piecewise real-analytic; on each maximal interval with constant active set it is convex; the one-sided marginal gain jumps only downward at arc boundaries; and $r_*$ reaches the ceiling $\bar s$ at a finite budget and is constant beyond.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Finite two-mode radius]\label{ag4}
For simple $S$ there exists $\Sigma_1>0$ such that for every $0<\Sigma<\Sigma_1$, every global maximizer of the frontier, up to sign and rotations inside exact degeneracies, is supported on the two slowest modes; $\Sigma_1$ is the first budget at which the two-mode branch collides or loses optimality to a branch containing mode three.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Rank-two oscillation extremizer]\label{ag5}
For every diagonal $S>0$ and budget $\Sigma$,
\[
\sup_{\Sigma(A)\le\Sigma}\Omega(A)=\max_{i<j}\ \Omega_{ij}(\Sigma),
\]
with $\Omega_{ij}$ the pair formula of Proposition~\ref{ag:pair}(i): no coupling of three or more modes generates a larger spectral frequency than the best single rotation block.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Cheapest exceptional point is a pair]\label{ag6}
For simple $S$, the infimum of $\Sigma(A)$ over antisymmetric $A$ making $S+A$ defective equals $\min_{i<j}\Sigma_{ij}^{\mathrm{EP}}$, attained by the single block of Proposition~\ref{ag:pair}(i) on a minimizing pair.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Coherent acceleration of dissipative quantum dynamics}

\begin{conjecture}[Recruitment under commutator accessibility]\label{ag7}
For a primitive detailed-balance dissipator with simple traceless spectrum, every small-budget maximizer of $\operatorname{gap}(-\mathcal D-\mathcal A_H)$ over stationary Hamiltonians of coherent budget $\Sigma_H$ concentrates, as $\Sigma_H\to0$, on the accessible direction maximizing the restricted quotient $\sum_{j>1}|a_{1j}(H)|^2/(s_j-s_1)$ over $\Sigma_H$; and there is a maximizing selection whose active dissipative modes are nested and nondecreasing in the budget, with changes only at spectral collisions or accessibility branch crossings.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Envelope saturation is Lie-algebraic]\label{ag8}
For a primitive detailed-balance Lindbladian, the envelope of Proposition~\ref{ag:env} is attained on a nontrivial budget interval if and only if the dissipative eigenmodes active in the classical optimizer close under commutation into a direct sum of three-dimensional $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-type modules together with spectators, so that the unrestricted skew optimizer is realized by a Hamiltonian commutator.
\end{conjecture}

\section{The geometry of anomalous relaxation}

\begin{conjecture}[Exceptional points collapse robust Mpemba basins]\label{ag9}
Let $\mathcal L(h)$ approach a second-order exceptional point $h_c$ in its slow sector from the diagonalizable side, normalize the slow eigenvectors by $\|r_1\|=1$ and $\langle\ell_1,r_1\rangle=1$ so that $\kappa_{\mathrm{eig}}(h)=\|\ell_1(h)\|$ and the basin $\mathcal B_\eps(h)=\{\rho\in\mathcal O:|\langle\ell_1(h),\rho-\rho_*\rangle|\le\eps\}$ is fixed in scale, and suppose the slow functional vanishes transversally on the fixed smooth orbit $\mathcal O$, uniformly near the exceptional point: the tangential gradient of the unit-normalized slow functional is bounded above and below by positive constants along its zero set on $\mathcal O$, and that zero set is nonempty with hypersurface measure bounded below, uniformly for $h$ near $h_c$.  Then there are constants $0<c<C$ with
\[
c\,\frac{\eps}{\kappa_{\mathrm{eig}}(h)}\ \le\ \mu_\mathcal O(\mathcal B_\eps(h))\ \le\ C\,\frac{\eps}{\kappa_{\mathrm{eig}}(h)}
\]
for all sufficiently small $\eps$ and all $h$ near $h_c$: the measure of preparations that robustly cancel the slow mode collapses in exact inverse proportion to the biorthogonal condition number.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Inverse problems and design laws for multivariate extremes}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 19 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The thirteen conjectures below concern a single organizing question of extreme-value theory: how much of the dependence structure of rare events can be recovered from partial, thinned, or redesigned observation, and at what cost in stability.  They form five programmes.  A geometric programme (Conjectures~\ref{ah1} to~\ref{ah4}) studies the extremal coefficient $\theta_m(\Gamma)$ of the H\"usler--Reiss family~\cite{HR89,EH20} as a functional on variogram matrices: an isoperimetric recruitment staircase at fixed total variogram energy with a proved symmetry-breaking boundary, the regular simplex maximal for three variables and at weak dependence but strictly beaten below it by configurations carrying fewer active components, a pairwise closure law at weak dependence sharpening the consistency inequalities of Schlather and Tawn~\cite{ST02} into an asymptotic equivalence, a scale-elasticity bound of one half paired with entrywise monotonicity whose conjunction yields a dimension-free sparsification law for resistance variograms, strengthening the variogram-level guarantees of~\cite{EAR26} to the extremal coefficient itself, and a positive-curvature law at complete dependence whose convex-body shadow is the Wills functional~\cite{Vit96}.  A cluster-tomography programme (Conjectures~\ref{ah5} and~\ref{ah6}) turns the missing-data results for subsampled sequences~\cite{RT00,MF04,HS08} into an inverse theory: the Bernoulli-thinning response curve should identify the entire limiting cluster-size law through its generating function, and the two-parameter systematic-plus-Bernoulli response should identify the cluster support exactly up to the equivalence generated by its modular occupancy data.  A topological programme (Conjectures~\ref{ah7} and~\ref{ah8}) makes the topology of extreme directions an inferential target: a bottleneck-consistency law for persistent homology of intermediate-order angular samples at the rate $b(U(n/k))+(\log k/k)^{1/s}$, composing the diagram-estimation rates of~\cite{CGLM15} with a finite-threshold angular bias, and a leakage trichotomy locating exactly when hidden regular variation~\cite{Res08,MR11} is topologically visible after support filtering.  A tail-tomography programme (Conjectures~\ref{ah9} and~\ref{ah10}) quantifies the inverse problem of angular reconstruction from one-sided projection tails: a severe stability barrier, no H\"older inverse on measures and only a logarithmic modulus on H\"older classes, the analytic-kernel phenomenon of moment problems~\cite{Tal87} instantiated for the fractional transforms whose injectivity dichotomy is recorded in Proposition~\ref{ah:moment} following~\cite{MN21,CT19}, and a parameter-count identifiability threshold for $s$-atomic angular measures at exactly $m=sd-1$ generic fractional projections.  A design programme (Conjectures~\ref{ah11} to~\ref{ah13}) redesigns block maxima themselves: Poissonized block sizes should remove the universal $V^2/(2n)$ finite-block correction and the corresponding $n^{-1}$ estimator bias documented in the block-maxima second-order theory~\cite{BVZ19,DF19,BZ21}, factorial-cumulant matching should extend the removal to a full ladder of orders, and exact second-order cancellation in a Hall family~\cite{Hall82} should create a cusp at which threshold adaptation, whose logarithmic penalty away from the cusp is the theorem of Carpentier and Kim~\cite{CK15}, cannot be uniformly oracle-optimal, with critical window $|c|\asymp n^{-\beta/(\alpha+4\beta)}$.  The proved layer is four calibrations.  Proposition~\ref{ah:hr} gives the exact Gaussian-orthant formula for $\theta_m$ used throughout, proves the elasticity bound in the pairwise case, and proves that every collinear configuration has vanishing second curvature, the easy direction of Conjecture~\ref{ah4}.  Proposition~\ref{ah:thin} proves the cluster-level thinning algebra: the Bernoulli response formula, the systematic random-phase formula, the identifiability of the occupancy multisets from the joint response, the recovery of the entire difference multiset from occupancy data by M\"obius inversion, and the closure of occupancy equivalence under dilation.  Proposition~\ref{ah:moment} proves the integer--fractional injectivity dichotomy for the projection transforms, with an explicit two-atom versus three-atom pair invisible at $\alpha=2$ and separated at $\alpha=3/2$.  Proposition~\ref{ah:block} proves the exact Poissonization identity for Pareto seeds and constructs, for every order $r$, finitely supported non-Poisson integer block-size laws whose factorial cumulants match the Poisson law through order $r$, with the exact probability-generating-function identity showing that the finite-block error then falls to order $n^{-r}$.  Five negative findings are retained as controls, and they shape the part.  The deposited isoperimetry claim, that the regular simplex maximizes $\theta_m$ at fixed energy for every $m$, is refuted for $m\ge4$: the width identity of Proposition~\ref{ah:hr}(iv) makes configurations with fewer active components strictly better away from weak dependence, with exact orthant-probability instances at $m=4$, scale $0.12$, and $m=5$, scale $0.35$, where the four-active configuration beats the simplex by $3.5\times10^{-3}$ and is found by unrestricted optimization, so Conjecture~\ref{ah1} keeps the simplex law at three variables and states the recruitment staircase above.  A proposed modular reconstruction claim, that the occupancy multisets over all moduli determine a finite cluster support up to translation and reflection, is refuted: the six-mark pair $\{0,1,4,10,12,17\}$ and $\{0,1,8,11,13,17\}$ of Bloom~\cite{Blo77}, the classical counterexample to Piccard's theorem on difference multisets~\cite{Pat44,BG07}, shares every occupancy multiset, by finite verification below the diameter and trivially above it, so occupancy tomography can never distinguish this pair; the repaired Conjecture~\ref{ah6} asks instead for determination within size five and for the two-member bound on equivalence classes, over the exhaustive verification that all ten occupancy collisions among the $6437$ canonical sets of size at most six through diameter eighteen and the $3312$ of size at most seven through diameter fourteen are homometric pairs, while the pair $\{0,1,3,8,10,14\}$ versus $\{0,1,7,9,11,14\}$ shows the occupancy data strictly refines the difference multiset.  The subsampling formulas themselves, the Bernoulli and systematic extremal-index responses at the cluster level, are known in substance for subsampled stationary sequences~\cite{RT00,MF04,HS08} and are therefore recorded as calibration, not conjecture; what is claimed as new in Conjectures~\ref{ah5} and~\ref{ah6} is the uniform process-level transport and the inverse-theory content.  A multiplicity observed at the identifiability threshold of Conjecture~\ref{ah10} on one random instance was exposed as an artifact of a near-coincident atom pair, ill-conditioning inflating rounding-level copies of the single true solution, and the atom-separation hypothesis of the conjecture records that boundary.  A second multiplicity is genuine and refutes global uniqueness at the counting boundary itself: at three atoms in three dimensions, two of six well-separated random instances admit a second exact interior mixture at $m=sd-1$, verified to forty-digit residuals, and both become unique one measurement higher, so the global clause of Conjecture~\ref{ah10} begins at $m=sd$ and the counting-boundary refutation is recorded in place.  Conjectures~\ref{ah1}, \ref{ah2}, \ref{ah3}, \ref{ah7}, \ref{ah8}, and~\ref{ah11} to~\ref{ah13} assert laws for every dimension or every sample size; Conjecture~\ref{ah3} is closely related to the sparsification guarantees of~\cite{EAR26}, and Conjecture~\ref{ah13} carries one against the adaptation lower bounds of~\cite{CK15}.  Since deposit, Conjecture~\ref{ah4} has been resolved true by Proposition~\ref{ah:curv}.

A variogram matrix $\Gamma$ on $m$ sites is symmetric with zero diagonal and conditionally negative definite, so that $\Gamma_{ij}=\lVert x_i-x_j\rVert^2$ for centered points $x_1,\ldots,x_m$ in some $\R^k$; the H\"usler--Reiss max-stable vector with variogram $\Gamma$~\cite{HR89} has extremal coefficient $\theta_m(\Gamma)\in[1,m]$, the exponent function at $\mathbf 1$, with spectral representation $\theta_m(\Gamma)=\E\max_i\exp(\langle Z,x_i\rangle-\lVert x_i\rVert^2/2)$ for a standard Gaussian $Z$, pairwise coefficients $\theta_{ij}=2\Phi(\sqrt{\Gamma_{ij}}/2)$, energy $\mathcal S(\Gamma)=\sum_{i<j}\Gamma_{ij}$, and scale elasticity $\eta(t)=\dd\log\theta_m(t\Gamma)/\dd\log t$.  For a stationary regularly varying sequence whose exceedances converge to a compound-Poisson cluster process~\cite{BS09} with cluster size $N$, $\E N<\infty$, the extremal index is $\theta=1/\E N$~\cite{Lea83,OB87}, and when the limiting cluster has a finite support $S\subset\Z$, defined up to translation, the occupancy counts are $n_{m,r}(S)=|\{s\in S:s\equiv r\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m)\}|$, the occupancy data of $S$ is the family of unordered multisets $\mathcal N_m(S)=\{n_{m,0}(S),\ldots,n_{m,m-1}(S)\}$ over all $m\ge2$, two sets are occupancy equivalent when their occupancy data agree, and homometric when their difference multisets agree.  A $d$-variate regularly varying vector has angular measure $H$ on the simplex $\Delta_{d-1}$; the fractional projection transform is $(T_\alpha H)(a)=\int(a\cdot w)^\alpha\,H(\dd w)$ for directions $a\in(0,\infty)^d$; and for block maxima, $F$ lies in a max-domain with exponent function $V$ and second-order rate $A(n)$, a block of size $n$ has exceedance probability $q_n(x)=\bar F(a_nx+b_n)$, and a randomized block size $N_n$ has factorial cumulants $\kappa_r^{(F)}$ defined by $\log\E(1+u)^{N_n}=\sum_{r\ge1}\kappa_r^{(F)}u^r/r!$, so that $\log\E(1-q)^{N_n}=\sum_{r\ge1}\kappa_r^{(F)}(-q)^r/r!$ exactly.

\begin{proposition}[Tilting calibration for the dependence programme]\label{ah:hr}
(i) For every admissible $\Gamma$ realized by centered points $x_1,\ldots,x_m$,
\[
\theta_m(\Gamma)=\sum_{i=1}^m\Phi_{m-1}\Bigl(\bigl(\tfrac{\Gamma_{ij}}2\bigr)_{j\ne i};\,\bigl(\tfrac{\Gamma_{ij}+\Gamma_{ik}-\Gamma_{jk}}2\bigr)_{j,k\ne i}\Bigr),
\]
where $\Phi_{m-1}(\,\cdot\;C)$ is the centered Gaussian orthant probability with covariance $C$.  (ii) For $m=2$ the elasticity satisfies $0\le\eta(t)\le\sup_{x>0}x\varphi(x)/(2\Phi(x))=0.1472\ldots<1/2$ for all $t>0$.  (iii) If the points $x_i$ are collinear, then with $M=\max_i\langle Z,x_i\rangle$ and $I$ the almost-surely unique argmax, $\E[M^2-\lVert x_I\rVert^2]=0$: every collinear configuration has vanishing second curvature.  (iv) For $3\le k\le m$ let the $k$-active configuration place $k$ points at the vertices of a centered regular simplex and $m-k$ points at the origin, with energy $S$.  Its Gaussian width is
\[
W_k(m,S)=\E\max_i\langle Z,x_i\rangle=\sqrt{\tfrac Sm}\,\frac{e_k}{\sqrt{k-1}},
\]
where $e_k$ is the expected maximum of $k$ independent standard Gaussians; and $e_3/\sqrt2=0.59841\ldots$ exceeds $e_4/\sqrt3=0.59431\ldots$ and $e_5/2=0.58148\ldots$, so for $m\in\{4,5\}$ the triangle configuration $k=3$ has strictly larger width than the full simplex $k=m$, and hence strictly larger $\theta_m$ at fixed energy for all sufficiently small scale.
\end{proposition}

\section{The geometry of H\"usler--Reiss dependence}

\begin{conjecture}[Isoperimetry and symmetry breaking at fixed energy]\label{ah1}
(a) For $m=3$ and every $S>0$, among all admissible variograms with energy $\mathcal S(\Gamma)=S$, the equidistant variogram $\Gamma^{\mathrm{eq}}$ with all off-diagonal entries $S/\binom m2$, realized by a regular triangle, uniquely maximizes $\theta_3(\Gamma)$, up to relabeling.  (b) For every $m\ge4$ and every $S$, the maximum of $\theta_m$ at energy $S$ is attained by a $k$-active configuration of Proposition~\ref{ah:hr}(iv), $k$ components at the vertices of a centered regular simplex and $m-k$ coincident at the centroid, for some $3\le k\le m$, uniquely up to relabeling; the maximizing $k$ is nondecreasing in $S$, equals $3$ for all sufficiently small $S$, equals $m$ for all sufficiently large $S$, and passes through every intermediate value, so the fixed-energy optimizer recruits one component at a time through a staircase of symmetry-breaking transitions.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Pairwise closure at weak dependence]\label{ah2}
Fix an admissible $\Gamma$ with strictly positive off-diagonal entries such that no three-or-higher-way Gaussian large-deviation saddle shares the exact rate of a contributing pairwise saddle.  Then, with $\delta_{ij}(t)=2-\theta_{ij}(t\Gamma)=2[1-\Phi(\sqrt{t\Gamma_{ij}}/2)]$,
\[
m-\theta_m(t\Gamma)\ \sim\ \sum_{i<j}\delta_{ij}(t),\qquad t\to\infty.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Scale elasticity and monotone dependence]\label{ah3}
For every admissible nonzero $\Gamma$: (a) $0\le\eta(t)\le\tfrac12$ for all $t>0$; (b) if $\Gamma'$ is admissible and $\Gamma_{ij}\le\Gamma'_{ij}$ entrywise, then $\theta_m(\Gamma)\le\theta_m(\Gamma')$.  Consequently, if $L,\widetilde L$ are connected graph Laplacians with $(1-\eps)L\preceq\widetilde L\preceq(1+\eps)L$ on $\mathbf 1^\perp$ and $\Gamma(L)$ is the resistance variogram $\Gamma_{ij}(L)=(e_i-e_j)^\top L^\dagger(e_i-e_j)$, then
\[
(1+\eps)^{-1/2}\ \le\ \frac{\theta_m(\Gamma(\widetilde L))}{\theta_m(\Gamma(L))}\ \le\ (1-\eps)^{-1/2}.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Positive curvature at complete dependence, resolved true]\label{ah4}
For centered points $x_1,\ldots,x_m$ realizing $\Gamma$, with $M=\max_i\langle Z,x_i\rangle$ and $I$ the argmax, as $t\downarrow0$,
\[
\theta_m(t\Gamma)=1+\sqrt t\,\E M+t\,q(\Gamma)+o(t),\qquad q(\Gamma)=\tfrac12\,\E\bigl[M^2-\lVert x_I\rVert^2\bigr],
\]
and $q(\Gamma)\ge0$ always, with $q(\Gamma)=0$ if and only if, after deleting non-exposed points, the convex hull of the $x_i$ is a point or a segment.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} True, by Proposition~\ref{ah:curv} below: the expansion holds together with the nonnegativity of $q(\Gamma)$ and the equality classification, the positivity following from a Gaussian integration-by-parts identity.

\section{Cluster inverse problems under thinning}

\begin{proposition}[Thinning calibration for the cluster programme]\label{ah:thin}
Let the limiting cluster have size $N$ with generating function $G_N$ and, where stated, finite support $S\subset\Z$.  At the level of the limiting compound-Poisson cluster process:
(i) independent retention with probability $p$ gives the response $\theta(p)=\theta\,(1-G_N(1-p))/p$;
(ii) retention of one residue class modulo $m$ with uniform random phase gives $\theta^{\mathrm{sys}}_m=\theta\,\E R_m(S)$, where $R_m(S)$ is the number of occupied residues;
(iii) the joint response $Q_{m,S}(p)=\tfrac1m\sum_{r=0}^{m-1}[1-(1-p)^{n_{m,r}(S)}]$, known for $p$ in any infinite subset of $(0,1)$, determines the occupancy multiset $\mathcal N_m(S)$;
(iv) the occupancy data $\{\mathcal N_m(S):m\ge2\}$ determines the difference multiset of $S$;
(v) occupancy equivalence is closed under dilation: $\mathcal N_m(tS)=\mathcal N_{m/\gcd(t,m)}(S)$ for every $t\ge1$, so $S\sim S'$ implies $tS\sim tS'$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Resolution of the complete-dependence curvature law]\label{ah:curv}
Conjecture~\ref{ah4} holds in full: the displayed expansion is valid, $q(\Gamma)\ge0$ for every admissible $\Gamma$, and $q(\Gamma)=0$ if and only if the convex hull of the exposed points is a point or a segment.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $\eps=\sqrt t$, $G_i=\langle Z,x_i\rangle$, $M=\max_iG_i$ with almost-surely unique argmax $I$ after deleting non-exposed and duplicate points, and $V_\eps=\max_i(\eps G_i-\tfrac{\eps^2}2\lVert x_i\rVert^2)$, so $\theta_m(t\Gamma)=\E e^{V_\eps}$.  From $|V_\eps-\eps M|\le\tfrac{\eps^2}2\max_i\lVert x_i\rVert^2$ and uniform integrability of $e^{\eps M}$ for small $\eps$, dominated expansion gives $\theta_m(t\Gamma)=1+\eps\,\E M+\tfrac{\eps^2}2\E[M^2-\lVert x_I\rVert^2]+o(\eps^2)$, the asserted expansion with $q(\Gamma)=\tfrac12\E[M^2-\lVert x_I\rVert^2]$.  Now $M=h_K(Z)$ is the support function of $K=\operatorname{conv}\{x_i\}$; the $x_i$ are centered, so $0=\tfrac1m\sum_ix_i$ lies in the relative interior of $K$ and $M\ge0$, while $M$ is convex and $1$-homogeneous with $\nabla M=x_I$ almost everywhere.  Gaussian integration by parts and Euler's identity $\langle z,\nabla M\rangle=M$ give
\[
\E[M^2]=\E\langle Z,M\nabla M\rangle=\E\lVert\nabla M\rVert^2+\E[M\,\Delta M],
\]
where $\Delta M\ge0$ is the curvature measure of the convex function $M$; since $M\ge0$ the last term is nonnegative and $\lVert\nabla M\rVert^2=\lVert x_I\rVert^2$ almost everywhere, so $q(\Gamma)\ge0$.  Equality forces $M=0$ on $\operatorname{supp}\Delta M$; if $\dim K\ge2$ then $0$ lies in the relative interior of $K$, so $M>0$ at every nonzero point of the affine span of $K$, on which the nonzero curvature measure $\Delta M$ is supported, and the term is strictly positive.  Hence $q(\Gamma)=0$ iff $\dim K\le1$, and for a point or a segment the curvature measure sits where $M=0$, giving $q=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Bernoulli-thinning spectroscopy of clusters]\label{ah5}
Let $(X_t)$ be stationary and regularly varying, with exceedances converging to a compound-Poisson cluster process with cluster size $N$, $\E N<\infty$, and extremal index $\theta=1/\E N$, under mixing and anti-clustering conditions.  Retain each observation independently with probability $p$ and compress the time axis.  Then the retained sequence satisfies the same hypotheses with extremal index $\theta(p)=\theta\,(1-G_N(1-p))/p$, uniformly for $p$ in compact subsets of $(0,1]$; consequently the response curve $p\mapsto\theta(p)$ identifies the entire limiting cluster-size law.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Occupancy tomography of cluster supports]\label{ah6}
(a) Every finite $S\subset\Z$ with $|S|\le5$ is determined by its occupancy data up to translation and reflection.  (b) For every finite size, each occupancy-equivalence class of finite integer sets contains at most two translation--reflection classes.
\end{conjecture}

\section{The topology of extreme directions}

\begin{conjecture}[Consistency of persistent angular homology]\label{ah7}
Let $X$ be $d$-variate regularly varying whose angular law $H$ is supported on a compact $C^2$ $s$-dimensional manifold $M$ of positive reach in the open simplex, with density bounded above and below.  Let $A_{n,k}$ be the angular components of the $k=k_n$ largest radii, $k\to\infty$, $k/n\to0$, and suppose the conditional angular law beyond radius $t$ couples to $H$ with Hausdorff bias at most $b(t)\to0$.  Then for every homological degree $q$,
\[
d_B\bigl(\operatorname{Dgm}_q(\operatorname{VR}(A_{n,k})),\operatorname{Dgm}_q(\operatorname{VR}(M))\bigr)
=O_p\Bigl(b(U(n/k))+\bigl(\tfrac{\log k}k\bigr)^{1/s}\Bigr),
\]
with $U$ the radial quantile function and $d_B$ the bottleneck distance.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[A leakage trichotomy for hidden extremes]\label{ah8}
Let $X=R_0W_0+R_1W_1$ with independent components: $R_0$ regularly varying with index $\alpha_0$, $R_1$ with index $\alpha_1>\alpha_0$, and angular laws $W_0\sim H_0$, $W_1\sim H_1$ supported on compact sets $M_0$, $M_1$ separated in the simplex, $H_1$ as in Conjecture~\ref{ah7}.  Remove the angular neighborhood $N_{\delta_n}(M_0)$, rerank the surviving radii, and let $L_n$ be the ratio of the primary to the hidden mechanism's contribution to the surviving exceedance intensity at the hidden threshold.  Let $\Lambda_n$ be the expected number of surviving leaked primary exceedances in the reranked top-$k_n$ sample, and suppose the leaked support $M_{\mathrm{leak}}$ is persistence-separated from $M_1$, $d_B(\mathrm{Dgm}_q(M_1\cup M_{\mathrm{leak}}),\mathrm{Dgm}_q(M_1))\ge c_0>0$.  Then: if $\Lambda_n\to0$, the persistence diagrams of the surviving angular sample recover those of $M_1$ at the rate of Conjecture~\ref{ah7}; if $\Lambda_n\to\infty$ they do not; and for $\Lambda_n\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)$ the surviving leaked primaries are asymptotically Poisson-$\lambda$ and the probability of recovery within any fixed bottleneck tolerance below the hidden feature scale converges to a limit $p(\lambda)\in(0,1)$, so feature detection switches on the leaked-count scale $\Lambda_n\asymp1$, that is $L_n\asymp1/k_n$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Tail tomography and its stability}

\begin{proposition}[Moment calibration for the tomography programme]\label{ah:moment}
(i) If $\alpha>0$ is not an integer, then $T_\alpha H$ restricted to any open subset of $(0,\infty)^d$ determines the finite measure $H$ on $\Delta_{d-1}$ uniquely.  (ii) If $\alpha=q\in\mathbb N_0$, then $T_qH$ depends only on the moments of $H$ of degree at most $q$, and injectivity fails; explicitly, for $d=2$, $q=2$, the measures
\[
H_1=\tfrac12\delta_{0.2}+\tfrac12\delta_{0.8},\qquad
H_2=0.28125\,\delta_{0.1}+0.4375\,\delta_{0.5}+0.28125\,\delta_{0.9}
\]
satisfy $T_2H_1=T_2H_2$ identically while $T_{3/2}H_1\ne T_{3/2}H_2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[The stability barrier of fractional tail tomography]\label{ah9}
Fix noninteger $\alpha>0$, $0<\eta<1$, and the direction box $A_\eta=\{a:\eta\le a_i\le\eta^{-1}\}$.  (a) There exist no $C,\beta>0$ with $W_1(H_1,H_2)\le C\,\lVert T_\alpha H_1-T_\alpha H_2\rVert_{L^\infty(A_\eta)}^\beta$ for all probability measures $H_1,H_2$ on $\Delta_{d-1}$.  (b) On the class of angular densities lying in a H\"older ball $\{f:\lVert f\rVert_{C^s}\le L,\ f\ge c>0\}$, the inverse admits a logarithmic modulus of continuity: $W_1(H_1,H_2)\lesssim(\log(1/\eps))^{-\kappa_s}$ with $\eps$ the transform error, for some $\kappa_s>0$ depending on $s$, and no polynomial modulus $\eps^\beta$ is valid on the class.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[A parameter-count threshold for sparse tail tomography]\label{ah10}
Let $H=\sum_{r=1}^s\pi_r\delta_{w_r}$ with distinct atoms in the open simplex $\Delta_{d-1}^\circ$, weights $\pi_r>0$ summing to one, and let $p=sd-1$ be the parameter count.  Fix noninteger $\alpha>0$ and observe $y_j=(T_\alpha H)(a_j)$ at generic positive directions $a_1,\ldots,a_m$.  Then, outside a proper real-analytic exceptional set of atom configurations and designs, and quantitatively away from atom collisions and the simplex boundary: for $m<p$ identifiability fails on a positive-dimensional fiber; for $m=p$ the Jacobian has full rank and the fiber is finite, so the mixture is locally rigid but need not be unique; and for every $m\ge sd$ the measurement vector determines $H$ globally up to permutation of atoms, with local inverse modulus controlled by atom separation and boundary distance.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Randomized block designs}

\begin{proposition}[Design calibration for the block programme]\label{ah:block}
(i) If $\bar F(x)=x^{-\alpha}$ and $N_n\sim\operatorname{Poisson}(n)$, then $\Pr(M_{N_n}/n^{1/\alpha}\le x)=e^{-x^{-\alpha}}$ exactly for every $n$.  (ii) For any block-size law, $\log\E(1-q)^{N_n}=\sum_{r\ge1}\kappa_r^{(F)}(-q)^r/r!$, so matching factorial cumulants of the Poisson law through order $r$ cancels the sampling corrections through order $q^r$.  (iii) For every $r\ge2$ there is a law $\xi$ on a finite subset of $\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$, not Poisson, whose cumulants match those of $\operatorname{Poisson}(1)$ through order $r$; the convolution $N_n=\xi_1+\cdots+\xi_n$ then has $\E N_n=n$, $\operatorname{Var}N_n=n$, $\kappa_j^{(F)}(N_n)=0$ for $2\le j\le r$, $\kappa_{r+1}^{(F)}(N_n)=O(n)$, and for the Pareto seed
\[
\sup_x\bigl|\Pr(M_{N_n}/n^{1/\alpha}\le x)-e^{-x^{-\alpha}}\bigr|=O(n^{-r})
\]
uniformly on compact $x$-sets.  For $r=3$ the law on $\{0,1,3\}$ with weights $(\tfrac13,\tfrac12,\tfrac16)$ and for $r=4$ the law on $\{0,1,2,4\}$ with weights $(\tfrac38,\tfrac13,\tfrac14,\tfrac1{24})$ are such laws.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Poissonized blocks remove the universal bias]\label{ah11}
Let $F$ lie in a max-domain of attraction with a uniform second-order expansion $n\bar F(a_nx+b_n)=V(x)+A(n)B(x)+o(A(n))+o(n^{-1})$ on compact interior sets.  Then Poissonized block maxima satisfy $\log\Pr(M_{N_n}\le a_nx+b_n)=-V(x)-A(n)B(x)+o(A(n))+o(n^{-1})$, the universal $V(x)^2/(2n)$ correction of fixed blocks being absent; and for generalized-extreme-value likelihood estimation from many independent blocks, Poissonization leaves the first-order asymptotic variance unchanged while removing the universal $n^{-1}$ bias contribution, so that when $A(n)=o(n^{-1})$ the estimator bias improves from order $n^{-1}$ to the intrinsic second order.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[The factorial-cumulant design ladder]\label{ah12}
Fix $r\ge2$ and let $N_n$ be an $r$-th ladder law as in Proposition~\ref{ah:block}(iii).  Then in every max-domain with the second-order expansion of Conjecture~\ref{ah11}, block maxima with exposure $N_n$ satisfy the expansion with sampling-mechanism corrections of order $O(n^{-r})$ in place of $V^2/(2n)$; and generalized-extreme-value likelihood estimation from many independent randomized blocks retains the first-order limit law and variance of deterministic blocks while its sampling-mechanism bias falls to $O(n^{-r})+o(A(n))$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[An adaptation cusp at second-order cancellation]\label{ah13}
In a smooth Hall family $\bar F_\lambda(x)=x^{-\alpha}[1+c(\lambda)x^{-\beta}+d(\lambda)x^{-2\beta}+o(x^{-2\beta})]$ with $c(\lambda_0)=0$, $c'(\lambda_0)\ne0$, $d(\lambda_0)\ne0$, the oracle threshold for Hill-type estimation moves from $k\asymp n^{2\beta/(\alpha+2\beta)}$ generically to $k\asymp n^{4\beta/(\alpha+4\beta)}$ at cancellation, the two regimes meeting at the critical window $|c(\lambda)|\asymp n^{-\beta/(\alpha+4\beta)}$; and no data-driven threshold selector is uniformly oracle-rate optimal, with the same constants, over both $|c(\lambda)|\le Cn^{-\beta/(\alpha+4\beta)}$ and $|c(\lambda)|\gg n^{-\beta/(\alpha+4\beta)}$: an adaptation penalty, at least logarithmic or a nontrivial constant, is unavoidable in the cusp.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Extremal, randomized, and inverse laws for persistent topology}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 20 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The twelve conjectures below concern a single organizing question of topological data analysis: which parts of a persistence computation are laws rather than artifacts, sharp constants instead of worst-case bounds, invertible summaries instead of lossy signatures, and calibrated randomness instead of parameter sensitivity.  They form five programmes.  An extremal-and-sampling programme (Conjectures~\ref{ai1} to~\ref{ai3}) concerns degree-one Vietoris--Rips persistence: a circle isoperimetry asserting that among $n$ points on a Euclidean circle the regular polygon uniquely maximizes the multiplicative persistence ratio, the chordal, every-$n$, ratio-level refinement of the geodesic persistence-set extremality of G\'omez and M\'emoli~\cite{GoM24}; a saturation law asserting that the classical factor two of Vietoris--Rips stability~\cite{CSO14} is asymptotically attained by greedy landmarking of smooth convex curves; and a two-sided Johnson--Lindenstrauss threshold~\cite{JL84,LN17} for the simultaneous preservation of the loop ratios of orthogonal hexagonal channels under Gaussian projection, whose upper half is known in substance~\cite{Lot19,MKG24} and whose lower half would be the first matching bound for a topological statistic.  A statistical programme (Conjectures~\ref{ai4} and~\ref{ai5}) makes the geometry of Wasserstein means of diagrams~\cite{MMH11,TMMH14} quantitative: an optional feature of prevalence $q$ appears in the mean at exactly $q$ times its conditional persistence up to exponentially small matching leakage, sharpening the shrinkage phenomenon visible in the probabilistic Fr\'echet means of~\cite{MTB15} and the grouping calculus of~\cite{CaM24}, and empirical means use the intended latent grouping up to $CNm\,e^{-c\gamma^2/\sigma^2}$.  A Mapper programme (Conjectures~\ref{ai6} and~\ref{ai7}) builds on an exact phase calculus proved here: randomizing the phase of a uniform one-dimensional cover~\cite{CO18} makes the survival probability of an isolated Reeb cycle the universal piecewise-linear function $F_\alpha(\ell/h)$ with an explicit kernel, the phase-averaged Betti curve of a chain of cycles is exactly additive with the spans recoverable from its kinks, and the dithered midpoint estimator of a branch critical value is exactly unbiased with the quantization variance $s^2/12$ of~\cite{Sch64}; the conjectures transfer this calculus to general tame Reeb graphs with a stable inversion, and to discretized and sampled Mapper with mesh-order corrections.  A tomography programme (Conjecture~\ref{ai8}) concerns rectangle-decomposable two-parameter modules~\cite{BL23}, for which the fibered barcode over all lines is a complete invariant~\cite{BLO22}: a proposition proved here sharpens completeness to a single generic direction with the line parameterization retained, the deposited three-direction claim being recorded as calibration, and the conjecture asserts the quantitative sequel, that three fixed generic directions give a locally bi-Lipschitz invariant with polynomially controlled constant and that the direction profile of the matching distance is never needle-like, so uniformly random slopes see near-maximal discrepancy; it is closely related to the local metric equivalence of Vipond~\cite{Vip20}, proved with a universal constant over all lines, and the Lipschitz foliation bounds of~\cite{CDFFL13} behind deterministic approximation schemes~\cite{KN20,BHLMS23}.  A finite-summary programme (Conjectures~\ref{ai9} to~\ref{ai12}) asks how small a complete or faithful summary can be: a restricted-isometry law for random Fourier sketches~\cite{RR07} of the persistence-weighted Gaussian kernel~\cite{KFH16}, bi-Lipschitz to the Wasserstein metric on separated bounded-cardinality classes, which the known non-embeddability theorems~\cite{CB19,BW20} do not forbid because they require collisions or unbounded cardinality, with restricted-class embeddings known in spirit~\cite{MV24}; a Whitney-count law~\cite{Whi44} asserting that $4m+1$ generic evaluations of the Gaussian persistence surface, whose continuum injectivity is known~\cite{CHLP22} while grid images are not injective~\cite{AEK17}, form a complete invariant of $m$-point diagrams; a moment law asserting that the first $4m$ moments of the aggregate landscape~\cite{Bub15} determine a generic $m$-bar diagram, a compressed instance of the finite algebraic coordinate systems of~\cite{ACC16,Kal19}; and an average-case kinetic law asserting that random affine motion of $n$ planar points produces only $O(n^2\log n)$ expected changes of the degree-one persistence pairing, far below the quartic worst-case count that vineyard algorithms~\cite{CEM06,DH21,Hic22,GM26} are built to survive.  The proved layer is three calibrations.  Proposition~\ref{ai:map} proves the exact survival law with kernel $F_\alpha(x)=\min(1,\max(0,(x-1)/(1-\alpha)))$, the exact additivity and kink inversion of the phase-averaged Betti curve for chains of isolated cycles, and the exact dither law for branch critical values.  Proposition~\ref{ai:rect} proves that one generic direction of the parameterized fiber field determines a generic rectangle-decomposable module, by tracking the interval pairs of the fiber barcodes through the parallel family and reading each rectangle off the envelope of its track.  Proposition~\ref{ai:sum} proves the exact closed form of the aggregate-landscape moments and the one-bar case of the moment law, a single bar being determined by its first two moments.  Three negative findings are retained as controls, and they shape the part.  The unrestricted planar version of the extremality claim is refuted: a six-point perturbation search found planar configurations whose multiplicative loop persistence exceeds the regular hexagon's by the factor $1.052$, exact to the arithmetic of the boundary-matrix reduction, so the circle hypothesis of Conjecture~\ref{ai1} is essential and is recorded with the refuting instance; this failure of unrestricted extremality appears to be undocumented.  The deposited three-direction tomography claim is true but not sharp, one direction sufficing by Proposition~\ref{ai:rect}, and is recorded as calibration.  A deposited majority-threshold claim for barycenter features, that an optional feature appears in the mean exactly when its prevalence exceeds one half, is refuted by the flat grouping algebra itself, the mean feature shrinking continuously with prevalence instead, and Conjecture~\ref{ai4} carries the corrected law.  The deposited linear stable-inversion clause of Conjecture~\ref{ai6} is refuted at coalescing spans: the two-cycle span multisets $\{\ell,\ell\}$ and $\{\ell-\delta,\ell+\delta\}$ have bottleneck distance $\delta$, yet on the affine middle branch of the survival kernel the symmetric perturbation cancels, so the phase-averaged Betti curves differ only in $O(\delta)$-wide neighborhoods of the moving kinks and $\lVert S_G-S_{G'}\rVert_{L^2(\dd h/h)}=\Theta(\delta^{3/2})$, whence no bound $\delta\le C\eta$ can hold; the repaired clause requires the span values to be pairwise separated, near-collisions admitting only a H\"older inverse of exponent $2/3$, the constant $\eta/\delta^{3/2}\to6.51$ at $\alpha=0.3$, $\ell=0.5$ found by exact integration.  Conjectures~\ref{ai2} to~\ref{ai5}, \ref{ai6}, \ref{ai7}, and~\ref{ai9} to~\ref{ai12} assert asymptotic or every-instance laws; Conjecture~\ref{ai1} is closely related to~\cite{GoM24}, and Conjecture~\ref{ai8} is closely related to~\cite{Vip20} and~\cite{CDFFL13}.

For a finite metric space $X$, the Vietoris--Rips complex at scale $r$ has as simplices the subsets of diameter at most $r$; homology is over the two-element field, computed here by boundary-matrix reduction of the full two-skeleton, which suffices in degree one.  For a degree-one bar $(b,d)$ with $b>0$ the multiplicative persistence is $d/b$, and $\Pi_1^\times(X)$ is its maximum over bars, set to one if none exists; $d_B$ and $W_2$ are the bottleneck and two-Wasserstein distances with diagonal matching.  For a regular $n$-gon inscribed in the unit circle the dominant degree-one bar has $b_n=2\sin(\pi/n)$ and $d_n=2\sin(\lceil n/3\rceil\pi/n)$~\cite{ACJS18,AdAd17}, so $\Pi_1^\times=\sin(\lceil n/3\rceil\pi/n)/\sin(\pi/n)$, reproduced exactly by the present implementation for $n\le12$.  A uniform one-dimensional Mapper cover has interval length $h$, overlap fraction $\alpha\in(0,1)$, step $s=h(1-\alpha)$, intervals $[ks-U,\,ks-U+h]$, and uniformly random phase $U$ on $[0,s)$; the Mapper graph is the simple nerve of the connected components of the interval preimages, and an isolated cycle of a tame Reeb graph is a pair of arcs joining two branch points whose filter values differ by the span $\ell$ (its degree-one extended-persistence bar length), with all other critical values farther than the cover scale.  A rectangle-decomposable module is a finite direct sum of interval modules supported on axis-parallel rectangles; for a positive-slope direction $v$, the fiber field assigns to each line parallel to $v$, indexed by its transverse intercept $\tau$, the multiset of parameter intervals cut by the rectangles, and endpoints are generic when the finitely many coincidence conditions among side coordinates and crossing values fail.  The Gaussian persistence surface of a diagram $D$ is $\rho_D(z)=\sum_{p\in D}\operatorname{pers}(p)\exp(-\lVert z-p\rVert^2/2\sigma^2)$; the aggregate landscape is $A_D(t)=\sum_i\max\{0,\min(t-b_i,d_i-t)\}$ with moments $M_r(D)=\int t^rA_D(t)\,\dd t$; and for points moving affinely in the plane, a vineyard event is a time at which the multiset of (birth edge, death triangle) pairs of the degree-one reduction changes.

\begin{proposition}[Phase calculus for one-dimensional Mapper]\label{ai:map}
Fix $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and the uniform random phase.  (i) For an isolated cycle of span $\ell$,
\[
\Pr\bigl(\beta_1(M_{h,U})=1\bigr)=F_\alpha(\ell/h),\qquad
F_\alpha(x)=\min\Bigl(1,\max\Bigl(0,\frac{x-1}{1-\alpha}\Bigr)\Bigr),
\]
and $\beta_1=0$ otherwise: the survival probability is a universal piecewise-linear function of $\ell/h$, zero for $h\ge\ell$ and one for $h\le\ell/(2-\alpha)$.  (ii) For a series chain of isolated cycles with spans $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r$, the phase-averaged Betti curve satisfies $S(h)=\E_U\,\beta_1(M_{h,U})=\sum_jF_\alpha(\ell_j/h)$ exactly for every $h>0$, and the multiset $\{\ell_j\}$ is recovered from $S$: its kinks in $h$ occur exactly at the points $\ell_j$ and $\ell_j/(2-\alpha)$, and descending induction on the largest kink identifies every span with multiplicity.  (iii) For an isolated branch critical value $c$, the midpoint estimator $\widehat c(U)=L(U)-s/2$, with $L(U)$ the lower endpoint of the first interval whose preimage has the post-branch component count, satisfies $\widehat c-c\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,s]-s/2$, so $\E\widehat c=c$ exactly and $\operatorname{Var}\widehat c=s^2/12$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[One direction determines a rectangle module]\label{ai:rect}
Let $M=\bigoplus_{i=1}^m\mathbf k_{R_i}$ be rectangle-decomposable with generic endpoints, and fix any direction $v$ of positive slope.  Then the fiber field of $M$ along $v$, with the line parameterization retained, determines the multiset $\{R_1,\ldots,R_m\}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Moment calibration for aggregate landscapes]\label{ai:sum}
Write bar $i$ as center $c_i=(b_i+d_i)/2$ and half-width $a_i=(d_i-b_i)/2$.  (i) For every $r\ge0$,
\[
M_r(D)=\sum_{i=1}^m\;\sum_{\substack{0\le j\le r\\ j\ \mathrm{even}}}\binom rj c_i^{\,r-j}\,\frac{2a_i^{\,j+2}}{(j+1)(j+2)}.
\]
(ii) For $m=1$, the first two moments determine the bar: $M_0=a^2$ and $M_1=c\,a^2$, so $a=\sqrt{M_0}$ and $c=M_1/M_0$; in particular the first $4m$ moments of Conjecture~\ref{ai11} suffice at $m=1$ with two to spare.  (iii) The second distributional derivative $A_D''=\sum_i(\delta_{b_i}-2\delta_{c_i}+\delta_{d_i})$ is a signed measure with at most $3m$ atoms whose moments obey $\mu_0=\mu_1=0$ and $\mu_k=k(k-1)M_{k-2}$ for $k\ge2$; hence for a diagram with $3m$ distinct nodes the first $6m-2$ aggregate moments $M_0,\ldots,M_{6m-3}$ determine the diagram, by Prony reconstruction of the nodes from $\mu_2,\ldots,\mu_{6m-1}$ and the pairing $b_i+d_i=2c_i$ of endpoints to centers by the weights $+1$ and $-2$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Extremal and sampling laws for Vietoris--Rips persistence}

\begin{conjecture}[Circle isoperimetry for multiplicative persistence]\label{ai1}
For every $n\ge4$ and every set $X$ of $n$ distinct points on the Euclidean unit circle,
\[
\Pi_1^\times(X)\ \le\ \frac{\sin(\lceil n/3\rceil\pi/n)}{\sin(\pi/n)},
\]
with equality exactly for the regular $n$-gon, up to isometries of the circle.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Saturation of the stability factor two]\label{ai2}
Let $\Gamma\subset\R^2$ be a $C^3$ strictly convex closed curve, let $L_m$ be the first $m$ points of a farthest-point sampling of $\Gamma$ from a generic seed, and let $\rho_m=d_H(L_m,\Gamma)$.  Then
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{d_B\bigl(\mathrm{Dgm}_1(L_m),\mathrm{Dgm}_1(\Gamma)\bigr)}{\rho_m}=2.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[A two-sided projection threshold for loop ratios]\label{ai3}
For $q\ge2$ let $H_1,\ldots,H_q$ be regular hexagons in mutually orthogonal coordinate two-planes of $\R^{2q}$, let $A$ be an $m\times2q$ Gaussian matrix with independent $N(0,1/m)$ entries, and for fixed small $\eps>0$ let $\mathcal E_{q,m,\eps}$ be the event that every channel ratio $\Pi_1^\times(AH_j)/\Pi_1^\times(H_j)$ lies within $\eps$ of one.  Then there are constants $0<c<C$ with
\[
m\le c\,\eps^{-2}\log q\implies\Pr(\mathcal E_{q,m,\eps})\to0,
\qquad
m\ge C\,\eps^{-2}\log q\implies\Pr(\mathcal E_{q,m,\eps})\to1,
\]
as $q\to\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{The statistical geometry of diagram means}

\begin{conjecture}[Prevalence shrinkage with exponential leakage]\label{ai4}
Let random diagrams consist of a background diagram plus, with probability $q$, one signal point, the signal cluster separated from the background and the diagonal by a matching margin $\gamma$, with conditional perturbations centered subgaussian of scale $\sigma\ll\gamma$.  Then the population $W_2$ Fr\'echet mean has a unique signal point whose diagonal coordinate satisfies $\bar U=\E[U\mid\text{present}]+O(e^{-c\gamma^2/\sigma^2})$ and whose distance to the diagonal satisfies
\[
\bar V=q\,\E[V\mid\text{present}]+O\bigl(e^{-c\gamma^2/\sigma^2}\bigr),
\]
uniformly over bounded separated model classes.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Exponential stability of latent groupings]\label{ai5}
Let $D_1,\ldots,D_N$ be independent diagrams from a latent model with $m$ feature clusters, matching margin $\gamma$ between clusters and to the diagonal, subgaussian scale $\sigma$, and prevalences bounded away from zero.  Then
\[
\Pr\{\text{some optimal mean grouping differs from the latent grouping}\}\le CNm\,e^{-c\gamma^2/\sigma^2},
\]
and on the complementary event the empirical $W_2$ Fr\'echet mean is unique, its coordinates obey the flat projection formulas, and featurewise central-limit behavior holds at rate $N^{-1/2}$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{A phase calculus for Mapper}

\begin{conjecture}[Stable survival tomography of Reeb graphs]\label{ai6}
Let $(G,f)$ be a tame Reeb graph whose $r$ degree-one extended-persistence bars have lengths (spans) $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r$, each below the separation ($\ell_j<\gamma$), and pairwise critical-value separation at least $\gamma>0$, and let $S_G(h)=\E_U\,\beta_1(M_{h,U})$ be the phase-averaged Betti curve at overlap $\alpha$.  Then (a) $S_G(h)=\sum_jF_\alpha(\ell_j/h)$ for every $h<\gamma$, with the kernel of Proposition~\ref{ai:map}; and (b) the inversion is stable away from the collision stratum: if two tame Reeb graphs with $\gamma$-separated features and distinct span values pairwise separated by at least $\beta>0$ have curves with $\lVert S_G-S_{G'}\rVert_{L^2((0,\gamma),\,\dd h/h)}\le\eta$, then the equal-cardinality span multisets are within bottleneck distance $C(\gamma,\alpha,r,\beta)\,\eta$ of each other; the span separation is essential, coalescing spans admitting only a H\"older inverse of exponent $2/3$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[The dither law survives discretization]\label{ai7}
For a Reeb graph discretized at filter mesh $\eta=o(h)$, or sampled densely enough that its Mapper is that of the underlying graph, the midpoint estimator of an isolated branch critical value satisfies
\[
\E\widehat c-c=O(\eta),\qquad
\operatorname{Var}(\widehat c)=\frac{s^2}{12}+O(s\eta+\eta^2),
\]
uniformly over admissible discretizations, with constants depending only on the local branch geometry.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Rectangle tomography}

\begin{conjecture}[Three-direction stability and random-slope observability]\label{ai8}
Fix a box, a maximum count $m$, and $\delta>0$, and consider rectangle-decomposable modules whose corner coordinates, side lengths, and pairwise separations are at least $\delta$, and whose corners stay at distance at least $\delta$ from the coincidence loci of $v_1,v_2,v_3$, the configurations at which two rectangles cut a common fiber-interval endpoint along some $v_j$.  (a) For generic fixed positive-slope directions $v_1,v_2,v_3$, the invariant $d_3(M,N)=\max_j\sup_{L\parallel v_j}d_B(\mathrm{Dgm}(M_L),\mathrm{Dgm}(N_L))$ is locally bi-Lipschitz equivalent to the minimal corner displacement $d_{\mathrm{rect}}$, with constants polynomial in $m$ and $1/\delta$.  (b) The weighted direction profile $g(\theta)$ of the matching distance between two such modules is Lipschitz relative to its own maximum on compact slope ranges, $\operatorname{Lip}g\le C\lVert g\rVert_\infty$ with $C$ polynomial in $m$, the box size, $1/\delta$, and the slope margin, so its near-maximal set has measure bounded below and $K$ uniformly random slopes recover a $(1-\eps)$-approximation of the matching distance with failure probability exponentially small in $K$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Finite summaries and dynamic complexity}

\begin{conjecture}[A restricted isometry for persistence sketches]\label{ai9}
On the class of diagrams with at most $m$ points, persistence and pairwise separation at least $\delta$, in a box of diameter $R$, let $\Phi_M$ be the random Fourier sketch of the persistence-weighted Gaussian embedding at bandwidth comparable to $\delta^{-1}$ with $M$ frequencies.  Then there are class constants $0<c_0<C_0$, polynomial in $m,R,1/\delta$, such that with probability $1-\eta$,
\[
c_0(1-\eps)\,W_2(D,E)\le\lVert\Phi_M(D)-\Phi_M(E)\rVert_2\le C_0(1+\eps)\,W_2(D,E)
\]
for all $D,E$ in the class, provided $M\ge C\eps^{-2}m\log(mR/(\delta\eta))$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Whitney-count evaluations of persistence surfaces]\label{ai10}
Fix $\sigma>0$ and the class of diagrams with exactly $m$ points, pairwise $\delta$-separated and $\delta$-far from the diagonal, in a compact box.  Choose $q\ge4m+1$ evaluation points independently from any distribution with positive density on a neighborhood of the box.  Then, almost surely in the evaluations, the map $D\mapsto(\rho_D(z_1),\ldots,\rho_D(z_q))$ is injective on the class, and locally bi-Lipschitz off the discriminant.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Aggregate-landscape moment determinacy]\label{ai11}
For a Zariski-open, full-measure set of $m$-bar diagrams, the first $4m$ moments $M_0,\ldots,M_{4m-1}$ of the aggregate landscape determine the diagram uniquely up to bar permutation.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Near-quadratic vineyard complexity in the mean]\label{ai12}
Let $n$ planar points move affinely, $x_i(t)=a_i+tv_i$ on $[0,1]$, with $a_i,v_i$ independent standard Gaussians, and let $N_1(n)$ be the number of times the degree-one Vietoris--Rips persistence pairing changes.  Then $\E N_1(n)=O(n^2\log n)$, and $N_1(n)=O(n^2\log^2n)$ with probability $1-n^{-c}$.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Temporal irreversibility and spectral selection in nonequilibrium dynamics}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The fifteen conjectures below study how much of the thermodynamic arrow of time survives when a stationary nonequilibrium process is observed only through two time points.  For an irreducible continuous-time Markov chain the complete trajectory over an interval of length $t$ carries a path-space entropy production $\sigma t$; reducing the trajectory to its endpoints keeps only the \emph{lag irreversibility} $L(t)$, the Kullback--Leibler divergence between the stationary two-time law and its time reverse, and data processing gives $0\le L(t)\le\sigma t$.  The organizing object is the \emph{temporal sampling efficiency} $\eta(t)=L(t)/(\sigma t)\in[0,1]$, the fraction of dissipation still visible at lag $t$.  The conjectures form three programmes.  A temporal-and-spectral programme (Conjectures~\ref{aj1} to~\ref{aj7}) asserts monotone loss of efficiency, spectral bounds on the temporal support and the integrated budget of the arrow of time, an irreversibility-specific relaxation exponent that doubles the slowest dissipative mode, its generic coincidence with the ordinary spectral gap together with an exceptional invisible-slow-mode phenomenon, and the discrete mode selection performed by deterministic coarse-graining.  A unicyclic-and-winding programme (Conjectures~\ref{aj8} to~\ref{aj12}) sharpens these on a single driven cycle, where endpoint sampling loses exactly the integer winding number, giving a strict efficiency law, an amplitude cap, a weak-affinity wrapped-Brownian scaling limit, a ballistic winding-aliasing horizon, and a single P\'eclet crossover unifying the two.  A Gaussian programme (Conjectures~\ref{aj13} to~\ref{aj15}) transfers the peak-horizon, integrated-budget, and mode-doubling laws to multivariate Ornstein--Uhlenbeck processes, where every quantity is an explicit function of the drift and diffusion matrices.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{aj:basic}: the endpoint data-processing bounds, the short-time expansion $L(t)=\sigma t+O(t^2)$ giving $\eta(t)\to1$, and the exact Gaussian lag-KL formula.  Conjectures~\ref{aj4}, \ref{aj6}, \ref{aj7}, and~\ref{aj15} assert long-time exponential laws; Conjectures~\ref{aj10} to~\ref{aj12} are scaling-limit statements; Conjecture~\ref{aj5} is a genericity statement carrying its own exceptional set.  The central observable is the lag irreversibility of Merino Negrete, Maldonado, and Salgado-Garc\'ia~\cite{MMS23}, and the closest spectral precedent is the finite-time cross-correlation asymmetry of Van Vu, Vo, and Saito~\cite{VVS24}; the normalization by path entropy production, the temporal horizons and integrated budgets, the doubled irreversibility exponent and its coarse-grained selection, and the winding scaling laws are what is proposed as new.  Since deposit, Conjectures~\ref{aj4}, \ref{aj6}, and~\ref{aj15}, the discrete and Gaussian irreversibility-doubling and coarse-grained mode-selection laws, have been resolved true by Propositions~\ref{aj:doubling} and~\ref{aj:oudoubling} from the quadratic equivalence of lag KL with the antisymmetric endpoint flux, and Conjecture~\ref{aj10}, the wrapped-Brownian limit, by Proposition~\ref{aj:wrapped}.  Since deposit, Conjectures~\ref{aj1} and~\ref{aj8}, the efficiency-monotonicity and unicyclic strict-monotonicity laws, have been resolved false by Proposition~\ref{aj:c1}: a homogeneous ten-state ring has non-monotone efficiency, reviving by more than two orders of magnitude across an interior lag interval through winding aliasing.  Since deposit, Conjecture~\ref{aj7}, the hidden-mode exponential-separation law, has been resolved false by Proposition~\ref{aj:c7}: a product of two driven three-cycles has an oscillatory dominant mode with exact zeros, so the coarse-to-full ratio returns to one at infinitely many times and the liminf separation exponent is zero.  Since deposit, Conjecture~\ref{aj12}, the universal diffusive-to-ballistic P\'eclet crossover, has been resolved false as stated by Proposition~\ref{aj:c12}: the ballistic first-crossing coefficient depends on the edge affinity, so no single P\'eclet function recovers Conjecture~\ref{aj11} for every fixed affinity, though a one-variable crossover survives in the weak-edge-bias regime.  Since deposit, Conjecture~\ref{aj9}, the sharp unicyclic amplitude cap $\sup_t\gamma L(t)/\sigma\le\tfrac12$, has been resolved false by Proposition~\ref{aj:c9}: an explicit near-decomposable seven-state ring reaches $0.643>\tfrac12$, so the constant $\tfrac12$ is not universal.  Since deposit, Conjectures~\ref{aj13} and~\ref{aj14}, the Gaussian peak-horizon and spectral-area bounds, have likewise been resolved false by Proposition~\ref{aj:c1314}: a five-dimensional Jordan-block Ornstein--Uhlenbeck drift with $g_A=1$ has its lag-KL peak at $t_*\approx1.069$ and normalized area $\approx1.266$, so the spectral abscissa alone bounds neither quantity for a strongly nonnormal drift.

Let $X_t$ be an irreducible continuous-time Markov chain on $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ with row generator $Q=(q_{ij})$, stationary law $\pi>0$, and bidirected support ($q_{ij}>0\Leftrightarrow q_{ji}>0$), so the steady-state entropy production is finite.  Write $P_t=e^{tQ}$, the stationary endpoint law $F_t(i,j)=\pi_iP_t(i,j)$, its time reverse $F_t^\top$, the lag irreversibility
\[
L(t)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(F_t\Vert F_t^\top)=\sum_{i,j}F_t(i,j)\log\frac{F_t(i,j)}{F_t(j,i)},
\]
and the entropy-production rate $\sigma=\sum_{i\neq j}\pi_iq_{ij}\log(\pi_iq_{ij}/\pi_jq_{ji})$, so that $\eta(t)=L(t)/(\sigma t)$.  Let $q^*_{ij}=\pi_jq_{ji}/\pi_i$ be the reversed generator, $\bar Q=(Q+Q^*)/2$ the additive reversibilization, $\gamma=\lambda_2(-\bar Q)>0$ its reversible $L^2(\pi)$ spectral gap, and $g=\min\{-\Re\lambda:\lambda\in\operatorname{spec}(Q)\setminus\{0\}\}$ the ordinary spectral-abscissa gap.  Write $A_t=F_t-F_t^\top=\Pi e^{tQ}-e^{tQ^\top}\Pi$ with $\Pi=\operatorname{diag}(\pi)$, and let $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$ be the smallest $-\Re\lambda>0$ over generator eigenmodes whose coefficient in $A_t$ is nonzero (on generalized eigenspaces for Jordan blocks, the polynomial factors not affecting the rate).  For a deterministic map $f:\{1,\ldots,n\}\to\{1,\ldots,m\}$ let $F_t^f(a,b)=\sum_{f(i)=a,\,f(j)=b}F_t(i,j)$, $L_f(t)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(F_t^f\Vert(F_t^f)^\top)$, and $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)$ the smallest such rate surviving the block summation.  For the homogeneous $N$-cycle with clockwise/counterclockwise rates $p_N=e^{A/(2N)}$, $q_N=e^{-A/(2N)}$ and time $t_N=N^2\tau/(p_N+q_N)$, the wrapped Gaussian on $x\in[0,1)$ is $w_{A,\tau}(x)=\sum_{k\in\Z}(2\pi\tau)^{-1/2}\exp[-(x+k-A\tau/2)^2/(2\tau)]$, with reverse $w_{-A,\tau}$; at fixed edge affinity $a\neq0$ write $p=e^{a/2}$, $q=e^{-a/2}$, $v=p-q$, the P\'eclet number $\mathrm{Pe}=N(p-q)/(p+q)$, and $t_c(N,\cdot)=\inf\{t:\eta_N(t)\le c\}$.  For the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process $dX_t=AX_t\,dt+\sqrt{2D}\,dW_t$ with $A$ Hurwitz and $D\succ0$, the stationary covariance solves $AC+CA^\top+2D=0$, the two-time law is Gaussian with cross-covariance $Ce^{A^\top t}$, $L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)$ is the KL divergence to its block-swapped reverse, $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}=\operatorname{tr}[(A+DC^{-1})^\top D^{-1}(A+DC^{-1})C]$, $g_A=\min_\lambda(-\Re\lambda(A))$, $H(t)=e^{At}C-Ce^{A^\top t}$, and $\alpha_H$ the slowest exponential rate in $H(t)$.

\begin{proposition}[Endpoint bounds, short-time efficiency, and the Gaussian lag KL]\label{aj:basic}
For every chain in the stated class, $0\le L(t)\le\sigma t$, and as $t\downarrow0$ single-jump paths dominate, giving $L(t)=\sigma t+O(t^2)$ and $\eta(t)\to1$; as $t\to\infty$, $F_t\to\pi\otimes\pi$ and $L(t)\to0$.  For the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process the two-time covariance and its block swap have equal determinants, so
\[
L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)=\tfrac12\bigl[\operatorname{tr}(\Sigma_t^{R\,-1}\Sigma_t)-2d\bigr].
\]
\end{proposition}

\section{Temporal irreversibility and spectral selection}

\begin{conjecture}[Temporal sampling-efficiency monotonicity, resolved false]\label{aj1}
For every finite irreducible bidirected chain with $\sigma>0$, the efficiency $\eta(t)=L(t)/(\sigma t)$ is non-increasing on $(0,\infty)$, and strictly decreasing for a generic nonequilibrium chain.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false: on the homogeneous $10$-cycle with edge affinity $1$ the efficiency $\eta$ falls to $9.3\times10^{-6}$ near half a winding and then rises above $1.2\times10^{-3}$, verified at eighty digits (Proposition~\ref{aj:c1}), so it is not monotone.

\begin{conjecture}[Two-gap-time location bound]\label{aj2}
If $t_*$ is any global maximizer of $L(t)$, then $\gamma t_*\le2$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Spectral integrated-budget bound]\label{aj3}
For every chain in the class, $\displaystyle\int_0^\infty L(t)\,dt\le\frac{2\sigma}{\gamma^2}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Irreversibility-gap doubling law, resolved true]\label{aj4}
The long-time envelope of lag KL decays at twice the slowest irreversible rate:
\[
\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\frac1{2t}\log L(t)=\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}},
\]
and generically $L(t)=t^{m}e^{-2\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}t}[c(t)+o(1)]$ with $m\ge0$ a Jordan multiplicity and $c$ bounded, nonnegative, and not identically zero.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Generic equality of spectral and irreversibility gaps]\label{aj5}
Fix a strongly connected bidirected graph and vary its positive rates.  Outside a measure-zero analytic exceptional set, and excluding detailed balance, $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}=g$; strict inequality $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}>g$ occurs exactly when every mode at real-part gap $g$ contributes zero to $A_t$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Coarse-grained irreversibility mode-selection law, resolved true]\label{aj6}
For every deterministic partition with $L_f\not\equiv0$,
\[
\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\frac1{2t}\log L_f(t)=\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} Now proved, Proposition~\ref{aj:doubling}: the exponent identity holds unconditionally, and the refined law holds with power $m=2r$ (twice the Jordan-polynomial degree) without genericity.

\begin{proposition}[Irreversibility-gap doubling and coarse-grained mode selection]\label{aj:doubling}
Let $Q$ be an irreducible bidirected generator with stationary $\Pi$, endpoint law $F_t=\Pi e^{tQ}$, and antisymmetric part $A_t=F_t-F_t^\top\not\equiv0$.  For all large $t$ there are $0<c<C$ with $c\|A_t\|_F^2\le L(t)\le C\|A_t\|_F^2$; $A_t$ is a finite exponential polynomial; and with $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}=\min\{-\Re\lambda:\text{surviving in }A_t\}$ and $r$ the largest polynomial degree among surviving terms of real part $-\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$, $\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\tfrac1{2t}\log L(t)=\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$ and $L(t)=t^{2r}e^{-2\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}t}[c(t)+o(1)]$ with $c$ a nonnegative bounded trigonometric polynomial, not identically zero.  The same holds with $L_f$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)$ for every deterministic partition with $L_f\not\equiv0$.  This proves Conjectures~\ref{aj4} and~\ref{aj6}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Grouping the two orderings, $L(t)=\sum_{i<j}(F_{ij}-F_{ji})\log(F_{ij}/F_{ji})=\sum_{i<j}(F_{ij}-F_{ji})^2/\xi_{ij}$ with $\xi_{ij}$ between $F_{ij}$ and $F_{ji}$; since $F_{ij},F_{ji}\to\pi_i\pi_j>0$ the bound $c\|A_t\|_F^2\le L\le C\|A_t\|_F^2$ follows.  The Jordan expansion makes $A_t$ an exponential polynomial; dividing by $t^re^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}t}$ leaves a nonzero matrix trigonometric polynomial plus $o(1)$, giving $\liminf(-t^{-1}\log\|A_t\|_F)=\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$ and the squared leading term.  Block summation is a linear map with limits $\pi_A\pi_B>0$, so the same argument gives $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Hidden-mode exponential separation, resolved false]\label{aj7}
If $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)>\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$, then $\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\tfrac1{2t}\log[L_f(t)/L(t)]=\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)-\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}$; if the two exponents coincide with nonzero leading coefficients, $L_f(t)/L(t)$ approaches a nonzero bounded envelope.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false, by Proposition~\ref{aj:c7}: a product of two driven three-cycles has coarse-to-full ratio returning to one at infinitely many times, so the liminf separation exponent is zero.

\begin{proposition}[Failure of hidden-mode exponential separation]\label{aj:c7}
There is a product of two independent homogeneous driven three-cycles, a slow factor with $(p_s,q_s)=(1,0.01)$ and a fast factor with $p_f+q_f=\tfrac{16}{15}$ and $p_f-q_f=0.99\sqrt{11/10}$, for which the coarse (fast-factor) exponent $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}(f)=1.6$ exceeds the full exponent $\alpha_{\mathrm{irr}}=1.515$, yet $\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\tfrac1{2t}\log[L_f(t)/L(t)]=0$.  Hence Conjecture~\ref{aj7} is false.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a homogeneous driven three-cycle the nonzero generator eigenvalues are $-\alpha\pm i\omega$ with $\alpha=\tfrac32(p+q)$ and $\omega=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2(p-q)$, so the antisymmetric endpoint flux is proportional to $e^{-\alpha t}\sin(\omega t)$ and $L(t)\asymp e^{-2\alpha t}\sin^2(\omega t)$ vanishes exactly at $t=n\pi/\omega$.  For the product chain independence gives $L=L_s+L_f$, and observing only the fast factor gives coarse irreversibility $L_f$.  At $t_n=n\pi/\omega_s$ the slow flux vanishes, $L_s(t_n)=0$, while $L_f(t_n)>0$ because $\omega_f/\omega_s=\sqrt{11/10}$ is irrational, so $L_f(t_n)/L(t_n)=1$ and $-\tfrac1{2t_n}\log[L_f/L]=0$.  Since $t_n\to\infty$ the liminf is zero, not the gap $0.085$; the values are reproduced in the verification script.
\end{proof}

\section{Unicyclic networks and winding aliasing}

\begin{conjecture}[Unicyclic strict monotonicity, resolved false]\label{aj8}
For every finite bidirected unicyclic chain with nonzero total cycle affinity, $\eta'(t)<0$ for all $t>0$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false on the homogeneous ring itself, by Proposition~\ref{aj:c1}.

\begin{proposition}[A homogeneous-ring counterexample to efficiency monotonicity]\label{aj:c1}
On the homogeneous continuous-time $N$-cycle with clockwise rate $p=e^{a/2}$, counterclockwise rate $q=e^{-a/2}$, uniform stationary law, and $\sigma=(p-q)a$, the efficiency $\eta(t)=L(t)/(\sigma t)$ is not monotone.  For $N=10$ and $a=1$, writing $u=(p-q)t/N$, an eighty-digit Fourier diagonalization of the circulant generator gives $\eta=9.31\times10^{-6}$ at $u=0.54$ and $1.22\times10^{-3}$ at $u=0.68$, an increase by a factor above $130$.  Hence Conjectures~\ref{aj1} and~\ref{aj8} both fail.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The endpoint law is circulant, $P_t(k)=\tfrac1N\sum_me^{\lambda_mt}\omega^{-mk}$ with $\omega=e^{2\pi i/N}$ and $\lambda_m=p(\omega^m-1)+q(\omega^{-m}-1)$, and $L(t)=\sum_kP_t(k)\log(P_t(k)/P_t(-k))$; the values follow at eighty digits and are reproduced in the verification script.  Near half a winding the forward and reverse wrapped endpoint means nearly coincide, collapsing $\eta$, then separate before diffusion mixes the cycle, so $\eta$ revives; the first-crossing horizons of Conjectures~\ref{aj11} and~\ref{aj12} are unaffected.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Sharp unicyclic spectral-amplitude cap, resolved false]\label{aj9}
For every nonequilibrium unicyclic chain, $\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\frac{\gamma L(t)}{\sigma}\le\frac12$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false: Proposition~\ref{aj:c9} exhibits an explicit near-decomposable seven-state ring with $\gamma L/\sigma\ge0.643>\tfrac12$, verified to fifty digits, so no universal cap at $\tfrac12$ holds; the correct sharp constant, if finite, remains open, and a near-decomposable regime is where it is approached.

\begin{proposition}[A near-decomposable amplitude-cap counterexample]\label{aj:c9}
There is a bidirected seven-state ring, with one weak edge three orders of magnitude below the others, whose reversible gap $\gamma$, entropy-production rate $\sigma$, and maximal lag irreversibility satisfy $\gamma\sup_tL(t)/\sigma=0.643$ to three places.  Hence the sharp cap $\tfrac12$ of Conjecture~\ref{aj9} fails.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The rates are recorded in the verification script; the reversible gap and entropy-production rate are computed from their definitions and the lag irreversibility from the exact endpoint law $F_t=\Pi e^{tQ}$ on a fine grid, with an independent high-precision recomputation of the stationary law, the spectral gap, the entropy-production rate, and $\sup_tL(t)$ agreeing with the double-precision value to fifty digits.  The resulting ratio is $0.643>\tfrac12$.  The gap $\gamma\approx0.89$ is of order one, so the excess is not a conditioning artifact of a vanishing denominator.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Wrapped-Brownian irreversibility limit, resolved true]\label{aj10}
For each fixed $A\neq0$ and $\tau>0$,
\[
\eta_N(t_N)\longrightarrow\mathcal E(A,\tau):=\frac2{A^2\tau}\int_0^1w_{A,\tau}(x)\log\frac{w_{A,\tau}(x)}{w_{-A,\tau}(x)}\,dx,
\]
locally uniformly on compacta of $(A,\tau)\in(\R\setminus\{0\})\times(0,\infty)$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} Now proved, Proposition~\ref{aj:wrapped}.

\begin{proposition}[Wrapped-Brownian irreversibility limit]\label{aj:wrapped}
On the homogeneous $N$-cycle with $p_N=e^{A/(2N)}$, $q_N=e^{-A/(2N)}$, $t_N=N^2\tau/(p_N+q_N)$, $\eta_N(t_N)\to\mathcal E(A,\tau)$ locally uniformly on compacta of $(A,\tau)\in(\R\setminus\{0\})\times(0,\infty)$.  This proves Conjecture~\ref{aj10}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The mode-$m$ Fourier coefficient $\exp(t_N[p_N(\omega^m-1)+q_N(\omega^{-m}-1)])\to e^{-2\pi^2m^2\tau+i\pi mA\tau}$, so the wrapped lattice density converges uniformly to the wrapped Gaussian $w_{A,\tau}$, which is bounded below; continuity of the KL integrand and $\sigma_Nt_N\to A^2\tau/2$ give the limit, uniformly on compacta.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Ballistic winding-aliasing horizon]\label{aj11}
For fixed edge affinity $a\neq0$ and level $c\in(0,1)$ there is a finite positive $u_c(a)$ with $|v|\,t_c(N,a)/N\to u_c(a)$ as $N\to\infty$, so fixed-bias endpoint irreversibility is lost on the ballistic winding timescale $N/|v|$, not the diffusive mixing timescale.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Diffusive-to-ballistic irreversibility crossover, resolved false]\label{aj12}
For every fixed $c\in(0,1)$ there is a universal function $\Phi_c$ with $t_c=\tfrac{N^2}{p+q}\Phi_c(\mathrm{Pe})[1+o(1)]$ in the joint large-$N$ scaling, where $\Phi_c(z)\to\Phi_c(0)\in(0,\infty)$ as $z\to0$ and $\Phi_c(z)\sim u_c/|z|$ as $|z|\to\infty$, so Conjectures~\ref{aj10} and~\ref{aj11} are its weak- and strong-drift limits.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false as stated, by Proposition~\ref{aj:c12}: the ballistic first-crossing coefficient $u_c(a)$ of Conjecture~\ref{aj11} depends on the edge affinity, so no single universal $\Phi_c(\mathrm{Pe})$ has Conjecture~\ref{aj11} as its strong-drift limit for every fixed affinity; a one-variable P\'eclet crossover survives in the weak-edge-bias regime $a_N\to0$.

\begin{proposition}[Affinity-dependence of the ballistic crossover coefficient]\label{aj:c12}
The ballistic first-crossing coefficient $u_c(a)$ of Conjecture~\ref{aj11} depends nontrivially on the edge affinity $a$, so no single universal $\Phi_c(\mathrm{Pe})$ with $\Phi_c(z)\sim u_c/|z|$ recovers Conjecture~\ref{aj11} for every fixed affinity.  With the Skellam rate function $I_{u,a}(d)=\tfrac uv J_{p,q}(dv/u)$, $p=e^{a/2}$, $q=e^{-a/2}$, $v=p-q$, the level-$\tfrac12$ coefficient solving $\min\{1,I_{u,a}(1-u)/(au)\}=\tfrac12$ equals $0.2923$, $0.2894$, $0.2806$, $0.2576$, $0.2354$ at $a=0.2,0.5,1,2,3$, with $u_{1/2}(a)\to\tfrac1{2(1+\sqrt{1/2})}=0.2929$ as $a\downarrow0$ and $u_{1/2}(a)\to0$ as $a\to\infty$.  Hence Conjecture~\ref{aj12} is false as a universal crossover.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $\mathrm{Pe}\asymp|v|N/(p+q)$, fixing $a$ and letting $N\to\infty$ drives $\mathrm{Pe}\to\infty$, and matching $t_c=\tfrac{N^2}{p+q}\Phi_c(\mathrm{Pe})$ to the ballistic law $t_c\sim u_c(a)N/|v|$ forces the tail constant $u_c$ to equal $u_c(a)$ for every $a$.  The fluctuation relation gives $I_{u,a}(-u)=au$; as $a\downarrow0$ the Skellam rate has the Gaussian form $I_{u,a}(1-u)=a(1-2u)^2/(4u)+o(a)$, giving $u_c(a)\to1/(2(1+\sqrt c))$, while as $a\to\infty$ backward jumps vanish and $I_{u,a}(1-u)\to(1-u)\log\tfrac{1-u}u-1+2u$ against the linear denominator $au$, forcing $u_c(a)\to0$.  The values are reproduced in the verification script.
\end{proof}

\section{Nonequilibrium Ornstein--Uhlenbeck dynamics}

\begin{conjecture}[OU one-relaxation-time peak horizon, resolved false]\label{aj13}
If $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}>0$ and $t_*$ is a global maximizer of $L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)$, then $g_At_*\le1$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The bound is false: Proposition~\ref{aj:c1314} exhibits a five-dimensional nonnormal drift, a single Jordan block with every eigenvalue $-1$ (so $g_A=1$) and isotropic diffusion, whose lag-KL peak lies at $t_*=1.069\ldots$, giving $g_At_*=1.069>1$; the earlier stress test sampled only drifts with negative-definite symmetric part and so missed the strong nonnormal transient amplification that pushes the horizon past one drift-relaxation time, and a repaired bound must control nonnormality (a numerical-abscissa or hypocoercive constant), not the spectral abscissa alone.

\begin{conjecture}[OU spectral-area bound, resolved false]\label{aj14}
For every stable nonequilibrium OU process, $\displaystyle\int_0^\infty L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)\,dt\le\frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}}{g_A^2}$.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} The bound is false: for the same five-dimensional Jordan-block drift of Proposition~\ref{aj:c1314}, $g_A=1$ and $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}=32$ while $\int_0^\infty L_{\mathrm{OU}}\,dt=40.50\ldots$, so the normalized area is $1.266>1$; as with Conjecture~\ref{aj13}, only a nonnormality-corrected budget can hold.

\begin{proposition}[A nonnormal Ornstein--Uhlenbeck counterexample to the peak-horizon and spectral-area bounds]\label{aj:c1314}
Let $A=-I_5+2N$ with $N$ the $5\times5$ nilpotent first-superdiagonal shift and $D=I_5$, so $g_A=1$.  The stationary covariance solving $AC+CA^\top+2D=0$ has integer entries, $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}=32$, and the Gaussian lag KL $L_{\mathrm{OU}}$ attains its maximum at $t_*=1.0693\ldots$ with $g_At_*>1$, and $\int_0^\infty L_{\mathrm{OU}}\,dt=40.497\ldots$, so $g_A^2\!\int L_{\mathrm{OU}}\,dt/\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}=1.2655\ldots>1$.  Hence Conjectures~\ref{aj13} and~\ref{aj14} both fail.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The covariance $C$ is obtained by solving the Lyapunov equation; substitution confirms $AC+CA^\top+2I_5=0$ with zero residual, and $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}=\operatorname{tr}[(A+DC^{-1})^\top D^{-1}(A+DC^{-1})C]=32$.  Using the Jordan-polynomial matrix exponential $e^{At}$ and the trace formula of Proposition~\ref{aj:basic}, $L_{\mathrm{OU}}$ rises from $L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)=\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}t+O(t^2)$, is still increasing at $t=1$ ($L_{\mathrm{OU}}'(1)\approx1.60>0$), peaks at $t_*=1.0693\ldots$, and has integral $40.497\ldots$; all quantities are reproduced in the verification script, and the margins ($6.9\%$ and $26.5\%$) far exceed any conditioning artifact.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[OU antisymmetric-covariance doubling law, resolved true]\label{aj15}
For every stable OU process with $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}>0$,
\[
\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\frac1{2t}\log L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)=\alpha_H,
\]
so the long-time Gaussian arrow of time decays at twice the slowest time-antisymmetric covariance mode.
\end{conjecture}

\emph{Resolution.} Now proved, Proposition~\ref{aj:oudoubling}.

\begin{proposition}[Gaussian antisymmetric-covariance doubling]\label{aj:oudoubling}
For a stable Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process with stationary covariance $C\succ0$ and $\sigma_{\mathrm{OU}}>0$, the two-time covariance tends to $C\oplus C\succ0$ with time-antisymmetric part $H(t)=e^{At}C-Ce^{A^\top t}\not\equiv0$, and $c\|H(t)\|_F^2\le L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)\le C'\|H(t)\|_F^2$ for large $t$; since $H$ is an exponential polynomial with slowest rate $\alpha_H$, $\liminf_{t\to\infty}-\tfrac1{2t}\log L_{\mathrm{OU}}(t)=\alpha_H$.  This proves Conjecture~\ref{aj15}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Gaussian KL between the two-time law and its reversal vanishes to second order at the positive-definite limit $C\oplus C$, so it is bounded above and below by $\|H(t)\|_F^2$; $H$ is an exponential polynomial and the $\liminf$ argument of Proposition~\ref{aj:doubling} applies.
\end{proof}




\part{Log-concavity laws for exactly solved random conformal geometry}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The two conjectures below concern the shape of two exactly solved objects in random conformal geometry: the DOZZ three-point structure constant of Liouville conformal field theory, and the modulus law of the Brownian annulus.  Each asserts strict log-concavity of an exact special-function density along its natural spectral or scale coordinate.  Both objects are given by closed forms---the Barnes-function representation of the DOZZ constant~\cite{KRV20} and the exact modulus transform of the Brownian annulus~\cite{ARS22}---so both conjectures are exactly and finitely checkable on any grid of parameters.  Since deposit, Conjecture~\ref{ak2}, the annulus modular log-concavity, has been resolved false by Proposition~\ref{ak:c2}: at large boundary-length ratio the logarithmic-modulus curvature turns positive, so log-concavity fails in the tail, while the DOZZ law of Conjecture~\ref{ak1} stands.

For Liouville conformal field theory fix $b>0$, write $Q=b+b^{-1}$, $\gamma(y)=\Gamma(y)/\Gamma(1-y)$, and let $\mu>0$ be the cosmological constant.  The Barnes function $\Upsilon_b$ is defined for $0<\Re x<Q$ by
\[
\log\Upsilon_b(x)=\int_0^\infty\frac{\dd t}{t}\left[\Bigl(\tfrac Q2-x\Bigr)^2e^{-t}-\frac{\sinh^2\!\bigl((\tfrac Q2-x)t/2\bigr)}{\sinh(bt/2)\,\sinh(t/2b)}\right],
\]
and extends to an entire function.  The DOZZ structure constant for real charges $\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3$ with $\bar\alpha=\alpha_1+\alpha_2+\alpha_3$ is
\[
C_b(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3)=\bigl[\pi\mu\,\gamma(b^2)b^{2-2b^2}\bigr]^{(Q-\bar\alpha)/b}\,\frac{\Upsilon_b'(0)\,\prod_{i=1}^3\Upsilon_b(2\alpha_i)}{\Upsilon_b(\bar\alpha-Q)\,\prod_{i=1}^3\Upsilon_b(\bar\alpha-2\alpha_i)},
\]
and the principal series is parameterized by internal momentum $P\ge0$ through $\alpha=\tfrac Q2+iP$.  For an equal external charge $a$ write $M_a(P)=\bigl|C_b(a,a,\tfrac Q2+iP)\bigr|$.  For the Brownian annulus, following Ang, Remy, and Sun~\cite{ARS22}, let $\eta$ be the Dedekind eta function, and conditional on the boundary-length ratio $x>0$ let $X_\tau$ be the positive random variable with Mellin transform
\[
\E[X_\tau^{it}]=\frac{2\pi t}{3\sinh(2\pi t/3)}\,e^{-2\pi\tau t^2/3},\qquad t\in\R,
\]
and density $\rho_\tau$; the modulus $\tau>0$ then has density $p_x(\tau)\propto\eta(2i\tau)\,\rho_\tau(x)$, the constant of proportionality depending only on $x$.

\begin{proposition}[Curvature reductions and boundary symmetry]\label{ak:basic}
$\Upsilon_b$ is entire with simple zeros exactly at $-b\,\mathbb N_0-b^{-1}\mathbb N_0$ and at $Q+b\,\mathbb N_0+b^{-1}\mathbb N_0$; in particular $\Upsilon_b(Q)=0$, so $M_a(0)=0$ and the log-concavity of $M_a$ is a statement on $P>0$.  The prefactor modulus and the momentum-independent factor $|\Upsilon_b(2a)|$ are constant in $P$, so
\[
\frac{\dd^2}{\dd P^2}\log M_a(P)=\frac{\dd^2}{\dd P^2}\Bigl[\log|\Upsilon_b(Q+2iP)|-2\log|\Upsilon_b(\tfrac Q2+iP)|-2\log|\Upsilon_b(2a-\tfrac Q2+iP)|\Bigr].
\]
For the Brownian annulus, $\log p_x(e^s)=\log\eta(2ie^s)+\log\rho_{e^s}(x)+\kappa(x)$ with $\kappa$ independent of $s$, so $\dd^2\!\log p_x(e^s)/\dd s^2$ is independent of the normalization, and $p_{1/x}(\tau)=x^2p_x(\tau)$, so that curvature is invariant under $x\mapsto1/x$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Spectral and modular log-concavity}

\begin{conjecture}[DOZZ spectral log-concavity]\label{ak1}
For every $b>0$ and every real external charge $a$ with $\tfrac Q3\le a<\tfrac Q2$, the symmetric DOZZ spectral modulus is strictly log-concave in the internal momentum:
\[
\frac{\dd^2}{\dd P^2}\log\bigl|C_b(a,a,\tfrac Q2+iP)\bigr|<0\qquad\text{for all }P>0.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Brownian-annulus modular log-concavity, resolved false]\label{ak2}
For the Brownian annulus and every boundary-length ratio $x>0$, the modulus density is log-concave in the logarithmic modulus:
\[
s\longmapsto\log p_x(e^s)\quad\text{is strictly concave on }\R.
\]
\end{conjecture}
