\emph{Resolution.} The conjecture is false at large boundary ratio, by Proposition~\ref{ak:c2}.

\begin{proposition}[Failure of annulus log-concavity at large boundary ratio]\label{ak:c2}
For the Brownian-annulus modulus density $p_x(\tau)\propto\eta(2i\tau)\rho_\tau(x)$, the logarithmic-modulus curvature $\dd^2\log p_x(e^s)/\dd s^2$ is positive for sufficiently large boundary ratio $x$.  At $x=e^5$, $\tau=0.30$ an independent Fourier inversion of the exact modulus transform, multiplied by the exact eta factor, gives curvature $+0.3798$; the value grows to $+0.90$ at $x=e^7$ and to the positive limit $\mathcal C_\infty(0.30)=0.9746$ as $x\to\infty$.  Hence Conjecture~\ref{ak2} is false.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
From the Mellin transform $\mathbb E[X_\tau^{it}]=\tfrac{2\pi t}{3\sinh(2\pi t/3)}e^{-2\pi\tau t^2/3}$, $\log X_\tau=Z+G_\tau$ with $Z$ centred logistic of scale $\tfrac23$ and $G_\tau\sim N(0,\tfrac{4\pi\tau}3)$ independent.  Since $f_Z(z)=\tfrac38\operatorname{sech}^2(\tfrac{3z}4)\sim\tfrac32e^{-3z/2}$ as $z\to\infty$, Gaussian averaging gives $g_\tau(y)\sim\tfrac32e^{-3y/2}\,\mathbb E[e^{3G_\tau/2}]=\tfrac32e^{-3y/2}e^{3\pi\tau/2}$ locally uniformly in $\tau$, and likewise after two $\tau$-derivatives.  With $\eta(2i\tau)$ real and $\log\eta(2i\tau)=-\pi\tau/6+\sum_{n\ge1}\log(1-e^{-4\pi\tau n})$, and writing $x_n=4\pi n\tau$, $q_n=e^{-x_n}$, the curvature of $\log p_x(e^s)$ in $s=\log\tau$ tends as $x\to\infty$ to $\mathcal C_\infty(\tau)=\tfrac{4\pi}3\tau+\sum_{n\ge1}\bigl(\tfrac{x_nq_n}{1-q_n}-\tfrac{x_n^2q_n}{(1-q_n)^2}\bigr)>0$, in which the $\tfrac{4\pi}3\tau$ term is the logistic--Gaussian tail contribution $(-\tfrac\pi6+\tfrac{3\pi}2)\tau$ and the sum is the eta $q$-series, which does not vanish at finite $\tau$ and cannot be dropped.  At $\tau=0.30$ this gives $\mathcal C_\infty=0.9746$.  The finite value at $x=e^5$, $\tau=0.30$ is obtained by direct high-precision Fourier inversion of the exact transform, stable under refinement of the step, and is reproduced in the verification script.
\end{proof}

\part{Bold conjectures in theoretical fluid dynamics}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The fifteen conjectures below are sharp, falsifiable statements about the small-scale and singular behaviour of the incompressible Euler, Navier--Stokes, Boussinesq, generalized surface quasi-geostrophic, and point-vortex systems.  They fall in six groups: two-dimensional small-scale creation (Conjectures~\ref{al1} to~\ref{al3}), enhanced dissipation and hypoelliptic mixing (Conjectures~\ref{al4} and~\ref{al5}), the geometry and regularity of three-dimensional Euler and Navier--Stokes singularities (Conjectures~\ref{al6} to~\ref{al8}), band-limited kinematics and rapid rotation (Conjectures~\ref{al9} and~\ref{al10}), stratified and active-scalar singularities (Conjectures~\ref{al11} to~\ref{al13}), and point-vortex collapse and non-Beltramization (Conjectures~\ref{al14} and~\ref{al15}).  Each is a determinate law with a stated mechanism, a nearest-literature boundary, a first decisive theorem, and a route to disproof, and each is deliberately bold: a proof or a counterexample would reorganize how one reads the corresponding small-scale or singular problem.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{al:anchors}: the exact three-vortex self-similar collapse anchor, the degenerate one-drift enhanced-dissipation exponent, the homogeneity-reduced generalized-SQG neck reduction, and the exact Beltrami Lamb-vector identity with Frenet dilation invariance.  These are statements about continuum solutions---blow-up signatures, vanishing-viscosity and large-bandwidth limits, and long-time or asymptotic laws---so they lie and every one; the accompanying diagnostics test reduced models, exact anchors, or prerequisite scalings, never the full law.  Several carry attributions where a sharp exponent or anchor is due to earlier work: the degenerate-shear exponent of Albritton, Beekie, and Novack~\cite{ABN22} (Conjecture~\ref{al5}), the Lorentz-endpoint boundary of Phuc~\cite{Phuc15} (Conjecture~\ref{al8}), and the self-similar three-vortex collapse of Aref~\cite{Aref10} (Conjecture~\ref{al14}); Conjecture~\ref{al11} is where the part's novelty is most in question, since at equal viscosity and diffusivity its critical-layer anomaly may reduce to known inviscid repeated-root asymptotics times the Kelvin diffusion factor, and only its sharp generic coefficient is claimed as new.

Throughout, $u$ is a divergence-free velocity field on the flat torus or on $\mathbb R^d$, $\omega=\nabla\times u$ (the scalar curl in two dimensions) its vorticity, $S=\tfrac12(\nabla u+\nabla u^\top)$ the strain, $\xi=\omega/|\omega|$ the vorticity direction, and $\hat f(k)$ the Fourier coefficients.  Objects specific to a single conjecture are defined in its statement.

\begin{proposition}[Exact anchors and reductions]\label{al:anchors}
{\rm(i)} For three planar point vortices with circulations $\Gamma=(\Gamma_1,\Gamma_2,\Gamma_3)$ satisfying $\Gamma_1\Gamma_2+\Gamma_2\Gamma_3+\Gamma_3\Gamma_1=0$ and vanishing angular impulse, the motion is exactly self-similar with a finite-time collapse or expansion; the symmetric choice $\Gamma=(1,1,-\tfrac12)$ satisfies the collapse condition.  {\rm(ii)} For the degenerate one-drift shear generator $X_0=y^m\partial_x$, $X_1=\partial_y$ with dissipation $-\nu X_1^\ast X_1$, the enhanced-dissipation gap obeys $\operatorname{gap}(L_\nu)\asymp\nu^{m/(m+2)}$ as $\nu\downarrow0$.  {\rm(iii)} The homogeneity-reduced neck equation $\dot d=-d^{\,1-\beta}$, $0<\beta\le1$, has $d(t)=[\beta(T-t)]^{1/\beta}$, so $d(t)\sim a_\beta(T-t)^{1/\beta}$ and, under the reciprocal-curvature ansatz $\kappa_{\max}\sim b_\beta(T-t)^{-1/\beta}$, the product $d\,\kappa_{\max}$ tends to a finite nonzero constant.  {\rm(iv)} A Beltrami field ($u\times\omega\equiv0$), in particular the ABC field, has identically vanishing Lamb vector; and the dimensionless pair (total curvature, scale-invariant torsion variation) of a $C^4$ space curve is invariant under spatial dilation.
\end{proposition}

\section{Small-scale creation in two-dimensional Euler and Navier--Stokes}

\begin{conjecture}[Bulk-enstrophy spectral front]\label{al1}
Let $\omega$ be a smooth mean-zero solution of the $2$D Euler equation on $\mathbb T^2$ and, for $q\in(0,1)$, let $K_q(t)=\inf\{K\ge0:\sum_{|k|\le K}|\hat\omega(k,t)|^2\ge q\|\omega(t)\|_2^2\}$ be the radius carrying a fraction $q$ of the enstrophy.  For every $0<q<q'<1$ there are constants $C,c$ depending only on $q,q'$ such that $K_q(t)\le CK_{q'}(0)\exp(c\|\omega_0\|_\infty t)$ for all $t\ge0$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Super-exponential gradient sparsity]\label{al2}
For $\eta\in(0,1)$ let $G_\eta(t)=\{x:|\nabla\omega(x,t)|\ge\eta\|\nabla\omega(t)\|_\infty\}$.  If a smooth $2$D Euler solution has times $t_n\to\infty$ with $\log\|\nabla\omega(t_n)\|_\infty/t_n\to\infty$, then $|G_\eta(t_n)|\to0$ for every fixed $\eta\in(0,1)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Vanishing-viscosity global branch selection]\label{al3}
On $\mathbb T^2$ fix mean-zero vorticity and initial palinstrophy $P(\omega_0)=P_0>0$.  For viscosity $\nu>0$ and horizon $T$ let $D_{\nu,T}=\nu\int_0^T\|\nabla\omega(t)\|_2^2\,dt$ and $M_{\nu,T}$ its supremum under the palinstrophy constraint.  Fix $\tau\in(0,\infty)$, let $\nu_j\to0$ and $T_j\sqrt{P_0}\to\tau$, and take relative near-maximizers $D_{\nu_j,T_j}(\omega_{0,j})\ge(1-\varepsilon_j)M_{\nu_j,T_j}$ with $\varepsilon_j\to0$.  With $\ell_j=\|\omega_{0,j}\|_2/\|\nabla\omega_{0,j}\|_2$ and $W_j(y)=P_0^{-1/2}\omega_{0,j}(x_j+\ell_jy)$, every concentration limit is, after torus isometries, reflection, and sign, the same nontrivial profile $W_\tau$; and, when the normalized objectives are equicoercive, they $\Gamma$-converge at fixed $\tau$ to a variational problem whose maximizer is unique modulo these symmetries.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Enhanced dissipation and hypoelliptic mixing}

\begin{conjecture}[Reeb-graph spectral desert]\label{al4}
Let $H$ be a Morse Hamiltonian on a compact surface whose regular level sets form finitely many cells with quadratic saddles, and let $L_\nu=-\nabla^\perp H\cdot\nabla+\nu\Delta$ on mean-zero scalars.  In every fixed bounded window of imaginary frequency, as $\nu\to0$ the spectrum splits into slow eigenvalues $\lambda_j(\nu)=-\nu\mu_j+o(\nu)$, where $\mu_j$ are the eigenvalues of the effective weighted diffusion on the Reeb graph of $H$, and remaining eigenvalues with $\Re\lambda\le-c_H\nu^{1/2}$; in particular no eigenvalue branch decays at a rate $\nu^\alpha$ with $\tfrac12<\alpha<1$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[H\"ormander-step enhanced-dissipation universality]\label{al5}
Consider $\partial_tf+X_0f=-\nu\sum_{j=1}^rX_j^\ast X_jf$ on a compact manifold with smooth invariant volume, $X_0$ volume-preserving, in a one-drift equiregular H\"ormander class where the missing drift directions are generated by Lie words containing exactly one $X_0$ and repeated commutators with the diffusive fields.  Let $m$ be the maximal number of diffusive commutators needed to span the tangent space.  If there is no nonconstant common first integral, then $\operatorname{gap}(L_\nu)\asymp\nu^{m/(m+2)}$ as $\nu\to0$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Geometry and regularity of three-dimensional singularities}

\begin{conjecture}[Frenet-frame completion barrier]\label{al6}
Suppose a Type-I Euler singularity at $T$ is carried by a connected high-vorticity tube $R_t$ containing a maximum-vorticity point, with radius comparable to the length $L(t)$ of a $C^4$ central vortex-line segment $\gamma_t$ of uniform chord-arc constant and nonvanishing curvature, so that Frenet torsion $\tau$ and $\partial_s\tau$ are defined.  With $K(t)=\int\kappa\,ds$ and $\mathrm{Tor}(t)=\int L(t)|\partial_s\tau|/(1+L(t)|\tau|)\,ds$, one has $\limsup_{t\to T}[K(t)+\mathrm{Tor}(t)]=\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Type-I stretching coherence]\label{al7}
Let $\Omega(t)=\|\omega(t)\|_\infty$, $A_\lambda(t)=\{x:|\omega(x,t)|\ge\lambda\Omega(t)\}$, $\alpha=\xi\cdot S\xi$, and let $\bar\alpha_\lambda,V_\lambda$ be the $|\omega|^2$-weighted mean and standard deviation of $\alpha$ over $A_\lambda$.  If a Type-I singularity satisfies $0<c\le(T-t)\Omega(t)\le C$, then for every $\lambda$ sufficiently close to $1$ there is a sequence $t_n\to T$ with $(T-t_n)\bar\alpha_\lambda(t_n)\to1$ and $(T-t_n)V_\lambda(t_n)\to0$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Weak-Lorentz projected-Lamb regularity criterion]\label{al8}
Let $u$ be a Leray--Hopf solution of the $3$D Navier--Stokes equations on $\mathbb R^3$, or on $\mathbb T^3$ in the Galilean frame with mean velocity zero, let $\omega=\nabla\times u$, $P$ the Leray projector, and $\Lambda=P(u\times\omega)$ the projected Lamb vector.  If $\Lambda\in L^2((0,T);L^{3/2,\infty}_x)$, then $u$ is smooth on $(0,T]$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Band-limited kinematics and rapid rotation}

\begin{conjecture}[Helicity-defect law for stretching extremals]\label{al9}
Let $V_N$ be divergence-free mean-zero fields on $\mathbb T^3$ with Fourier support $|k|\le N$.  Fix admissible kinetic energy $K$ and enstrophy $E$, put relative helicity $\rho=H/(2\sqrt{KE})$, and let $M_N(\rho;K,E)=\sup|\int\omega\cdot S\omega|$ over $V_N$ at these constraints and helicity $\rho$.  Whenever $\rho$ and $0$ are both uniformly feasible as $N\to\infty$ and the enstrophy-to-energy ratio $E/K$ is bounded away from its first-shell minimum, so that zero-sum stretching triads persist and $M_N(0;K,E)>0$ uniformly, $M_N(\rho;K,E)/M_N(0;K,E)\to(1-\rho^2)^{3/2}$ locally uniformly for $\rho$ in compact subsets of $(-1,1)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Diophantine small-divisor rotation law]\label{al10}
For $K\ge1$ let $\delta(K)$ be the minimum nonzero inertial-wave triad detuning among triads with maximal wavenumber at most $K$ on an anisotropic torus, and assume the sharp arithmetic law $\delta(K)=K^{-\tau+o(1)}$.  For fixed data of finite Sobolev regularity $H^s$ whose Fourier coefficients obey a polynomial lower envelope $|\hat u(k)|\gtrsim|k|^{-s'}$ along the wavenumbers of the triads realizing $\delta(K_\ast)$ (a frequency-rich nondegeneracy, which also excludes a single inertial-wave mode, for which $(u\cdot\nabla)u\equiv0$; for $C^\infty$ data the super-polynomial Fourier decay empties this condition, so the lower envelope forces finite regularity and only the upper bound $E(\Omega,\nu)\lesssim\epsilon_\ast^{1+o(1)}$ survives without it) and $O(1)$ times let $E(\Omega,\nu)$ be the error between rotating $3$D Navier--Stokes at rotation $\Omega$ and its exact-resonant normal form in a fixed lower Sobolev norm, with a strictly positive data constant.  In the joint regime $K_\ast=(\Omega/\nu)^{1/(\tau+2)}\to\infty$ and $\epsilon_\ast=(\Omega^2\nu^\tau)^{-1/(\tau+2)}\to0$, one has $E(\Omega,\nu)=\epsilon_\ast^{1+o(1)}$ up to Sobolev-loss, subpower, and data constants, and the $\Omega$ power cannot be uniformly improved over tori realizing the same sharp $\tau$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Stratified and active-scalar singularities}

\begin{conjecture}[Critical-Richardson viscous log anomaly]\label{al11}
For a fixed Kelvin ray $(k,\eta)$, $k\ne0$, in the linearized equally viscous and diffusive $2$D Boussinesq system about Couette flow at Richardson number $\mathrm{Ri}=\tfrac14$, let $\hat\omega_{k,\eta}(t)$ be the vorticity amplitude along the sheared frequency $(k,\eta-kt)$.  For generic data with nonzero projection onto the repeated critical indicial mode, $\sup_{1\le t\le c(\nu k^2)^{-1/3}}|\hat\omega_{k,\eta}(t)|/(1+t)^{1/2}\sim C_{k,\eta}\log(1/\nu)$ as $\nu\to0$, with $C_{k,\eta}>0$; for every fixed $\mathrm{Ri}>\tfrac14$ the analogous normalized supremum is $O(1)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Generalized-SQG neck-curvature similarity law]\label{al12}
Use $u=\nabla^\perp\Lambda^{-2+\beta}\theta$ with $0<\beta\le1$.  Suppose a smooth generalized-SQG patch boundary develops a single symmetric self-similar neck singularity at $T$, with minimum opposing-boundary distance $d(t)$ and maximum curvature $\kappa_{\max}(t)$ in the same similarity core.  Then $d(t)\sim a_\beta(T-t)^{1/\beta}$, $\kappa_{\max}(t)\sim b_\beta(T-t)^{-1/\beta}$, and $d(t)\kappa_{\max}(t)\to c_\beta\in(0,\infty)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Boussinesq source-core co-location and alignment]\label{al13}
On $\mathbb T^2$ let $\theta^\circ=\theta-\langle\theta\rangle$, $\ell_\theta=\|\theta^\circ\|_\infty/\|\nabla\theta\|_\infty$, $A^\omega_\lambda=\{|\omega|\ge\lambda\|\omega\|_\infty\}$, and $A^\theta_\lambda=\{|\partial_x\theta|\ge\lambda\|\partial_x\theta\|_\infty\}$.  If a smooth inviscid Boussinesq solution develops an isolated finite-time singularity at $x_\ast$, then, intersecting both intense sets with a fixed ball isolating the core, for every $\lambda$ close to $1$ the distance between the localized sets divided by $\ell_\theta$ tends to zero; moreover, along a sequence in the core with distinct strain eigenvalues, $\nabla\theta$ aligns with the most compressive strain eigenvector.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Point-vortex collapse and non-Beltramization}

\begin{conjecture}[Minimal-admissible point-vortex collapse core]\label{al14}
Fix nonzero circulations for which at least one collapse-admissible subset exists---a subset being admissible if its isolated subsystem has a nontrivial self-similar finite-time collapse---and let $m_\ast$ be the least admissible cardinality.  Let $\mathcal C$ be the set of initial configurations undergoing a finite-time collision, with the resonance loci where two distinct minimal cores collapse simultaneously removed.  Then the union of collision strata whose fastest-collapsing core contains exactly $m_\ast$ vortices is relatively open and dense in $\mathcal C$, while strata with a strictly larger fastest core lie in positive-codimension substrata; and along every collision in this relatively generic set, similarity rescaling of the fastest core converges to an isolated self-similar $m_\ast$-vortex collapse with every spectator asymptotically farther than the core diameter.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[High-vorticity non-Beltramization barrier]\label{al15}
On $\mathbb R^3$ with finite energy, or on $\mathbb T^3$ in the zero-mean Galilean frame, let $C_\lambda(t)$ be the connected component of $\{|\omega|\ge\lambda\Omega(t)\}$ containing a maximum-vorticity point, and $B_\lambda(t)=\int_{C_\lambda}|u\times\omega|^2/\int_{C_\lambda}|u|^2|\omega|^2$ when the denominator is nonzero.  Any Type-I Euler singularity with $0<c\le(T-t)\Omega(t)\le C$ satisfies $\limsup_{t\to T}B_\lambda(t)>0$ for every $\lambda$ sufficiently close to $1$.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Metric geometry and spectral convexity in random conformal geometry}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The thirteen conjectures below complete the random-conformal-geometry bundle whose two exactly solved shape members---strict log-concavity of the DOZZ structure constant and of the Brownian-annulus modulus---are Conjectures~\ref{ak1} and~\ref{ak2} of Part~XX.  They fall in three groups: the metric geometry of Liouville quantum gravity (LQG), covering geodesic stars, the radial metric residual, metric-ball volume and boundary, space-filling SLE, and distance profiles (Conjectures~\ref{am1} to~\ref{am8}); the spectral convexity of Liouville conformal field theory, covering monotone likelihood ratio and log-concavity of sphere and torus blocks and DOZZ-weighted spectral densities (Conjectures~\ref{am9} to~\ref{am12}); and quantum-area nesting in conformal loop ensembles (Conjecture~\ref{am13}).  Each is a determinate, sharp, falsifiable law with a stated mechanism, a nearest-literature boundary, and a first decisive theorem.  The metric-geometry statements are continuum-limit laws (Hausdorff dimensions, scaling exponents, mixing and functional central limit theorems); the CFT statements are exact special-function shape properties whose descendant contributions are here tested only in the global (\(SL(2)\) or global-torus) block limit, a reduced-model check and a verification target.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{am:triangle}: the exact scaling triangle relating the boundary-length, roughness, and branching exponents, and the anchor reductions at the Brownian point \(\gamma=\sqrt{8/3}\).  Several statements are sharp interpolations of recognized open problems and attribute to the works whose anchors they extend.

Fix \(\gamma\in(0,2)\), write \(Q=\tfrac2\gamma+\tfrac\gamma2\), let \(h\) be a Gaussian free field variant, \(\mu_h\) the \(\gamma\)-LQG area measure, and \(D_h\) the \(\gamma\)-LQG metric, with Hausdorff dimension \(d_\gamma=\dim_{\mathrm H}(\mathbb C,D_h)\) and \(\xi=\gamma/d_\gamma\); the metric obeys Weyl scaling \(D_{h+c}=e^{\xi c}D_h\) and the area obeys \(\mu_{h+c}=e^{\gamma c}\mu_h\), and \(d_{\sqrt{8/3}}=4\).  For Liouville CFT write \(b>0\), \(Q_b=b+b^{-1}\), central charge \(c=1+6Q_b^2\), principal-series weights \(\Delta(P)=\tfrac{Q_b^2}4+P^2\), sphere OPE blocks \(\mathcal F_P(z)\) and torus blocks \(\mathcal F^{\mathrm{tor}}_{a,P}(q)\) in the standard normalizations, and \(C_b\) for the DOZZ structure constant of Part~XX.  Objects specific to a single conjecture are defined in its statement.

\begin{proposition}[Scaling triangle and Brownian anchors]\label{am:triangle}
Set \(p_\gamma=d_\gamma/2\), \(H_\gamma=2/d_\gamma\), and \(\alpha_\gamma=1+2/d_\gamma\).  Then \(p_\gamma=1/H_\gamma=1/(\alpha_\gamma-1)\), so a single scalar controls the metric boundary-length dimension, the distance-profile roughness exponent, and the perimeter branching index.  At the Brownian point \(d_\gamma=4\) these are \(2,\tfrac12,\tfrac32\), the known values.  The affine star spectrum \(s_k(\gamma)=2+\tfrac{3-k}2(d_\gamma-2)\) satisfies \(s_1=d_\gamma\), \(s_3=2\) for every \(d_\gamma\), and \(s_k=5-k\) at \(d_\gamma=4\); and \(s_k\to2\) as \(d_\gamma\downarrow2\).
\end{proposition}

\section{Metric geometry of Liouville quantum gravity}

\begin{conjecture}[Affine LQG geodesic-star spectrum]\label{am1}
For a point \(z\) call \(z\) a \(k\)-star point if there are \(r>0\) and unit-speed \(D_h\)-geodesics \(\eta_1,\dots,\eta_k\) from \(z\) with disjoint interiors on \((0,r]\); let \(S_k\) be the set of such points.  With \(s_k(\gamma)=2+\tfrac{3-k}2(d_\gamma-2)\): if \(s_k(\gamma)>0\) then almost surely \(\dim_{\mathrm H}^{D_h}S_k=s_k(\gamma)\); if \(s_k(\gamma)<0\) then \(S_k=\varnothing\) almost surely; at the threshold \(s_k=0\) the set has \(D_h\)-dimension at most zero.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Exponential scale-mixing of the radial metric residual]\label{am2}
Let \(h\) be a whole-plane GFF with \(h_1(0)=0\), \(h_r(0)\) its circle average, and \(X_t=\log[e^{\xi Qt-\xi h_{e^{-t}}(0)}D_h(0,\partial B(0,e^{-t}))]\), a process stationary in \(t\) by scale covariance.  Its strong-mixing coefficient satisfies \(\alpha_X(s)\le Ce^{-cs}\), and \(\sup_t\Pr[\sup_{0\le u\le1}|X_{t+u}|>x]\le Ce^{-cx}\); consequently \(\sup_{0\le u\le T}|X_u|=O(\log T)\) almost surely and the radial log-distance process has the Brownian functional scaling limit and law of the iterated logarithm.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Quantum-cone metric-ball shell functional CLT]\label{am3}
For a rooted LQG surface let \(V(r)=\mu_h(B_{D_h}(z,r))\) and \(Y_j^{(r)}=-\log[V(re^{-(j+1)})/V(re^{-j})]\).  There is a stationary inward-scale version \((Y_j^{\mathrm{cone}})\) for the rooted \(\gamma\)-quantum-cone tangent law that is exponentially \(\alpha\)-mixing with \(\mathbb E Y_0^{\mathrm{cone}}=d_\gamma\) and finite positive asymptotic variance \(\sigma_\gamma^2\), with \(\tfrac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{j<nt}(Y_j^{\mathrm{cone}}-d_\gamma)\Rightarrow\sigma_\gamma B_t\) in \(D([0,1])\); and for any finite-volume surface rooted at a quantum-typical point, \((Y_j^{(r)})_{j\in J}\Rightarrow(Y_j^{\mathrm{cone}})_{j\in J}\) as \(r\downarrow0\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Pointwise sfSLE metric H\"older exponent]\label{am4}
Let \(\eta\) be a whole-plane space-filling \(\mathrm{SLE}_{16/\gamma^2}\) independent of \(h\), parameterized by LQG area, \(\mu_h(\eta([s,t]))=t-s\).  For Lebesgue-almost every \(t\), almost surely \(\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\log\operatorname{diam}_{D_h}\eta([t-\varepsilon,t+\varepsilon])/\log\varepsilon=1/d_\gamma\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Stable-CSBP perimeter universality]\label{am5}
For every \(\gamma\in(0,2)\), filled metric balls in a whole-plane or quantum-cone \(\gamma\)-LQG surface admit a canonical scalar outer-boundary length \(L_h^\gamma(r)\), measurable from the metric-measure surface, with \(L_{h+c}^\gamma(e^{\xi c}r)=e^{\gamma c/2}L_h^\gamma(r)\).  Under reverse metric-radius exploration, after the natural normalization and excursion/Doob conditioning, the perimeter process is the conditioned/time-reversed stable continuous-state branching process of index \(\alpha_\gamma=1+2/d_\gamma\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Linear confluence response]\label{am6}
On a unit-area \(\gamma\)-LQG sphere sample \(z,x,y\) independently from \(\mu_h\).  On \(\{D_h(x,y)\le\varepsilon\}\) let \(b\) be the terminal point of the common initial segment of the geodesics from \(z\) to \(x,y\), \(\Delta=\max\{D_h(b,x),D_h(b,y)\}\), and \(C_\varepsilon=\Delta/D_h(x,y)\).  Conditioned on \(D_h(x,y)\le\varepsilon\), \(C_\varepsilon\) converges in law as \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) to a nondegenerate \(C_\gamma\in(0,\infty)\) with finite moments in a neighbourhood of zero.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Filled metric-ball outer boundary dimension]\label{am7}
Let \(h\) be a whole-plane GFF and \(B_{D_h}^{\infty,\bullet}(0,r)\) the filled metric ball targeted at infinity.  For every deterministic \(r>0\), almost surely \(\dim_{\mathrm H}^{D_h}\partial B_{D_h}^{\infty,\bullet}(0,r)=d_\gamma/2\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Fractional-Brownian tangent of distance profiles]\label{am8}
Let \(\mathcal D\) be a finite-boundary-length \(\gamma\)-LQG disk, \(z\) sampled from \(\mu_h\), and \(X(u)=D_h(z,\partial(u))\) the distance to the boundary point at length coordinate \(u\).  At boundary-length-almost every \(u_0\) there is \(\sigma_\gamma>0\) with \(\varepsilon^{-H_\gamma}[X(u_0+\varepsilon t)-X(u_0)]\Rightarrow\sigma_\gamma B_{H_\gamma}(t)\), \(H_\gamma=2/d_\gamma\), in local uniform topology, where \(B_H\) is two-sided fractional Brownian motion.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Spectral convexity in Liouville conformal field theory}

\begin{conjecture}[Sphere spectral monotone likelihood ratio]\label{am9}
Fix \(b>0\), four equal real external charges \(Q_b/3\le a<Q_b/2\), and \(0<z_1<z_2\le\tfrac12\).  The OPE-normalized sphere block \(\mathcal F_P(z)\) is nonzero on this domain and \(E=P^2\mapsto|\mathcal F_{\sqrt E}(z_2)|^2/|\mathcal F_{\sqrt E}(z_1)|^2\) is strictly increasing; equivalently \(\partial_E\partial_z\log|\mathcal F_{\sqrt E}(z)|^2>0\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Four-point spectral log-concavity]\label{am10}
For \(b>0\), \(Q_b/3\le a<Q_b/2\), \(0<z\le\tfrac12\), and \(P>0\), the principal-series magnitude \(I_z(P)=|C_b(a,a,\tfrac{Q_b}2+iP)|^2|\mathcal F_P(z)|^2\) is positive and \(\tfrac{d^2}{dP^2}\log I_z(P)<0\).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Torus thermal spectral monotone likelihood ratio]\label{am11}
Fix \(b>0\) and a real external charge \(Q_b/3\le a<Q_b/2\), and use the full torus block \(\mathcal F^{\mathrm{tor}}_{a,P}(q)=q^{\Delta(P)-c/24}\mathcal H^{\mathrm{tor}}_{a,P}(q)\) with \(q=e^{-2\pi\beta}\).  For \(\beta_2>\beta_1\) the blocks are nonzero for \(P>0\) and \(E=P^2\mapsto|\mathcal F^{\mathrm{tor}}_{a,\sqrt E}(q_2)|^2/|\mathcal F^{\mathrm{tor}}_{a,\sqrt E}(q_1)|^2\) is strictly decreasing.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Multipoint joint spectral log-concavity of the modulus]\label{am12}
For \(n\ge5\), real moduli \(\mathbf z\) in a positive OPE cell, equal physical external charges \(Q_b/3\le a<Q_b/2\), and internal momenta \(\mathbf P=(P_1,\dots,P_{n-3})\) on the principal series, let \(K_{\mathbf z}(\mathbf P)\) be the standard-normalized comb sewing kernel (product of DOZZ vertices and the multipoint block) and \(\Phi_{\mathbf z}(\mathbf P)=\log|K_{\mathbf z}(\mathbf P)|\).  Then \(K_{\mathbf z}\ne0\) and \(\nabla^2_{\mathbf P}\Phi_{\mathbf z}\prec0\) for every \(P_j>0\).
\end{conjecture}

\section{Nesting in conformal loop ensembles}

\begin{conjecture}[Quantum-cone nesting area-ratio functional CLT]\label{am13}
Let \(\kappa\in(8/3,4)\cup(4,8)\) with \(\gamma=\sqrt\kappa\) for \(\kappa<4\) and \(\gamma=4/\sqrt\kappa\) for \(\kappa>4\).  For nested \(\mathrm{CLE}_\kappa\) around the marked point of the matched \(\gamma\)-quantum-cone tangent law, with \(U_n\) the successive marked components, \(A_n=\mu_h(U_n)\), and \(Y_n=-\log(A_{n+1}/A_n)\): the sequence \((Y_n)\) admits a stationary version that is exponentially \(\alpha\)-mixing with analytic scaled cumulant generating function near zero and positive asymptotic variance, so \(\tfrac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{j<nt}(Y_j-v_{\gamma,\kappa})\Rightarrow\sigma_{\gamma,\kappa}B_t\) in \(D([0,1])\); and finite-volume deep-nesting increments converge to this stationary law.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Hydrodynamic limits of interacting particle systems}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The fifteen conjectures below propose hydrodynamic scaling limits for interacting particle systems whose microscopic rules carry a new structural ingredient: an endogenous local jump index, refreshed or fatigued environments, non-Markovian renewal clocks, singular reaction surfaces, finite crossing budgets, shared common noise, odd multi-species transport, and moving or storing membranes.  They fall in five groups (Conjectures~\ref{an1} to~\ref{an3}, \ref{an4} to~\ref{an6}, \ref{an7} to~\ref{an9}, \ref{an10} to~\ref{an12}, and~\ref{an13} to~\ref{an15}).  Each states a microscopic model, the conjectured limiting equation, its conserved quantities, the critical scaling that makes the new mechanism survive, and a route to disproof.  These are scaling-limit statements about continuum PDE or SPDE,, and every one; the diagnostics test event-count scalings, conservation, reduced linearizations, or exact ODE reductions, never the limit theorem.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{an:reductions}: exact mass conservation of the limiting equations, the fatigue mobility-budget identity, the perturbative self-arrest residual, and the critical event-count exponents.  Four statements have explicit high overlaps with prior work, since a closely related continuum object or one-particle input appeared in 2025--2026 work: the Markov-switching Robin equation of Colantoni~\cite{Colantoni26} (Conjecture~\ref{an10}), the scalar vorticity/odd-diffusion exclusion of De Carlo, Gabrielli, and Gon\c calves~\cite{DeCarloGabrielliGoncalves25} (Conjecture~\ref{an11}), the critical-percolation random-walk scaling limit of Dankovic, Markering, Miller, and Yuan~\cite{DankovicMMY26} (Conjecture~\ref{an13}), and the finite-reservoir Wentzell theorem of Franco, Franco, and Gon\c calves~\cite{FrancoFrancoGoncalves26} (Conjecture~\ref{an15}); for these the novel content is the exact microscopic derivation and the specific interior/two-sided/multi-species structure, not the limiting object itself.

Throughout, $\eta$ is an exclusion configuration on the discrete torus $\mathbb T_N$ or on $\mathbb R$, $\rho$ the limiting density, and time is diffusively scaled unless stated otherwise.  Objects specific to a single conjecture are defined in its statement.

\begin{proposition}[Conservation, mobility budget, self-arrest residual, and critical counts]\label{an:reductions}
{\rm(i)} Each limiting equation displayed below conserves the relevant total mass (for multi-species models, each color mass): integrating the gain--loss or divergence form and interchanging dummy variables cancels the flux.  {\rm(ii)} For the fatigue ODE $\dot a=c(1-a)^p$ with $c=2\rho(1-\rho)>0$, the conductance reaches zero in finite time iff $p<1$, with $t_{\rm hit}=1/(c(1-p))$, while the integrated mobility $\int_0^\infty(1-a)^p\,dt=1/c$ is finite for every $p$.  {\rm(iii)} For the occupation-deepened trap system linearized about $\rho=\bar\rho$ with $b=0$, the first-order perturbation solves $\partial_tv=e^{-a\bar\rho t}\Delta v$, so $v(t)=e^{\tau(t)\Delta}v_0$ with $\tau(t)=(1-e^{-a\bar\rho t})/(a\bar\rho)$ and $v_\infty=e^{\Delta/(a\bar\rho)}v_0$, generally nonzero.  {\rm(iv)} A codimension-one reaction surface at generator rate $N^\theta$ contributes at order $N^{\theta-1}$, critical at $\theta=1$; and a membrane of step $N^a$, move rate $N^{-b}$, and slow conductance $\alpha/N$ has order-one Brownian motion, healing length, and slow activity per dwell simultaneously iff $b=2a$ and $a=\tfrac12$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Density-adaptive and refreshed exclusion hydrodynamics}

\begin{conjecture}[Density-adaptive L\'evy exclusion]\label{an1}
On $\mathbb T_N$ let $r_x^N(\eta)$ be the mean occupation in a block of radius $\ell_N$ with $\ell_N\to\infty$, $\ell_N/N\to0$, and $\log N/\sqrt{\ell_N}\to0$, and let $\alpha:[0,1]\to[\alpha_-,\alpha_+]\subset(0,2)$ be $C^1$.  A particle at $x$ jumps to a vacant $y$ at rate $N^{\alpha(r_x^N)}c(\alpha(r_x^N))|y-x|_N^{-1-\alpha(r_x^N)}$.  For smooth profiles bounded away from $0,1$ the empirical density converges to the conservative variable-order gain--loss equation
\[
\partial_t\rho(u)=\mathrm{PV}\!\int_{\mathbb T}\bigl[K_{\alpha(\rho(v))}(u-v)\rho(v)(1-\rho(u))-K_{\alpha(\rho(u))}(v-u)\rho(u)(1-\rho(v))\bigr]\,dv.
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Refreshing heavy traps: crossover trichotomy]\label{an2}
Start from a reversible heavy-trap exclusion model with static anomalous clock $\theta_N$ and index $\beta\in(0,1)$, refresh each trap at rate $q_N$ independently of the particles, and set $\lambda_N=q_N\theta_N$.  If $\lambda_N\to0$ the static fractional-kinetics limit persists; if $\lambda_N\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)$ the hydrodynamics is governed by a model-dependent renewal/Bernstein symbol $\psi_\lambda(s)$ with $\psi_0(s)=cs^\beta$ and $\psi_\lambda(s)\sim m_\lambda s$ as $s\downarrow0$; if $\lambda_N\to\infty$ the limit is ordinary diffusion after effective-diffusivity renormalization.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Critical sparse shortcuts add a teleportation operator]\label{an3}
Let nearest-neighbor SSEP run on $\mathbb T_N$ at rate $N^2$, and add $M_N$ macroscopic shortcut edges ringing at rate $\kappa$, with $M_N/N\to\lambda/2$ and endpoint-pair measure converging to uniform.  The empirical density converges to $\partial_t\rho=D\partial_{uu}\rho+\kappa\lambda(\bar\rho-\rho)$, with $\bar\rho=\int_{\mathbb T}\rho$; for a nonuniform endpoint kernel the nonlocal term becomes the corresponding conservative jump operator.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Renewal exclusion, fatigue, and boundary memory}

\begin{conjecture}[Crowding-renormalized successful-reset renewal exclusion]\label{an4}
Let a particle of age $a$ attempt jumps with hazard $\beta/(a+a_0)$, $0<\beta<1$, resetting its age only after a successful jump.  In dimension $d\ge2$, linearizing about a homogeneous equilibrium $\rho_*$, on the clock $t_{N,*}=N^{2/\gamma(\rho_*)}$ with $\gamma(\rho_*)=\beta(1-\rho_*)$ the density perturbation converges to the fractional diffusion $\partial_t^{\gamma(\rho_*)}u=D(\rho_*)\Delta u$; a genuinely nonconstant profile admits no single global polynomial clock, requiring a variable-order-in-time renewal kernel.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Activity-fatigued conductances]\label{an5}
Attach damage $a_b(t)\in[0,1]$ to each bond, exchanging symmetrically at rate $N^2(1-a_b)^p$, with $a_b$ increased by $N^{-2}$ at each successful crossing (a crossing budget of order $N^2$).  Writing $g(a)=(1-a)^p$, the damage field then evolves macroscopically as $\partial_ta=2g(a)\rho(1-\rho)$, this law derived from the increment rule rather than imposed.  The coupled hydrodynamic limit is $\partial_t\rho=\partial_u(g(a)\partial_u\rho)$, $\partial_ta=2g(a)\rho(1-\rho)$, with finite-time loss of ellipticity at $p<1$ but finite integrated mobility for every $p$, so some smooth nonconstant data retain nonconstant long-time limits.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Common heavy-tailed boundary clock: random Stieltjes boundary law]\label{an6}
Let a single heavy-tailed renewal process control all left-boundary updates, scaled so that $N^{-1}R_N(N^2t)\Rightarrow\kappa_LE_t^L$ with $E^L$ an inverse-$\gamma$-stable clock, and analogously on the right.  The bulk solves $\partial_t\rho=\partial_{uu}\rho$, but the boundary fluxes are singular random Stieltjes measures: in weak form $d\langle\rho_t,G\rangle=\langle\rho_t,G''\rangle dt+\kappa_LG(0)[\alpha-\rho_t(0)]dE_t^L+\kappa_RG(1)[\beta_R-\rho_t(1)]dE_t^R$, remaining random after averaging over the clocks.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Catalysts, mixed clocks, and crossing budgets}

\begin{conjecture}[Codimension-one nonlinear catalyst transmission law]\label{an7}
Let $\Sigma_N$ approximate a smooth hypersurface $\Sigma\subset\mathbb T^d$ with bulk SSEP at rate $N^2$ and catalyst birth/death flips at generator rate $N^\theta$ with reaction $R(r)=b(r)(1-r)-d(r)r$.  The critical exponent is $\theta=1$, at which $\partial_t\rho=\Delta\rho$ off $\Sigma$ with continuous trace and derivative jump $[\partial_n\rho]_\Sigma=-R(\rho|_\Sigma)$; for $\theta<1$ the catalyst disappears and for $\theta>1$ a stable zero of $R$ pins the trace.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Mixed-clock two-species Maxwell--Stefan hydrodynamics]\label{an8}
Let species A use exponential attempt clocks and species B use i.i.d.\ heavy-tailed attempt clocks of index $\gamma\in(0,1)$, reset after every attempt.  Observed on time $N^{2/\gamma}$ with A's microscopic rate $N^{2-2/\gamma}$, both species stay hydrodynamically visible, and around a homogeneous composition the linearized law is the mixed-order Maxwell--Stefan system $s\,\widehat{\delta a}-\delta a_0=-k^2(D_*\widehat{\delta}\,)_A$, $s^\gamma\widehat{\delta b}-s^{\gamma-1}\delta b_0=-k^2(D_*\widehat{\delta}\,)_B$ with non-diagonal exclusion cross-diffusion $D_*$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Finite crossing-budget membrane: endogenous stopping-time transition]\label{an9}
Give a critical slow bond (microscopic rate $\alpha/N$) a budget $B_N=\lfloor\kappa N\rfloor$ of successful exchanges, after which its rate is zero forever.  With unsigned local activity $A(r_-,r_+)=r_-(1-r_+)+r_+(1-r_-)$, the normalized success count satisfies $S_N(t)/N\to\alpha\int_0^tA(\rho(s,0^-),\rho(s,0^+))\,ds$, and the density obeys the critical Robin transmission law before the exhaustion time $\tau=\inf\{t:\alpha\int_0^tA\ge\kappa\}$ and reflecting conditions after, so the PDE changes type at an endogenous stopping time.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Common noise, odd transport, and self-generated arrest}

\begin{conjecture}[Telegraph-modulated critical membrane]\label{an10}
Let a single two-state Markov chain $Z_t\in\{0,1\}$, converging on diffusive time to a finite-rate telegraph process, multiply a critical slow bond's rate, $\alpha Z_t/N$.  Conditional on $Z$, the density converges to the heat equation with time-dependent Robin coefficient $\alpha Z_t$, so the empirical density converges jointly with $Z$ to a random hydrodynamic limit; the fast-switch regime converges to the averaged Robin law.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Multi-species chiral-vorticity odd cross-diffusion]\label{an11}
Use at least two conserved colors plus vacancies, with symmetric color-vacancy exchanges and plaquette rotations at rates $c_\pm(\xi)=N^2c_0[1\pm\varepsilon\Phi(\mathcal N(\xi))]$ depending only on the rotation-invariant plaquette composition $\mathcal N(\xi)$, so grand-canonical product color measures are invariant.  The diffusive hydrodynamics is $\partial_tr_i=\nabla\cdot\sum_j[D^S_{ij}(r)\nabla r_j+D^O_{ij}(r)R\nabla r_j]$ with $R$ rotation by $\pi/2$ and a composition-dependent odd cross matrix $D^O$ that changes sign with $\varepsilon$ and affects individual density equations.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Perturbative self-generated arrest]\label{an12}
Attach trap depth $E_x(t)\ge0$ with symmetric bond rate $N^2\exp[-(E_x+E_{x+1})/2]$ and $dE_x/dt=a\eta_t(x)-bE_x$.  The finite-time limit is $\partial_t\rho=\partial_u(e^{-e}\partial_u\rho)$, $\partial_te=a\rho-be$; with $b=0$, linearizing about $\rho=\bar\rho$ gives residual $v_\infty=e^{\Delta/(a\bar\rho)}v_0$, so for small smooth mean-zero data the full nonlinear system converges to the nonconstant limit $\rho_\infty=\bar\rho+\epsilon e^{\Delta/(a\bar\rho)}v_0+O(\epsilon^2)$.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Critical geometry and moving membranes}

\begin{conjecture}[Critical-percolation exclusion transfer]\label{an13}
Let $(V_N,c_N,\mu_N)$ be the rescaled critical triangular-percolation graph sequence for which simple random walk converges quenched to the semigroup $P_t^\omega$ of CLE$_6$ Brownian motion on the limiting resistance space, with time normalization $a_N$.  Run SSEP on $V_N$ with the same conductances and time change.  If the initial color field converges to $\rho_0\mu^\omega$, then quenched $\pi_t^N\Rightarrow\rho_t\mu^\omega$ with $\rho_t=P_t^\omega\rho_0$, so exclusion produces no additional transport exponent beyond the one-particle normalization.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Critically mobile slow membrane]\label{an14}
Let a distinguished slow bond of rate $\alpha/N$ move by microscopic distance $N^a$ at rate $N^{-b}$.  At the uniquely matched scaling $a=\tfrac12$, $b=1$ (step $\sqrt N$, move rate $N^{-1}$, conductance $\alpha/N$), the membrane path converges to Brownian motion and the density converges to a nontrivial random singular-interface heat flow conditional on that Brownian path; the membrane is not annealed away, and the exact moving-Robin generator is a sub-conjecture.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Interior two-sided capacitor membrane]\label{an15}
Split a one-dimensional system into left and right SSEP bulks and an interior storage node of capacity $\lfloor\kappa N\rfloor$, conserving total mass, with side-to-storage rates of order $N$.  The limit is $\mu_t=\rho_-du+\rho_+du+m(t)\delta_0$ with heat equations in the bulks, nonlinear vertex fluxes $F_\pm(r,m)=a_\pm r(1-m/\kappa)-b_\pm(m/\kappa)(1-r)$, boundary conditions $-\partial_{n_\pm}\rho_\pm=F_\pm$, and $\dot m=F_-+F_+$, conserving $\int\rho_-+\int\rho_++m$.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Fixed-traffic circulation laws for nonequilibrium Markov chains}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 22 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The four conjectures below concern a controlled family of finite-state continuous-time Markov chains---fixed-traffic circulation rays---in which the stationary law, every undirected stationary edge traffic, and every escape rate are held fixed while an irreversible stationary circulation of strength $\alpha$ is increased.  The ray therefore varies time-reversal asymmetry without touching the equilibrium landscape or the local activity.  Two conjectures concern visited-set statistics (Conjectures~\ref{ao1}, \ref{ao3}, \ref{ao4}) and one the spectral geometry of the generator (Conjecture~\ref{ao2}).  Each is a determinate law with a stated mechanism, a nearest-literature boundary, a first decisive theorem, and a route to disproof.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{ao:basic}: the endpoint spectral bounds, the exact even-analytic dependence of visited-set laws on $\alpha$, and an explicit high-precision counterexample showing that the natural stronger companion---whole-ray monotonicity of the fast real spectral edge---is \emph{false}, so only the endpoint bound survives there.  Conjecture~\ref{ao2} is the strongest of the four; its own stronger global-unimodality form is also false and is recorded as a control.  The visited-set laws are exact finite-state statements, tested here by exact augmented-chain propagation rather than trajectory sampling, but with the alternating-derivative hierarchy of Conjecture~\ref{ao1} checked only to finite order.

Let the state space be $\{1,\dots,n\}$ with stationary law $\pi>0$.  For each undirected edge fix a symmetric traffic $T_{ij}=T_{ji}>0$ and an antisymmetric divergence-free current $J_{ij}=-J_{ji}$, $\sum_jJ_{ij}=0$, with $|J_{ij}|<T_{ij}$.  The circulation ray has off-diagonal rates $L_{\alpha,ij}=(T_{ij}+\alpha J_{ij})/(2\pi_i)$, $0\le\alpha\le1$, and zero row sums; then $\pi$ is stationary, the undirected traffic $q_{ij}(\alpha)+q_{ji}(\alpha)=T_{ij}$ and the escape rate $r_i=\tfrac1{2\pi_i}\sum_jT_{ij}$ are $\alpha$-independent.  Writing $H_\alpha=-D^{1/2}L_\alpha D^{-1/2}$ with $D=\operatorname{diag}(\pi)$ and restricting off the stationary mode gives $G_\alpha=K+\alpha C$ with $K=K^\ast\succ0$ and $C^\ast=-C$.  Let $h(\alpha)=\max\{\Re\lambda:\lambda\in\sigma(-L_\alpha)\setminus\{0\}\}$ and $g(\alpha)=\min\{\Re\lambda:\lambda\in\sigma(-L_\alpha)\setminus\{0\}\}$ be the fast and slow real spectral edges.  Let $V_t=\{X_s:0\le s\le t\}$ be the visited set, $w_i^{(\gamma)}=\pi_i^\gamma/\sum_j\pi_j^\gamma$ escort weights, and $U_{\gamma,t}=\sum_iw_i^{(\gamma)}\mathbf 1_{\{\tau_i>t\}}$ the unexplored escort mass, with $x=\alpha^2$.

\begin{proposition}[Endpoint bounds, even dependence, and the failure of fast-edge ray monotonicity]\label{ao:basic}
For every ray, $\Re\lambda(G_\alpha)\in[\lambda_{\min}(K),\lambda_{\max}(K)]$, so $h(\alpha)\le h(0)=\lambda_{\max}(K)$ and $g(\alpha)\ge g(0)=\lambda_{\min}(K)$.  Under a stationary start, path reversal maps the law of $L_\alpha$ to that of $L_{-\alpha}$ and fixes every visited-set statistic, so $V_t^{(\alpha)}\overset d=V_t^{(-\alpha)}$ and all finite-state visited-set probabilities are even analytic functions of $\alpha$, whence $x=\alpha^2$ is the natural variable.  The endpoint bound does \emph{not} extend to whole-ray monotonicity of the fast edge: there is a bidirected six-state ray on which $h(\alpha)$ strictly increases over an interior interval by more than $0.6$, verified to $40$ digits, so the companion statement $h(\beta)\le h(\alpha)$ for $\alpha\le\beta$ is false.
\end{proposition}

\section{Visited-set and spectral circulation laws}

\begin{conjecture}[Escort unexplored-mass alternating-derivative hierarchy]\label{ao1}
For every integer $k\ge1$, integer $r\ge0$, time $t\ge0$, and $0\le\gamma\le1$, with $x=\alpha^2$,
\[
(-1)^r\frac{d^r}{dx^r}\,\mathbb E_{\sqrt x}\!\bigl[U_{\gamma,t}^{\,k}\bigr]\ge0
\]
throughout the admissible physical interval.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[One-maximum principle for the slow spectral edge]\label{ao2}
The slow real spectral edge $g(\alpha)$ has at most one strict interior local maximum on $(0,1)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Unicyclic range stochastic acceleration]\label{ao3}
For every bidirected chain whose undirected support is connected of cycle rank one, with $X_0\sim\pi$ and $R_t=|V_t|$: for every $m$, $t\ge0$, and $0\le\alpha\le\beta\le1$,
\[
\Pr_\beta(R_t\ge m)\ge\Pr_\alpha(R_t\ge m).
\]
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Pure-cycle stationary-mass coverage acceleration]\label{ao4}
For a simple-cycle support with $X_0\sim\pi$ and $M_t=\pi(V_t)$: for every $z\in[0,1]$, $t\ge0$, and $0\le\alpha\le\beta\le1$,
\[
\Pr_\beta(M_t\ge z)\ge\Pr_\alpha(M_t\ge z).
\]
\end{conjecture}


\part{Cross-shadow universality}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 24 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The five conjectures below share one principle: two limiting observables read off the same discrete randomness decouple because they probe distinct resolution, frequency, or representation-theoretic sectors.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{ap:basic}.

\begin{proposition}[Cross-shadow anchor identities]\label{ap:basic}
{\rm(i)} For an integral matrix $A_n$ with $\det A_n\ne0$, $\Z^n/A_n\Z^n$ is finite of order $|\det A_n|$.  {\rm(ii)} For a conjugation-invariant word measure $\mu_{w,N}$ on $S_N$, $\chi^2(\mu_{w,N}\Vert U_N)=\sum_{\rho\ne\mathbf1}|\E_w\chi_\rho|^2$.  {\rm(iii)} Every $\Q_p$ root of a Littlewood polynomial is a $p$-adic unit.  {\rm(iv)} The $\mathbb F_p$ singularity probability of a balanced random matrix is $c_p=1-\prod_{j\ge1}(1-p^{-j})=1/p+O(p^{-2})$, so $c_p-1/p$ is summable.  {\rm(v)} $t^{-1/2}|\operatorname{core}_e(\lambda_t)|$ and $(4/\pi)\Gamma_e^{\mathrm{Ros}}$ differ only by the factor $\pi/4$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
{\rm(i)} Smith normal form $A_n=U\operatorname{diag}(d_i)V$, $U,V\in\mathrm{GL}_n(\Z)$, gives $\Z^n/A_n\Z^n\cong\bigoplus\Z/d_i$ of order $\prod|d_i|=|\det A_n|$.  {\rm(ii)} With $f=N!\mu_{w,N}$, expanding $f-1$ in the orthonormal character basis gives coefficient $\E_w\chi_\rho$ at $\rho\ne\mathbf1$, and Parseval gives the identity.  {\rm(iii)} If $|x|_p<1$ the unit constant term dominates and if $|x|_p>1$ the leading term dominates, so any root has $|x|_p=1$.  {\rm(iv)} Expand the product.  {\rm(v)} Rostam's definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Local spectral--Smith factorization]\label{ap1}
For a symmetric integral Wigner matrix $A_n$ (centered entries, edge-universality moments, odd-prime anti-concentration) with rescaled real edge process $\mathcal E_n$ and $p$-primary paired cokernel $C_{n,p}$ (a cemetery state when $\det A_n=0$), and every fixed finite set $P$ of odd primes, $(\mathcal E_n,(C_{n,p})_{p\in P})\Rightarrow\mathcal E_{\mathrm{GOE}}\otimes\bigotimes_{p\in P}\mu_p^{\mathrm{sym}}$, and likewise with any fixed finite collection of microscopic bulk and edge windows; the $p=2$ analogue is a secondary statement.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Random-integer multiplicative universality of determinants]\label{ap2}
For $B_n$ i.i.d.\ Bernoulli-$\tfrac12$ in $\{0,1\}$ and $D_n=|\det B_n|\ge3$, $(\omega(D_n)-\log\log D_n)/\sqrt{\log\log D_n}\Rightarrow N(0,1)$ and $(\log Q_{i,n}/\log D_n)_i\Rightarrow PD(1)$ in the ranked simplex.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Plancherel modular-charge/Airy factorization]\label{ap3}
For poissonized Plancherel $\lambda_t$, fixed $e\ge2$, Rostam-normalized charge $Y_t^{(e)}$, and upper-edge process $\mathcal A_t$, $(Y_t^{(e)},\mathcal A_t)\Rightarrow G_e\otimes\mathcal A_{\mathrm{GUE}}$; consequently $(\tfrac{\pi}{4\sqrt t}|\operatorname{core}_e(\lambda_t)|,t^{-1/6}(\lambda_{t,1}-2\sqrt t))\Rightarrow\Gamma_e^{\mathrm{Ros}}\otimes F_2$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Stable-spectrum saturation of word measures]\label{ap4}
For $w\in F_r$ with primitive abelianization but non-primitive in $F_r$, with $\beta_{\mathrm{st}}(w)=\inf_{\lambda\ne\varnothing}\liminf_N\frac{-\log|\E_w\chi_{\lambda[N]}|}{\log N}$ and $N^{-\infty+o(1)}$ read as smaller than every power of $N$, $\chi^2(\mu_{w,N}\Vert U_N)=N^{-2\beta_{\mathrm{st}}(w)+o(1)}$ and $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mu_{w,N}\Vert U_N)=N^{-2\beta_{\mathrm{st}}(w)+o(1)}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Adelic root-process factorization]\label{ap5}
For a random Littlewood polynomial $F_n$ with real near-unit root process $\mathcal R_n$ and $p$-adic root process $\mathcal Z_{p,n}$ on $\Z_p^\times$, and every fixed finite set $P$ of odd primes, $(\mathcal R_n,(\mathcal Z_{p,n})_{p\in P})\Rightarrow\nu_\infty\otimes\bigotimes_{p\in P}\nu_p$; the prime $2$ is excluded.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Entropy and rare-event laws for random algebraic and topological structures}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 29 August 2026.}\par\medskip

The five conjectures below place deep algebraic and topological invariants on canonical random ensembles and assert entropy, threshold, and rare-event laws.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{aq:basic}.

\begin{proposition}[Anchor identities and reductions]\label{aq:basic}
{\rm(i)} At a two-face tropical breakpoint of $P_{\beta,N}(z)=\sum_ke^{\beta a_k}z^kG_{k,N}$, after rescaling, every inactive coefficient carries a factor $e^{-\beta\Delta_k}\le e^{-\beta\delta}$, and the zeros of the dominant pencil $G_{r,N}+\zeta^mG_{s,N}$ are the $m$-th-root lift of the complex spherical ensemble.  {\rm(ii)} Matroid Kazhdan--Lusztig polynomials are multiplicative under direct sums, forests give $P\equiv1$, and the cycle $C_k$ contributes $U_{k-1,k}$.  {\rm(iii)} For a diagram with $c$ crossings on $m$ strands, $\log Z(u,v)=\log\sum_{i,j}\dim_{\mathbb F_2}Kh^{i,j}e^{ui+vj}$ is convex and bounded by $C(c+m)(1+|u|+|v|)$.  {\rm(iv)} The Weil--Petersson short-geodesic Poisson mean is $\Lambda(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon^2/4+O(\varepsilon^4)$; for each fixed $k$, $\varepsilon^{-2k}\Pr(\ell_k^{\rm hom}\le\varepsilon)\to1/(4^kk!)$ in the large-genus then shrinking-window limit; and $\pi\,\mathrm{cap}(\ell)/\ell\to1$.  {\rm(v)} For a rational homology sphere, $\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_2}\widehat{HF}(Y)\ge|H_1(Y;\Z)|$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
{\rm(i)} Divide by $e^{\beta\Phi(x_*)}$; $\det(G_r+\zeta^mG_s)=\det(G_s)\prod_i(\zeta^m-\lambda_i)$ with $\lambda_i\in\operatorname{spec}(-G_s^{-1}G_r)$, the (rotation-invariant) spherical ensemble.  {\rm(ii)} Multiplicativity and $P_{B_n}\equiv1$ are Elias--Proudfoot--Wakefield; a bridge is a coloop and the block decomposition is classical.  {\rm(iii)} Log-sum-exp convexity; the resolution cube has $2^c$ vertices with at most $c+m$ circles each, and gradings are $O(c+m)$.  {\rm(iv)} $\lambda(\ell)=\sum_{r\ge1}\ell^{2r-1}/(2r)!$ integrates to $\varepsilon^2/4+\varepsilon^4/96+\cdots$; Mirzakhani--Petri give the Poisson law, and each fixed short multicurve has connected complement asymptotically almost surely, so its classes are homologically independent and the $k$th tail is asymptotic to $\Lambda(\varepsilon)^k/k!$.  {\rm(v)} $\widehat{HF}$ splits over $|H_1|$ $\mathrm{Spin}^c$ structures, each summand of Euler characteristic $\pm1$, hence nonzero over $\mathbb F_2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Double-scaling tropical shadowing]\label{aq1}
In the setting of Proposition~\ref{aq:basic}(i), if $\beta_N\delta/\log N\to\infty$, then on every compact annular sector within one $m$-th-root branch, after removing a boundary layer of vanishing limiting intensity, the zeros of the full matrix polynomial match the zeros of $G_{r,N}+\zeta^mG_{s,N}$ with maximal displacement $o_{\Pp}(N^{-1/2})$; hence the unfolded selected zero process has the spherical-ensemble branch-lift local limit.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Kazhdan--Lusztig entropy percolation]\label{aq2}
For $G_n\sim G(n,c/n)$ and $H_n(c)=n^{-1}\log P_{M(G_n)}(1)$, there is a deterministic $H_{\mathrm{KL}}\colon(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ with $H_n(c)\to H_{\mathrm{KL}}(c)$ in probability for every fixed $c\ne1$, $H_{\mathrm{KL}}(c)=0$ for $0<c<1$, $H_{\mathrm{KL}}(c)>0$ for $c>1$, and $H_n(1)\to0$ in probability.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Khovanov free energy of random braid closures]\label{aq3}
For $m\ge3$ and a finitely supported $\mu$ on $B_m$, aperiodic on the symmetric-group quotient and with nonelementary image in $B_m/Z(B_m)$ acting on the $m$-punctured disc, let $L_n$ be the closure of $\beta_n=X_1\cdots X_n$ and $Z_n(u,v)=\sum_{i,j}\dim_{\mathbb F_2}Kh^{i,j}(L_n;\mathbb F_2)e^{ui+vj}$ in normalized oriented bigradings.  For every fixed $(u,v)$ there is a deterministic finite $F_\mu(u,v)$ with $n^{-1}\log Z_n(u,v)\to F_\mu(u,v)$ almost surely and in $L^1$, and $F_\mu$ is convex.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Two-sided thin-handle Hodge successive-minima equivalence]\label{aq4}
For Weil--Petersson random $X_g$, let $m_j(J(X_g))$ be the Hodge successive minima and define
\[
\ell_j^{\rm hom}(X_g)=\inf\!\left\{L:\operatorname{rank}_{\Z}
\left\langle[\gamma]:\gamma\text{ primitive nonseparating simple closed
geodesic},\ \ell(\gamma)\le L\right\rangle\ge j\right\}.
\]
For every fixed $k\ge1$ and every $\eta>0$,
\[
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\lim_{g\to\infty}\Pp\!\left(
\max_{1\le j\le k}\left|\frac{\pi m_j(J(X_g))^2}{\ell_j^{\rm hom}(X_g)}-1\right|>\eta\
\middle|\ \min\{\pi m_k(J(X_g))^2,\ell_k^{\rm hom}(X_g)\}\le\varepsilon\right)=0,
\]
and consequently
$\varepsilon^{-2k}\Pp(\pi m_k(J(X_g))^2\le\varepsilon)\to1/(4^kk!)$
in the same sequential limit.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Floer cancellation saturation for random Heegaard splittings]\label{aq5}
For $g\ge2$ and finitely supported $\mu$ generating the genus-$g$ mapping class group as a semigroup, let $Y_n=H_g\cup_{\phi_n}H_g$ with $\phi_n=X_1\cdots X_n$ and $Q_n=\{b_1(Y_n)=0\}$.  Conditional on $Q_n$, the excess Floer entropy $E_n=\log\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_2}\widehat{HF}(Y_n)-\log|H_1(Y_n;\Z)|\ge0$ satisfies $E_n/n\to0$ in probability; equivalently, whenever the homological torsion exponent $\alpha_\mu$ exists in probability on $Q_n$, $n^{-1}\log\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_2}\widehat{HF}(Y_n)\to\alpha_\mu$.
\end{conjecture}


\part{Random Hamiltonian cocycle entropy}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 30 August 2026.}\par\medskip

\begin{proposition}[Deterministic and lazy-walk calibrations]\label{ar:basic}
{\rm(i)} For a point-mass driving law $\delta_\phi$ on the Hamiltonian
diffeomorphisms of a closed symplectic surface, all-class finite-bar barcode
entropy equals $h_{\mathrm{top}}(\phi)$.  {\rm(ii)} For
$\mu=(1-p)\delta_{\mathrm{id}}+p\delta_\phi$, both quenched random topological
entropy and all-class finite-bar entropy equal
$p\,h_{\mathrm{top}}(\phi)$ almost surely.
\end{proposition}

\begin{conjecture}[Quenched random Floer barcode entropy]\label{ar1}
Let $(\Sigma,\omega)$ be a closed symplectic surface, let $\mu$ be finitely
supported on $\operatorname{Ham}(\Sigma,\omega)$, and put
$\Phi_n=X_n\circ\cdots\circ X_1$ for i.i.d.\ $X_i\sim\mu$.  If
$b_\varepsilon^{\rm fin}(\psi)$ counts bars of finite length greater than
$\varepsilon$ in the unpinned absolute fixed-point Floer barcode over
$\mathbb F_2$, summed over every free homotopy class and extended to
degenerate maps by the lower-semicontinuous $C^\infty$ perturbation liminf,
and $h_{\mathrm{top}}^{\mathrm q}(\mu)$ is the quenched random topological
entropy defined by the sample-path Bowen metric, then almost surely
\[
 \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}
 \frac1n\log\max\{1,b_\varepsilon^{\rm fin}(\Phi_n)\}
 =h_{\mathrm{top}}^{\mathrm q}(\mu).
\]
\end{conjecture}


\input{part_xxviii_skeleton}

\part{Arithmetic games, prime continuants and Hessian shadows}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 2 September 2026.}\par\medskip

This part states fifteen conjectures on prime continuants of typical real numbers, Hausdorff dimensions of continued-fraction sets, Legendre-symbol and random Toeplitz and Hankel games, and plane quartics with their Hessians and duals over finite fields.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{at:basic}.
Throughout, $x\in(0,1)$ is irrational with continued fraction
$x=[0;a_1,a_2,\dots]$, convergents $p_n/q_n$, and $\mu$ is Lebesgue
measure; for an odd prime $p$, $\chi_p$ is the Legendre symbol and we write
$m=(p-1)/2$,
\[
 H_p=\bigl(\chi_p(i+j)\bigr)_{1\le i,j\le m},\qquad
 T_p=\bigl(\chi_p(i-j)\bigr)_{1\le i,j\le m},
\]
and $v(A)$ for the value of the zero-sum game with payoff matrix $A$ to the
row player, both players using mixed strategies.  For $p\equiv1\pmod4$,
$\eps_p>1$ denotes the fundamental unit of $\Q(\sqrt p)$; it has norm $-1$.
The Dedekind function is $\Psi(k)=k\prod_{\ell\mid k}(1+1/\ell)$ and the
L\'evy constant is $L=\pi^2/(12\log 2)$, so that $\log q_n(x)\sim nL$ for
$\mu$-almost every $x$.

\begin{proposition}[Local laws, symmetric games, exact Legendre values and quartic identities]\label{at:basic}
{\rm(i)} For every integer $k\ge2$ and $\mu$-almost every $x$, the set
$\{n:k\mid q_n(x)\}$ has density $1/\Psi(k)$.  For every prime $p$ and
almost every $x$, the set $\{n: p\nmid q_n(x)q_{n+1}(x)\}$ has density
$(p-1)/(p+1)$, and the set $\{n:p\mid q_n(x),\ p\mid q_{n+2}(x)\}$ has
density $g_p/(p+1)$, where
$g_p=\sum_{j\ge1}\log_2\bigl(1+1/(jp(jp+2))\bigr)$ is the Gauss measure of
$\{x:p\mid a_1(x)\}$.
{\rm(ii)} If $A$ is a circulant matrix, then $v(A)$ equals the mean of the
entries of $A$; if $A$ is antisymmetric, then $v(A)=0$; and for any
sequence $(\xi_k)$ the Toeplitz matrix $(\xi_{i-j})_{1\le i,j\le m}$ and the
Hankel matrix $(\xi_{i+j-m-1})_{1\le i,j\le m}$ have the same value.
{\rm(iii)} For
$p\in\{17,41,73,89,97,113,137,193,233,241,281,313,337,409,433,449,457,521,569,601,617\}$
one has $v(H_p)=-y/x$, where $\eps_p=x+y\sqrt p$; for $p\in\{5,13\}$ one
has $v(H_p)=-y/x$ with $\eps_p=(x+y\sqrt p)/2$, namely $v(H_5)=-1$ and
$v(H_{13})=-1/3$; for $p=401$ the unit is $\eps_p^5$ and for $p=577$ it is
$\eps_p^7$.  For
$p\in\{5,13,29,37,61,101,109,157,181,277,349,373,421,541,613\}$ one has
$v(T_p)=+y/x$, where $\eps_p^3=x+y\sqrt p$; for $p=229$ the unit is
$\eps_p^9$.  In every listed case $x+y\sqrt p$ has norm $-1$, and an optimal strategy
common to both players has full support $\{1,\dots,m\}$ for $T_p$ and
support $\{1,\dots,m-1\}$ for $H_p$.
{\rm(iv)} Let $q$ be an odd prime power and $F\in\Fq_q[X,Y,Z]$ a quartic
form defining a smooth curve.  Then
\[
 \sum_{P\in\mathbb P^2(\Fq_q)}\chi_q\bigl(F(P)\bigr)=q\,t(F),
\]
where $\chi_q$ is the quadratic character of $\Fq_q$ and $t(F)$ is the trace
of the Frobenius on the $E_7$ root lattice of the degree-two del Pezzo
surface $w^2=F$; in particular $t(F)\in\Z$ and $|t(F)|\le7$.
{\rm(v)} Let $C$ be a smooth plane quartic over $\Fq_q$, $q$ a power of a
prime larger than three so that the Gauss map is separable and $C$ is
reflexive, all of whose flexes are ordinary and whose $28$ bitangents are
ordinary (no hyperflex).  Write $b_{\rm s}$ for the
number of $\Fq_q$-rational bitangents whose two points of tangency are
$\Fq_q$-rational and $b_{\rm c}$ for the number whose two points of tangency
are swapped by the Frobenius.  Then the dual curve satisfies
$\#C^\vee(\Fq_q)=\#C(\Fq_q)-b_{\rm s}+b_{\rm c}$.
{\rm(vi)} Let $W$ be a standard Brownian motion on $[0,2]$ and, for a step
function $f\ge0$ on $[0,1]$ with $\int_0^1f=1$, let
$I_f(y)=\int_0^1f(x)\,dW(x+y)$ for $y\in[0,1]$, a continuous function of
$y$.  For $R\ge1$ let $V_R$ be the supremum of $\inf_{y\in[0,1]}I_f(y)$
over such $f$ with $\|f\|_2\le R$.  Then $V_R$ is a random variable,
nondecreasing in $R$, and almost surely
\[
 \inf_{y\in[0,1]}\bigl(W(y+1)-W(y)\bigr)\le V_R\le
 \sup_{x\in[0,1]}\bigl(W(x+1)-W(x)\bigr),
\]
so that $V=\lim_{R\to\infty}V_R$ exists and is almost surely finite.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
{\rm(i)} Write $r_n=q_n/q_{n-1}\in\mathbb P^1(\Fq_p)$ for the projective
class of the column $(q_{n-1},q_n)$ modulo $p$; since consecutive continuants
are coprime, $r_n$ is well defined, and the recursion
$q_{n+1}=a_{n+1}q_n+q_{n-1}$ gives $r_{n+1}=a_{n+1}+1/r_n$.  Each map
$r\mapsto a+1/r$ is a bijection of $\mathbb P^1(\Fq_p)$, so the product of the
Gauss measure with the uniform measure on $\mathbb P^1(\Fq_p)$ is invariant
under the skew product $(x,r)\mapsto(Gx,a_1(x)+1/r)$, $G$ the Gauss map;
this skew product is ergodic, which is the content of the equidistribution
theorems of Moeckel and Jager and
Liardet for $(q_{n-1},q_n)$ modulo $p$.  The event
$p\mid q_n$ is $r_n=0$, of stationary probability $1/(p+1)$, and for a
general modulus $k$ the same argument on $\mathbb P^1(\Z/k)$, which has
$\Psi(k)$ points, gives $1/\Psi(k)$; the ergodicity of the skew product
for composite $k$ is part of the cited theorems.  Since $r_n=0$ forces
$r_{n+1}=\infty$, the events $p\mid q_n$ and $p\mid q_{n+1}$ are disjoint,
whence the density $1-2/(p+1)$.  Finally $r_n=0$ forces $r_{n+2}=a_{n+2}$,
so $p\mid q_n$ and $p\mid q_{n+2}$ together mean $r_n=0$ and
$p\mid a_1(G^{n+1}x)$; the integrand $\mathbf 1_{r=0}\,\mathbf 1_{p\mid a_2(x)}$
has product-measure integral $g_p/(p+1)$, and the ergodic theorem for the
skew product gives the density.
{\rm(ii)} Every row and every column of a circulant matrix is a permutation of the
first, so the uniform strategy of either player makes every pure reply of
the other player yield the mean entry; both guarantees coincide.  Since $v(-A^{\mathsf T})=-v(A)$ for every
$A$, if $A^{\mathsf T}=-A$ then $v(A)=v(-A^{\mathsf T})=-v(A)$, so $v(A)=0$.  Reversing the order of the columns
turns $(\xi_{i-j})$ into $(\xi_{i+j-m-1})$ and does not change the value.
{\rm(iii)} Each matrix is symmetric.  For the listed $p$, the linear system
$\sum_i x_iA_{ij}=v$ for $j$ in the stated support, $\sum_ix_i=1$, $x_i=0$
off the support, has a unique rational solution, which is nonnegative and
satisfies $\sum_ix_iA_{ij}=v$ for every column $j$, including the column
$m$ omitted from the support of $H_p$.  Thus $x$ guarantees $v$ to the row
player, and by symmetry of $A$ the same vector guarantees $v$ to the column
player, so $v(A)=v$.  The rational numbers $v$ so obtained are the stated unit
coordinate ratios; the exact rational arithmetic is reproduced in the
stress-test section.
{\rm(iv)} Let $X$ be the surface $w^2=F(X,Y,Z)$ in the weighted projective
space $\mathbb P(1,1,1,2)$.  Since $F$ is smooth, $X$ is a smooth del Pezzo
surface of degree two, the double cover of the plane branched along
$C=\{F=0\}$, and $\#X(\Fq_q)=\sum_P\bigl(1+\chi_q(F(P))\bigr)$ because
$F(\lambda P)=\lambda^4F(P)$.  For a smooth rational surface Weil's formula
gives $\#X(\Fq_q)=q^2+q\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathrm{Frob}\mid
\operatorname{Pic}\overline X\bigr)+1$.  The Picard lattice
has rank eight and splits orthogonally as $\Z K_X\oplus K_X^\perp$ with
$K_X^\perp\cong E_7(-1)$, the Frobenius fixing $K_X$ and acting on
$K_X^\perp$ through the Weyl group $W(E_7)$.  Hence
$\operatorname{tr}=1+t(F)$ and the character sum equals
$\#X(\Fq_q)-(q^2+q+1)=q\,t(F)$; an orthogonal transformation of a rank-seven
lattice has trace at most seven in absolute value.
{\rm(v)} The Gauss map $C\to C^\vee$, $P\mapsto T_PC$, is the normalisation
of $C^\vee$ and is defined over $\Fq_q$.  Under the ordinariness hypotheses
the singular points of $C^\vee$ are the $24$ cusps, images of the flexes,
each with one branch, and the $28$ nodes, images of the bitangents, each
with two branches through the two points of tangency.  Away from the nodes
the Gauss map is a bijection between $\Fq_q$-points, and a rational point of
$C$ maps to a rational point of $C^\vee$.  A rational node whose branches
are rational receives two rational points of $C$, a rational node with
conjugate branches receives none, and a non-rational node receives no
rational point since its branches are not rational.  Counting gives the
identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Prime continuant law]\label{at1}
{\rm(i)} $\mu\{x:q_N(x)\text{ prime}\}=(2\log2+o(1))/N$ as $N\to\infty$.
{\rm(ii)} For $x$ uniformly distributed, the point process
$\sum_{n:\,q_n(x)\text{ prime}}\delta_{\log n-T}$ converges in law, as
$T\to\infty$, to the Poisson process on $\R$ of intensity $2\log2$.
{\rm(iii)} The count $\#\{n\le N:q_n(x)\text{ prime}\}$, for $x$ uniformly
distributed, has Gaussian fluctuations of variance $(2\log2+o(1))\log N$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Prime-pair correlations along the convergents]\label{at2}
{\rm(i)} $\mu\{x:q_n(x),q_{n+1}(x)\text{ both prime}\}
 =\bigl(24\log^22/\pi^2+o(1)\bigr)/n^2$, that is, primality at consecutive
indices is negatively correlated by the exact factor $6/\pi^2$ relative to
the product of the two marginal probabilities of Conjecture~\ref{at1}.
{\rm(ii)} The same asymptotic holds for
$\mu\{x:p_n(x),q_n(x)\text{ both prime}\}$.
{\rm(iii)} At lag two the correlation factor is
\[
 C_2=\prod_p\Bigl(1-\frac1{p^2}+\frac{g_p(p+1)}{p^2}\Bigr)=0.91957\ldots,
\]
with $g_p$ as in Proposition~\ref{at:basic}(i), so that
$\mu\{x:q_n,q_{n+2}\text{ both prime}\}\sim C_2(2\log2)^2/n^2$.
{\rm(iv)} For every fixed lag $h\ge1$,
$\mu\{x:q_n(x),q_{n+h}(x)\text{ both prime}\}\ll_hn^{-2}$; consequently,
for almost every $x$ and every fixed $h\ge1$, only finitely many $n$ have
$q_n(x)$ and $q_{n+h}(x)$ both prime, and only finitely many $n$ have
$p_n(x)$ and $q_n(x)$ both prime.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Squarefree and totient densities of continuants]\label{at3}
For $\mu$-almost every $x$,
\[
 \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\#\{n\le N:q_n(x)\text{ squarefree}\}
 =\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\sum_{n\le N}\frac{\varphi(q_n(x))}{q_n(x)}
 =\prod_p\Bigl(1-\frac1{p(p+1)}\Bigr)=0.70445\ldots,
\]
and more generally, for every multiplicative $f$ that is bounded on
prime powers and satisfies
$\sum_p\sum_{k\ge1}|f(p^k)-f(p^{k-1})|/\Psi(p^k)<\infty$, a class
containing the squarefree indicator and $\varphi(n)/n$,
$N^{-1}\sum_{n\le N}f(q_n(x))$ converges to
$\prod_p\bigl(1+\sum_{k\ge1}(f(p^k)-f(p^{k-1}))/\Psi(p^k)\bigr)$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Erd\H os--Kac law along the convergents]\label{at4}
For $\mu$-almost every $x$ and every real $t$,
\[
 \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\#\Bigl\{n\le N:
 \frac{\omega(q_n(x))-\log n}{\sqrt{\log n}}\le t\Bigr\}
 =\frac1{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^t e^{-u^2/2}\,du,
\]
where $\omega$ counts distinct prime divisors; the centring $\log n$ may be
replaced by $\sum_{p\le q_n}1/(p+1)$, which differs from
$\log\log q_n$ by a bounded amount.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Dimension of the prime-continuant sets]\label{at5}
{\rm(i)} The set $\{x\in(0,1):q_n(x)\text{ is prime for every }n\ge1\}$ has
Hausdorff dimension exactly $1/2$.
{\rm(ii)} The set $\{x\in(0,1):q_n(x)\text{ is composite for every }n\ge1\}$
has Hausdorff dimension $1$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Digit sets in arithmetic progressions]\label{at6}
For integers $1\le a\le m$ let $E_{a,m}$ be the set of $x\in(0,1)$ all of
whose partial quotients lie in $a+m\mathbb N_0=\{a,a+m,a+2m,\dots\}$.  Then,
uniformly for $a/m$ in any compact subinterval of $(0,1]$,
\[
 \dim_{\mathrm H}E_{a,m}
 =\frac12+\frac1{2m}-\frac{\log m}{2m^2}-\frac{\psi_\Gamma(a/m)}{2m^2}
 +O\Bigl(\frac{\log^2m}{m^3}\Bigr),
\]
where $\psi_\Gamma$ is the digamma function; in particular
$\dim_{\mathrm H}E_{m,m}=\tfrac12+\tfrac1{2m}-\tfrac{\log m}{2m^2}+\tfrac{\gamma}{2m^2}+O(m^{-3}\log^2m)$
with Euler's constant $\gamma$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Continuant avoidance dimension]\label{at7}
For a prime $p$ let $A_p=\{x\in(0,1):p\nmid q_n(x)\text{ for every }n\ge1\}$.
Then
\[
 \dim_{\mathrm H}A_p=1-\frac{6\log2}{\pi^2}\cdot\frac1p+O\Bigl(\frac1{p^2}\Bigr),
\]
and $\mu\{x:p\nmid q_n(x)\text{ for all }n\le N\}=\exp(-(\rho_p+o(1))N)$
with $\rho_p=(p+1)^{-1}(1+O(1/p))$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Unit law for the Legendre half-box games]\label{at8}
Let $p\equiv1\pmod4$ be prime.
{\rm(i)} The linear system $\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}x_i\chi_p(i+j)=v$
$(1\le j\le m-1)$, $\sum_{i<m}x_i=1$, is nonsingular and its value is
$v=-y/x$ for a unit $x+y\sqrt p$ of norm $-1$ in $\Z[\sqrt p]$ (a solution of
$x^2-py^2=-1$) or, when $p\equiv5\pmod8$, possibly a unit
$(x+y\sqrt p)/2$ of norm $-1$ in the maximal order; the unit is an odd power
$\eps_p^{e}$ of the fundamental unit, and $e\ne1$ only if $p$ is of the
form $k^2+1$ or $k^2+4$.
{\rm(ii)} The full linear system $\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_i\chi_p(i-j)=v$
$(1\le j\le m)$, $\sum_ix_i=1$, has value $v=+y/x$ with $x+y\sqrt p=\eps_p^{3e}$
when $p\equiv5\pmod8$, and $v=-y/x$ with the same unit as in {\rm(i)}
when $p\equiv1\pmod8$, in which case the solution always has a negative
coordinate.
{\rm(iii)} Whenever the solution in {\rm(i)} (respectively {\rm(ii)}) is
nonnegative it is the unique optimal strategy of both players and
$v(H_p)$ (respectively $v(T_p)$) equals the unit ratio exactly; this
happens for a set of primes $p\equiv1\pmod8$ of positive relative density in
case {\rm(i)} and for a set of primes $p\equiv5\pmod8$ of positive relative
density in case {\rm(ii)}.
{\rm(iv)} $\sqrt p\,v(H_p)\to-1$ as $p\to\infty$ through primes
$p\equiv1\pmod8$, and $\sqrt p\,v(T_p)\to+1$ as $p\to\infty$ through
primes $p\equiv5\pmod8$; in the unit regime of {\rm(iii)} this is
automatic, since $py^2/x^2=1+1/x^2$ or $1+4/x^2$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Least odd residue law in the complementary classes]\label{at9}
Let $\ell(p)$ denote the least odd prime that is a quadratic residue modulo
$p$.
{\rm(i)} As $p\to\infty$ through primes $p\equiv5\pmod8$ with
$\ell(p)\in\{3,5\}$, $\sqrt p\,v(H_p)\to-1$; as $p\to\infty$ through primes
$p\equiv1\pmod8$ with $\ell(p)\in\{3,5\}$, $\sqrt p\,v(T_p)\to+1$.
{\rm(ii)} For every prime $\ell\ge7$, along primes $p\equiv5\pmod8$ with
$\ell(p)=\ell$ one has $\limsup\sqrt p\,v(H_p)<0$ and
$\liminf\sqrt p\,v(H_p)>-1$, and along primes $p\equiv1\pmod8$ with
$\ell(p)=\ell$ one has $\liminf\sqrt p\,v(T_p)>0$ and
$\limsup\sqrt p\,v(T_p)<1$; consequently neither $\sqrt p\,v(H_p)$ over
$p\equiv5\pmod8$ nor $\sqrt p\,v(T_p)$ over $p\equiv1\pmod8$ converges.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Brownian shift games]\label{at10}
Let $(\xi_k)_{k\in\Z}$ be independent random variables with mean zero,
variance one and finite fourth moment, and let
$K^{(m)}=(\xi_{i+j})_{1\le i,j\le m}$; by Proposition~\ref{at:basic}(ii) the
Toeplitz game $(\xi_{i-j})_{1\le i,j\le m}$ has the same value as the Hankel
game of the shifted sequence, so it suffices to treat $K^{(m)}$.  Then
$\sqrt m\,v(K^{(m)})$ converges in law to a nondegenerate symmetric limit
$V$ that does not depend on the law of $\xi$, and $V$ is the value of the continuum game
\[
 V=\lim_{R\to\infty}\ \sup_{f}\ \inf_{y\in[0,1]}\int_0^1f(x)\,dW(x+y),
\]
where $W$ is a standard Brownian motion on $[0,2]$ and the supremum runs
over step-function probability densities $f$ on $[0,1]$ with
$\|f\|_2\le R$, as in Proposition~\ref{at:basic}(vi), which proves that the
capped values increase to an almost surely finite limit $V$; the assertion
includes that $V$ is nondegenerate and symmetric.  In contrast, for independent
$\xi$ arranged in a circulant matrix $\sqrt m\,v$ converges to a standard
Gaussian, and for a full matrix of independent entries $m\,v$ is tight.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Hessian big monodromy over finite fields]\label{at11}
For a smooth plane quartic $C$ over $\Fq_q$ let $H_C$ be its Hessian sextic
and write $a_C=(q+1-\#C(\Fq_q))/\sqrt q$, $a_H=(q+1-\#H_C(\Fq_q))/\sqrt q$.
As $q\to\infty$ through prime powers with characteristic at least five,
for $C$ uniformly random among smooth quartics with smooth Hessian, the pair
$(a_C,a_H)$ converges in law to $(\operatorname{tr}U_6,\operatorname{tr}U_{20})$
with $U_6\in\mathrm{USp}(6)$ and $U_{20}\in\mathrm{USp}(20)$ independent and
Haar distributed; the number of $\Fq_q$-rational flexes converges to the
fixed-point count of a uniform element of the symmetric group $S_{24}$,
independently of the pair.  The same holds for smooth curves of every
degree $d\ge4$ in characteristic larger than $d$, with the Hessian of degree
$3(d-2)$, genus $(3d-7)(3d-8)/2$, and the flexes numbering $3d(d-2)$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Sato--Tate independence of a curve and its Hessian]\label{at12}
Let $C$ be a smooth plane quartic over $\Q$ whose Hessian $H_C$ is smooth
and such that the Mumford--Tate groups of the Jacobians of $C$ and of $H_C$
are $\mathrm{GSp}(6)$ and $\mathrm{GSp}(20)$, with no nonzero homomorphism
between the two Jacobians over $\overline{\Q}$.  Write
$a_p(C)=p+1-\#C(\Fq_p)$.  Then, as $p\to\infty$ through primes of good
reduction, the pairs $\bigl(a_p(C)/\sqrt p,\ a_p(H_C)/\sqrt p\bigr)$ are
equidistributed with respect
to the product of the trace laws of $\mathrm{USp}(6)$ and $\mathrm{USp}(20)$.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Tangency law and dual-curve point counts]\label{at13}
As $q\to\infty$ with characteristic at least five, for a random smooth
plane quartic over $\Fq_q$ the triple
$(r,b_{\rm s},b_{\rm c})$, where $r$ is the number of $\Fq_q$-rational
bitangents and $b_{\rm s},b_{\rm c}$ are as in
Proposition~\ref{at:basic}(v), converges in law to $(F,B,F-B)$, where $F$ is
the number of fixed points of a Haar-random element of $\mathrm{Sp}(6,\Fq_2)$
on the $28$ odd theta characteristics and, conditionally on $F$, $B$ is
binomial with parameters $F$ and $\tfrac12$.  Consequently
$\#C^\vee(\Fq_q)-\#C(\Fq_q)$ converges in law to $F-2B$, a symmetric
integer law with variance one.  Jointly, the number of rational bitangents
whose two lines on the del Pezzo double cover are rational converges to the
count of fixed weights of a Haar-random element of $W(E_7)$ in its
minuscule $56$-point action, with $t(F)$ of Proposition~\ref{at:basic}(iv)
converging to the reflection-representation trace of the same element.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Positivity of the quarter-box Legendre games]\label{at14}
For a prime $p\equiv3\pmod4$ let $m=\lfloor p/4\rfloor$ and
$Q_p=(\chi_p(i+j))_{1\le i,j\le m}$.  Then $v(Q_p)>0$ for every prime
$p\equiv3\pmod4$ with $p\ge7$, and there are constants
$0<c_1<c_2<\infty$ with $c_1\le\sqrt m\,v(Q_p)\le c_2$ for all such $p$,
while $\sqrt m\,v(Q_p)$ does not converge.  For $p\equiv1\pmod4$ the sign
of $v(Q_p)$ is positive and negative for infinitely many $p$ each.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{conjecture}[Vanishing law for the half box in the class $3$ modulo $4$]\label{at15}
For primes $p\equiv3\pmod4$ one has $\sqrt p\,v(H_p)\to0$; more precisely
$v(H_p)=O(p^{-3/2})$ along $p\equiv7\pmod8$, while $p^{3/2}v(H_p)$ is not
bounded along $p\equiv3\pmod8$.  Moreover $v(H_p)\le0$ for every
$p\equiv7\pmod8$, while along $p\equiv3\pmod8$ the value is positive for
infinitely many $p$ and negative for infinitely many $p$.
\end{conjecture}

\part{Forking without stable witnesses: quantum modules over graphs}

\medskip\noindent\emph{Added to the deposit: 3 September 2026.}\par\medskip

\providecommand{\forkind}{\mathop{\raise.2ex\hbox{$\scriptstyle|$}}\hspace{-.75ex}\raise-.1ex\hbox{$\scriptstyle\smile$}}

This part states fifteen conjectures on stable forking after its refutation, on modules over the quantum algebras of graphs, and on the scalar division rings of those modules.  The proved layer is Proposition~\ref{au:basic}.
Throughout, $k$ is a countable field of characteristic different from two,
$q=-1$, and $\Gamma$ is a countable graph with edge relation $E$.  The
\emph{quantum algebra} of $\Gamma$ is the $k$-algebra
\[
 A_q(\Gamma)=k\langle x_v:v\in\Gamma\rangle\big/
 \bigl(x_ux_v+x_vx_u\ (uEv),\ \ x_ux_v-x_vx_u\ (u\ne v,\ \neg uEv)\bigr),
\]
so that generators at adjacent vertices anticommute and all other pairs
commute; the index $q$ records that the same constructions make sense for
any root of unity or any $q\in k^\times$ once the edges are oriented.
$D_q(\Gamma)$ is its division ring of fractions (Proposition~\ref{au:basic}(i)).
For $1\le n\le\omega$, $M_q^{(n)}(\Gamma)$ is the two-sorted structure with a
vertex sort carrying $E$, a vector sort $V$ carrying the abelian group
structure of a left $D_q(\Gamma)$-vector space of dimension $n$ together
with a unary function $w\mapsto\lambda w$ for each $\lambda\in k$, and the
function $(v,w)\mapsto x_vw$ from the vertex sort times $V$ to $V$;
$M_q(\Gamma)$ is $M_q^{(\omega)}(\Gamma)$ and $T_q(\Gamma)$ is its complete
theory.  The scalars of $D_q(\Gamma)$ are not a sort; the scalars named by the
language are the $k$-linear combinations of products of generators at
named vertices.
We write $\Gamma_{\rm bip}$ for the random bipartite graph with its
bipartition named and $\Gamma_{\rm rand}$ for the random graph.  A theory
has \emph{stable forking} if it satisfies the conclusion of the stable
forking conjecture for all sets $A\subseteq B$, and \emph{stable forking
over models} if it satisfies it whenever $A$ is a model.  For $k\ge1$, a
formula $\varphi(x;y_1,\dots,y_k)$ has the \emph{$k$-independence property}
if there are tuples $a^j_i$ ($i<\omega$, $1\le j\le k$) and $b_S$
($S\subseteq\omega^k$) with $\varphi(b_S;a^1_{i_1},\dots,a^k_{i_k})$ if and
only if $(i_1,\dots,i_k)\in S$;
a formula $\varphi(x;y)$ is \emph{$k$-dependent} if no partition of $y$
into $k$ blocks gives it the $k$-independence property.  In a simple
theory a formula is stable if and only if it is $1$-dependent.  When a
formula $\varphi(\bar x,\bar b)$ with parameters $\bar b$ is called stable,
all of its parameters, vertices included, are displayed as variables and
$\varphi(\bar x;\bar y)$ is required to have no order property; this is the
notion of the stable forking conjecture.  Where vertex parameters are
instead treated as named constants, as in Proposition~\ref{au:basic}(iv)
and Conjecture~\ref{au12}, this is said explicitly.  The
counterexample of Freitag and Mutchnik lives on a module over
$D_q(\Gamma_{\rm bip})$; the conjectures below concern the structures
$M_q(\Gamma)$ in the language just fixed, which is expected to be
interdefinable with theirs but is defined here independently, and the
statements about the counterexample itself are stated for $T_q(\Gamma_{\rm bip})$.

\begin{proposition}[Quantum algebras of graphs and their modules]\label{au:basic}
{\rm(i)} Fix a linear order on $\Gamma$.  The ordered monomials
$x_{v_1}^{a_1}\cdots x_{v_n}^{a_n}$ with $v_1<\dots<v_n$ form a $k$-basis of
$A_q(\Gamma)$; $A_q(\Gamma)$ is a domain graded by the exponent vector in $\mathbb N^{(\Gamma)}$, an iterated Ore extension of $k$
when $\Gamma$ is finite, and a directed union of such algebras in general;
it satisfies the left and right Ore conditions, so $D_q(\Gamma)$ exists.
An induced-subgraph embedding $\Gamma'\to\Gamma$ induces embeddings
$A_q(\Gamma')\to A_q(\Gamma)$ and $D_q(\Gamma')\to D_q(\Gamma)$, and every
automorphism of $\Gamma$ extends to an automorphism of $D_q(\Gamma)$; in
particular $D_q(\Gamma')$ embeds in $D_q(\Gamma_{\rm rand})$ for every
countable $\Gamma'$.
{\rm(ii)} An element of $A_q(\Gamma)$ is central if and only if each of its
monomials is central, and the monomial $x^a=\prod_vx_v^{a_v}$ is central if
and only if $\sum_{u:\,uEv}a_u$ is even for every vertex $v$, that is, the
exponent vector reduced modulo two lies in the kernel of the adjacency
matrix over $\Fq_2$.  Hence for finite $\Gamma$ of odd order the centre
